# v1.0 source-coverage and blocker audit

**Project domains:** Data processing, Web backend, Web frontend, Project infrastructure

**Assessment date:** 2026-10-31. **Implementation baseline:**
`9d820602ff556d280dedb3267fc51b116045c495`.

This is dated evidence for active completeness work, not a release acceptance or a
replacement backlog. [TODO](TODO.md) owns unfinished work;
[releasing](releasing.md#version-10-data-completeness-gate) defines the gate.
Retain this assessment while its source omissions and audit defects guide that work.

## Assessment

The current application has substantial working reference coverage. Its existing
audits do not yet establish v1.0 completeness. This pass confirmed four uncovered
core-reference areas and two reproducible validation defects, and sharpened four
existing scope/acceptance dependencies. It did not change gameplay data or runtime
artifacts, acquire newer sources, or accept a final candidate.

| Finding | Classification | Required outcome |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [V1-A01](#v1-a01--initiative-roll-reference-resolved-2026-11-05) | Resolved source-to-browser gap; reverified at `10424ad2` | Initiative Roll and its independent choices are cited, searchable and linked |
| [V1-A02](#v1-a02--combat-group-reference-and-ownership-resolved-2026-11-05) | Resolved source-to-browser gap; reverified at `10424ad2` | Source-reviewed capacity and marker ownership are published, cited and linked |
| [V1-A03](#v1-a03--army-retreat-and-standard-game-references-published) | Publication and bounded source/scope gap resolved 2026-11-05; final-candidate acceptance remains | Keep Army Retreat, Trooper State, Standard Game and scenario-specific endings distinct |
| [V1-A04](#v1-a04--general-setup-tables-published-and-source-verified) | Resolved presentation gap; reverified at `10424ad2` | Cited setup and threshold rows are published with their actual scope |
| [V1-A05](#v1-a05--weapon-gap-triage-aborts-on-completed-definitions) | Resolved 2026-11-05; current-input regression and CLI verified at `6edc7d4c` | Retain approved historical reviews after definitions publish without miscounting current omissions |
| [V1-A06](#v1-a06--the-enrichment-regression-has-a-stale-supporting-set) | Resolved 2026-11-05; exact sets covered by the `702536fe` full-assets suite | Distinguish 11 unresolved links from 13 supporting targets and assert each exact set |
| [V1-A07](#v1-a07--passing-catalog-audits-do-not-certify-the-10-scope) | Existing release-evidence gap, quantified | Reconcile historical classification policy and activate the correct primary gate |
| [V1-A08](#v1-a08--the-frozen-inventory-does-not-cover-the-current-record-set) | Existing completeness-review gap, quantified | Give the current source/record set an explicit acceptance disposition |
| [V1-A09](#v1-a09--occurrence-parameters-and-the-usage-index-remain-open) | Existing semantic/implementation work, quantified | Review exact occurrence meanings and deliver their required usable context |
| [V1-A10](#v1-a10--reinforcements-procedural-authority-is-unresolved) | Existing source/applicability dependency, reproduced | Establish authority or explicitly resolve the supported boundary |

These are necessary outcomes, not ten equally sized implementation tasks. Missing
facts and misleading scope block release; a missing specialized UI does not when a
basic linked reference can communicate the same information.

other publications on the live official site.

## Confirmed findings and dispositions

### V1-A01 — Initiative Roll reference (resolved 2026-11-05)

**Project domains:** Data processing, Web backend, Web frontend

English and Spanish N5.3 p. 15 describe the Lieutenant Attribute roll, repeat
conditions, open Attribute information, and the separation between choosing turn
order and choosing deployment. The archived `Initiative_and_Deployment` page
(revision 3955) corroborates this source area.

The current `rule:initiative-roll` record cites the Initiative Roll, Keeping
Initiative and Keeping Deployment sections on p. 15. It explains the Lieutenant
WIP test, repeat conditions, Open Information and the winner's choice between
turn order and Deployment. Its links connect the separate Deployment reference
and Lieutenant/Attribute context.

The prior finding that normal search had no Initiative result is no longer
current. The API and browser checks at `10424ad295806d62f42c661eb92f28fd21cc814a`
confirm the catalog entry, details, citations, reverse navigation from Deployment
and ordinary navigation at 1440/390 px. The evidence closes this bounded
source-to-browser gap; it does not certify all rules-source coverage.

### V1-A02 — Combat Group reference and ownership (resolved 2026-11-05)

**Project domains:** Data processing, Web backend, Web frontend

English and Spanish N5.3 p. 10 and archived `Combat_Group` revision 3847 establish
the ten-member group limit, initial assignment, ordinary separation of groups and
the Peripheral counting boundary. The pinned English and Spanish PDFs and Wiki
revision agree that Marker group identity is Open Information. English p. 10 and
Wiki revision 3847 identify Deployable Weapons and Equipment with the deploying
Trooper's group; Spanish p. 10 also names non-Trooper Marker examples, including
Decoys. This is a difference in example scope, not a conflict in the shared rule.
The maintained `facts.sourceNotes` preserves that distinction and does not
generalize the English examples to every Marker subtype.

The current `rule:combat-group` record publishes the source-reviewed capacity,
assignment, transfer, Peripheral and Marker facts, and distinguishes group
capacity from the separate 15-Trooper Army List maximum. Its English p. 10,
Spanish p. 10, English p. 11 and archived Wiki citations support those claims.
The current API/search/reverse-link checks and normal browser navigation passed at
`10424ad295806d62f42c661eb92f28fd21cc814a` at 1440/390/320 px. This resolves the
bounded capacity and ownership gap; V1S-082 remains open for the wider source
inventory and other explicit omissions. Do not introduce roster-construction
legality validation.

### V1-A03 — Army Retreat and Standard Game references published

**Project domains:** Data processing, Web backend, Web frontend

English N5.3 p. 146 and Spanish p. 150 distinguish army-level Retreat from the
Trooper's State. They establish the Tactical Phase survivor calculation, treatment
of undeployed survivors, threshold, Loss of Lieutenant interaction, replacement
Lieutenant restriction, skipped Impetuous Phase, recovery and scenario overrides.
Archived `Retreat!_situation` revision 3942 corroborates the army context.

The current `rule:army-retreat` and `rule:standard-game` references now separate
the army trigger from the Trooper's State and explain Standard Game scoring and
ending conditions. API checks verify the 25% threshold, undeployed survivors,
threshold comparison, scenario scope, links to the State and Loss of Lieutenant,
and the separate three-round/Victory Point/Retreat-ending description. Normal
search and browser routes passed at 1440/390/320 px at
`10424ad295806d62f42c661eb92f28fd21cc814a`.

The original missing-publication finding is resolved. Subsequent V1S-039/042–044
reviews reconcile shared scenario concepts, scenario objectives and endings, map
geometry, and scoped Skills/FAQ applicability. They found no additional
source-backed mismatch; the English/Spanish chart disagreements remain explicitly
disclosed, not adjudicated. These reviews keep Standard Game ending conditions
separate from scenario-specific endings.

**Disposition (2026-11-05):** the bounded V1-A03 source/scope review is complete
as a no-change outcome. Final-candidate scenario interaction and browser acceptance
remain in [V1S-045](TODO.md#four-core-scenarios) and
[V1S-088](TODO.md#final-frozen-candidate-acceptance-and-release). This disposition
does not close V1S-082, its 821 locator reviews, or whole-source and final release
acceptance. Avoid a live survivor calculator or blanket application of Standard
Game endings to scenarios.

### V1-A04 — General setup tables published and source-verified

**Project domains:** Data processing, Web backend, Web frontend

The direct PDF checks pass for six Game Modes rows on p. 6 and the independently
reviewed Retreat/table guidance on pp. 146–147. The maintained rows include Army
Points, base SWC, table/deployment-zone dimensions, recommended duration and Retreat
thresholds. The current `rule:game-modes` reference publishes the six reviewed rows
through normal search and `/rules/game-modes`, with citations to pp. 6, 146 and
147. Its scope identifies recommended general setup and keeps Retreat thresholds
separate; scenario-owned dimensions and overrides remain distinct. The prior
finding that these rows existed only in validation manifests is resolved.

Advisory status remains explicit; this does not make the information unusable to
players. Illustrative terrain names are not eighteen missing canonical Terrain Types.

At `10424ad295806d62f42c661eb92f28fd21cc814a`, a focused run of the four API
checks, fourteen browser-navigation cases across 1440/390/320 px, and
`tests/test_game_mode_source_table.py` passed **34 tests**. This verifies these
bounded references and source tables, not whole-source or final-candidate
acceptance.

### V1-A05 — Weapon gap triage aborts on completed definitions

**Project domain:** Project infrastructure

The October 31 baseline failed the documented enrichment → Weapon Chart →
Weapon-definition triage sequence with
`Reviewed Weapon is not an unresolved identity: Crazykoala`. Both Crazykoala and
Madtraps had acquired definitions while the 64-entry shared-effects policy retained
their earlier approvals. The initial isolated audit join used a separate local
62-review subset; that workaround did not verify the documented CLI.

**Resolved 2026-11-05:** `load_shared_reviews()` now preserves approved entries
when an exact published definition supersedes an unresolved gap, while retaining
the original profile, source-identity and canonical Weapon-link checks.
`test_current_enrichment_preserves_reviews_after_definitions_are_published`
passed **1 test** at `6edc7d4cae30482ee867c450fa8dc445da1a88a8`. The documented
enrichment and triage CLI sequence also completed successfully against current
inputs: Army database SHA-256
`8a5840d1ff632e89de26210ee7bcb47e54c6579c15b5c3436e226702e872d7c8`, rules
database SHA-256 `5dc249ced04b1180aab4b81ea233964adbc5df37cbcffdb54dff619b55375762`,
and English N5.3 PDF SHA-256
`53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`.
The output retains **62 reviewed shared-effect omissions**, Flash Pulse's **one
accepted historical citation**, and **2 resolved shared reviews** (Crazykoala and
Madtraps), with zero remaining source reviews or shared-effect candidates. These
results verify bounded triage, not whole-Weapon or v1.0 completeness.

### V1-A06 — The enrichment regression has a stale supporting set

**Project domain:** Data processing

The October 31 baseline had conflated two independent totals:
`unresolvedRelatedItemLinkCount` remained **11**, while the published Isolated
State relations brought `supportingRelationTargetCount` to **13**. The two
additional supporting targets are `rule:coordinated-orders` and
`rule:fireteam-general`.

**Resolved 2026-11-05:** `test_enrichment_coverage_reports_review_mapping_and_source_freshness`
now asserts both counts and the complete exact identity sets. It keeps the original
11 unresolved targets and their classification totals intact while including both
additional supporting targets. The corrected test is present in
`6a373242` and unchanged through `702536feecc3d2dddfd854ecd8e51e38509509e1`;
that pinned full-assets run passed **5,349 tests, 29 skips, zero failures**, with
all **808/808** published SVG assets present. The test and audit implementation
are unchanged between that verified revision and `6edc7d4`.

## Existing blockers and acceptance gaps sharpened by this pass

### V1-A07 — Passing catalog audits do not certify the 1.0 scope

**Project domains:** Data processing, Project infrastructure

The enrichment policy still defers missing Weapon definitions and Commlink from
0.10 to v1.0; its zero `releaseBlockerCount` is not a v1.0 verdict. The shared-effect
join above also shows why reclassifying every missing standalone definition as a
blocker would be wrong.

The default interaction command passes a historical **0.7.0 primary scope** and
its current supporting set. The explicit v1.0 candidate reports **184/185 primary
identities complete**, with Commlink pending, and **197 supporting identities**.
The default report's **200 supporting identities** has a different denominator;
neither number establishes whole-source completeness. The strict candidate command
fails on Commlink as intended.

**Close when:** reconcile enrichment/shared-effect dispositions for v1.0, resolve
the Commlink applicability boundary, and activate the appropriate primary gate
without rewriting historical review dates or suppressing unresolved facts.

#### V1S-082 bounded source review: Trooper glossary (2026-11-05)

The current `term:trooper` record is a Game glossary term, not an Army Unit
profile. The pinned English N5.3 Core Rules (S1, printed p. 173) and Spanish
N5.3 Core Rules (S17, printed p. 7) define the Trooper as an Attribute-bearing
game element belonging to a player's Army List, able to contribute and spend
Orders and declare and receive Attacks. The English N5.2 Core Rules (S3,
printed p. 172) retains the same defining facets. The local PDF hashes match
the S1/S17 pins recorded in [N5 source history](n5-source-history.md); the
locally supplied S3 file was independently hashed for this review. The Spanish
PDF's creation metadata is not treated as its publication date. This review
does not infer an Army profile or a new game mechanic.

| Source | Printed page | SHA-256 |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| S1 — N5.3 Core Rules, English | 173 | `53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f` |
| S17 — N5.3 Core Rules, Spanish | 7 | `7f564c864c6f26d6b218a0477c8afeba99da5c56e1a5369bb259f423689bb28f` |
| S3 — N5.2 Core Rules, English | 172 | `643ab0b6d7c3e9e5ffbf668543723161f252367e7788af09ec008be19c17851b` |

The previous curated summary described a Trooper as represented by a Unit
Profile and belonging to a player's force, omitting the source's Attribute,
Order, and Attack facets. The maintained record now includes those facets,
including adding Orders to the Order Pool and spending them to activate
Troopers. It retains the English N5.3 citation at p. 173; no additional
runtime rule or Army-profile claim was introduced. The record's review date
is 2026-11-05.

The v9 tracked rules database and its matched ignored deployed-object
publication were rebuilt through a deterministic, schema-preserving pair
refresh. All 498 other records were verified unchanged; `RulesDatabase.validate()`
and `tools.build_deployed_object_publication --check` passed. Rules SHA-256:
`a8ab4b267257d6b2cca3a00605c3c7a30711d57872b332db70c31c3b89bee3cf`;
publication SHA-256:
`987503f7e8619d97f774fec2f653ee197144527313fea29abf50c5ed31eb53b5`.

The tracked variant-usage publication still fingerprinted the prior rules
database and deployed-object publication. At implementation commit
`538fcebdf1cde9b8b31f0cb91fb95bdea9eb5465`, hosted Source checks
(run `42112870248`, all four matrix legs) and Deployment smoke
(run `42112870216`) failed with `Variant usage publication does not match
release artifacts`; Installed wheel smoke (`42112870240`) passed. Rebuilding
from the pinned Army pair and refreshed rules/deployed-object pair preserved
151 groups, 10,923 reviewed rows and 4,938 unresolved rows. Structural
comparison found only `sourceSnapshot.rulesDatabaseSha256` and
`sourceSnapshot.deployedObjectPublicationSha256` changed. The regenerated
publication passed both candidate and publication checks; its SHA-256 is
`96344e1f96d3c4529b8e5dd7e13e154b8598d64589a1d5331a56354a4aa5449c`.
The previous hosted failures therefore identify the stale dependent
publication, not an observed regression in the Trooper API or browser
assertions. The regression selectors and their execution outcome are listed
below.

The current `/api/glossary` payload publishes the entry at
`/glossary#term-trooper`. The regression assertions now cover curated export,
API identity/content, and ordinary browser display at 1440/390/320 px:

- `tests/test_web.py::test_glossary_projects_canonical_rules_and_embedded_attributes`
- `tests/test_application_rule_references.py::test_game_terms_are_current_canonical_embedded_records`
- `tests/test_verify_scenario_faq_browser.py::test_trooper_term_normal_browser`

**Execution (VR-0110):** At commit
`e773f20b1f04944e2500885bee1775d04e229d98`, the three registered selectors
passed **5 tests, zero skips** on Windows with required assets fully present
(808/808 SVGs). This includes ordinary browser display at 1440/390/320 px.
The dedicated `linux-dev` MCP job interface was unavailable in this session;
Windows is the recorded local execution host, and hosted Source CI remains an
independent gate.

**Disposition:** VR-0110 is retired. Keep V1S-082 open for the wider source
inventory and other explicit omissions. This bounded source comparison does
not approve the other pending source locators or whole-record acceptance.

**Close when:** retain the frozen evidence and join it to the current identities,
renames, later source reviews and browser evidence. Give each current in-scope
category/section a reviewed disposition and turn concrete omissions into bounded
work. Do not reset historical counts or treat every added record as unreviewed.

#### V1S-082 bounded source review: Peripheral glossary (2026-11-05)

**Project domains:** Data processing, Web backend, Web frontend

The pinned English N5.3 Core Rules (S1, printed p. 173; SHA-256
`53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`)
define a Peripheral as a special Trooper category that cannot contribute or
spend Orders by itself, takes no Army List space, activates when its Controller
spends an Order, and replicates the Controller's actions. The same glossary
definition states that its Cost appears in its own Troop Profile and is also
added to the Controller's Cost. The Spanish N5.3 Core Rules (S17, printed p. 7;
SHA-256
`7f564c864c6f26d6b218a0477c8afeba99da5c56e1a5369bb259f423689bb28f`)
contains the corresponding terminology definition for the Order, Army List,
activation and action-copy facets, but does not state the Cost treatment. This
is a source omission, not a conflicting Cost rule. The Spanish p. 173 reference
in the preceding Trooper review was incorrect; that page contains State
material, while S17 p. 7 contains the terminology definitions.

The existing `skill:peripheral` record (S1, printed pp. 106-108) already covers
the Controller relationship and common/type-specific behavior, including
independent-activation exceptions. Its Cost clarification concerns Victory
Points and area control, not the glossary's list-building Cost treatment. The
curated `term:peripheral` therefore retains its `trooper-category` scope and
English S1 p. 173 runtime citation while its summary now includes the
source-defined Order, Army List, activation/action and Cost facets, with a link
to the existing Special Skill. The type-specific caveat preserves the
distinction between the glossary baseline and Special Skill exceptions; this
does not add an executable rule or Army legality claim.

The updated source evidence is maintained in
[rules semantics](rules-semantics.md#rs-br-up-009--peripheral-and-controller-relationship)
and the curated
[`term:peripheral`](../data/curated/rules/n5-core-v5.3.json) record.

The tracked v9 rules database was exported twice byte-identically and
rebuilt through the schema-preserving release exporter. Only
`term:peripheral` changed among the 499 composed records; all 498 others,
the v9 schema, typed Restrictions and legacy relation graph are unchanged.
`RulesDatabase.validate()` passed. Rules SHA-256:
`163a88ae0896b3b66d54d1f49eebac581383778bbbb9495f3825753a2cc1bc3c`.

The matched ignored deployed-object publication retains all 17 reviewed
objects; only its `rulesDatabaseSha256` and `curatedSourceSha256` fingerprints
changed. `tools.build_deployed_object_publication --check` passed. Publication
SHA-256:
`dfdccb15e52fc33b3d7148813b8247b174b3ba5489dd3027e45230841ae1b3d2`.
The regenerated variant-usage publication retains 151 groups, 10,923 reviewed
source rows and 4,938 unresolved rows. Structural comparison found only
`sourceSnapshot.rulesDatabaseSha256` and
`sourceSnapshot.deployedObjectPublicationSha256` changed;
`tools.build_variant_usage --check-publication` and
`tools.audit_rules_rebuild_equivalence` passed. Variant-usage SHA-256:
`d1464385cd3bdadd2aab541d5d611461fad2141f39dda9d4baadfea5d353a9a6`.

VR-0111 passed on Windows with the local runner at commit
`26f457f001ab4b0671498bc1a70a4a400037cf02`: **5 passed, 0 failed, 0
skipped**. Required browser assets were complete (**808/808** published and
**808/808** browser-referenced). The selectors verified the curated export,
Glossary API Skill reference, and normal-browser rendering at 1440/390/320 px,
including keyboard focus, no page errors, and no horizontal overflow. Linux,
hosted CI and release acceptance remain independent; V1S-082 remains open for
the broader source inventory.

#### V1S-082 bounded source review: Unit Profile glossary (2026-11-06)

The current `term:unit-profile` summary described profile contents and
selectable options but did not define the profile as representing the Troopers
in a Unit, omitted the glossary's numerical Attribute and Characteristics
facets, and cited only English N5.3 p. 173 despite also describing options
documented on p. 8.

The pinned English N5.3 Core Rules (S1, printed pp. 8 and 173; PDF SHA-256
`53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`) define
the Unit Profile as representing the Troopers forming each Unit, supplying
their numerical Attribute values and listing Characteristics, Special Skills,
Equipment, and Armor. The English p. 8 profile structure also shows differing
Trooper options. The Spanish N5.3 Core Rules (S17, printed pp. 8 and 180; PDF
SHA-256
`7f564c864c6f26d6b218a0477c8afeba99da5c56e1a5369bb259f423689bb28f`)

#### V1S-082 bounded source review: remaining p. 173 glossary terms (2026-11-06)

A follow-up compared the current curated English-facing glossary with pinned
English N5.3 Core Rules S1 p. 173 (SHA-256
`53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`),
Spanish N5.3 S17 p. 180 (SHA-256
`7f564c864c6f26d6b218a0477c8afeba99da5c56e1a5369bb259f423689bb28f`),
and English N5.2 S3 p. 172 (SHA-256
`643ab0b6d7c3e9e5ffbf668543723161f252367e7788af09ec008be19c17851b`).
The three primary pages agree about the core element definitions; the Spanish
wording for the Deployable Weapon's Army List membership is stronger than
English ("belongs" versus "may belong"). Do not silently resolve that
cross-language difference as a universal ownership rule.

**No-change dispositions:** `term:model`, `term:marker`, `term:token`,
`term:state-token`, `term:scenery-element`, `term:victory-points`,
`term:ally`, and `term:enemy` have summaries and English page citations
consistent with the reviewed source clauses. Existing condensed phrasing
is not a claim to implement attack, target-validity or roster calculations.

**Confirmed curated-summary gaps (corrected on the tracked development pair):**

- `term:deployable-equipment` previously stated independence/targeting but
  omitted its Attribute-bearing Army List element role, its Special Skill/ Trait
  capability, and that it can receive Attacks.
- `term:deployable-weapon` previously stated independence/targeting but
  omitted its ability to perform **and** receive Attacks; English S1 says it
  **may** belong to an Army List, while Spanish S17 uses affirmative wording.
- `term:target` previously described an already designated subject rather
  than the source's capability to be chosen as the target of Attacks and effects
  from Skills or Equipment. The revised definition preserves this distinction.
- `term:hostile` previously omitted the source's own-Attributes prerequisite.
- `term:neutral` previously omitted the source's Attributes qualification;
  unlike Hostile, it does not acquire Enemy treatment merely by lacking Army
  List membership.

These are bounded source-to-curated-summary corrections, not five discovered
gameplay bugs. The 2026-11-06 reviewed text and original English p. 173
citations were published in the active v9 rules database. The English
`may belong` qualifier for a Deployable Weapon was retained; the stronger
Spanish wording is not silently applied to all N5 Army Lists. The two ordinary
`targeted` verb occurrences in the retained State-alias review were explicitly
re-fingerprinted for their changed paragraphs, without changing the original
`verb-not-state` disposition or weakening maintained-text gates.

The completed one-use Glossary verifier exported the candidate

passed; recovery copies remain in ignored
`local/glossary-v9-recovery-el9sw49u/` on Windows. The one-use
promotion script is retired; Git history preserves its implementation.

#### V1S-082 bounded source review: Label definitions (2026-11-06)

**Project domain:** Data processing

This review compares the 23 printed Labels with the maintained vocabulary and
its existing `/api/labels/{slug}` and `/labels/{slug}` publication path. It does
not treat the Wiki's additional FAQs category as a printed gameplay Label.
The independently pinned primary sources are:

| Source | Printed page | SHA-256 |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| S1 — English N5.3 | 174 | `53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f` |
| S17 — Spanish N5.3 | 181 | `7f564c864c6f26d6b218a0477c8afeba99da5c56e1a5369bb259f423689bb28f` |
| S3 — English N5.2 | 173 | `643ab0b6d7c3e9e5ffbf668543723161f252367e7788af09ec008be19c17851b` |

Supplementary Wiki evidence is the exact `Labels` member, revision **4067**,
SHA-256 `3b731a8f3ac150c66b79c4e5f0f57220fe10f257a2b18e7651ef68c2a3840bb9`,
in `WIKI-en 20260926-200832.zip`, archive SHA-256
`c0fe18c165473e3f273efd58aa7ecb0ea0d94ee274189de9361aad175b3de987`.
No upstream source was acquired or replaced.

**Confirmed definition gaps, corrected:** Airborne Deployment and Superior
Deployment omitted applicability to all Skills carrying their Label; Assignable
omitted recipient, timing, per-type and State Token/contact conditions; Attack
omitted the Allied/Neutral target restriction; CC Special Skill omitted MOD
combination and Active/Reactive scope; Comms Attack omitted Partial Cover;
Hackable omitted Allied Program targeting and its Characteristic/Label scope;
Hostile omitted Enemy treatment and Attack capability; Non-Reloadable omitted
Disposable applicability; No Roll omitted conditional fixed-result notation;
Null omitted the five named N5.3 States; Private Information incorrectly
described possessing the Label rather than the Skill or Equipment. These are
twelve reference-summary corrections, not twelve inferred gameplay-engine bugs.

**No-change:** BS Attack, CC Attack, Comms Equipment, Marker, Movement,
Negative Feedback, No LoF, Obligatory, Optional, States Phase and Supportware
retain their reviewed definitions. The separate `term:null-state` already
preserved the N5.3 five-State list and required no correction. The Wiki-only
FAQs category remains unchanged.

**Source differences:** English N5.3 and N5.2 tie Assignable to deploying its
owner; Spanish N5.3 states only the Deployment Phase. The ordinary definition
retains both statements and does not invent a shared timing ruling. The English
No Roll definition uses brackets; Spanish says parentheses. N5.3 explicitly
lists five Null States where the reviewed N5.2 Label paragraph did not. English
N5.3 and the archived English Wiki agree on the corrected clauses. These
differences are also maintained in
[rules semantics](rules-semantics.md#rs-br-base-003--label-definitions-retain-their-conditions-and-exceptions).

The maintained vocabulary retains its Wiki citation and adds English N5.3 p. 174
to `vocabularySources.labels`. No Label identity, record definition, relationship
or runtime schema changed. Semantic links use existing Skill, State, Trait and
Game-term identities. Reviewed ordinary `CC` and `State` name collisions were
fingerprinted to their new passages; obsolete Peripheral/targeted plain-text
fingerprints were removed after explicit terminology linking/rephrasing, without
weakening any reviewed-batch gate.

**Immediate artifact-integrity gate:** A private guarded publisher exported v9
twice byte-identically and confirmed exactly twelve changed descriptions among
24 Labels. Across 499 stored identities, composed-record changes were confined
to 51 records' attached Label descriptions; all other record fields, the schema,
typed Restrictions and relationships were unchanged. Only `labels`,
`vocabulary_sources` and generated `sqlite_stat1` rows differed. Maintained-text
coverage reported zero unlinked candidates or reviewed-batch residuals; the
existing Silent `scope-unclear` marker remains. Non-catalog deployed-object
classification passed before publication. The guarded three-artifact refresh
retained recovery bytes under ignored `local/` and preserved object and
variant-usage content except dependent fingerprints:

#### V1S-082 bounded source review: Trait definition conditions (2026-11-06)

**Project domain:** Data processing

Definition-level screening compared all 33 current Trait identities with English
N5.3 printed pp. 174–175. Five condensed summaries omitted source clauses that
are useful independently of a Weapon profile or a longer linked rule. The
reviewed corrections use these pinned primary inputs:

| Source | Printed page | SHA-256 |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| S1 — English N5.3 | 175 | `53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f` |
| S17 — Spanish N5.3 | 182 | `7f564c864c6f26d6b218a0477c8afeba99da5c56e1a5369bb259f423689bb28f` |
| S3 — English N5.2 | 174 | `643ab0b6d7c3e9e5ffbf668543723161f252367e7788af09ec008be19c17851b` |

Supplementary evidence is the archived English Wiki `Traits` member, revision
**4110**, SHA-256
`3ff5e1a13a4ecb9445ee2305b5d79ac952fa7d7585fd042f7f5cd24bb5ffd94f`,
from `WIKI-en-history 20260928-165727.zip` (archive SHA-256
`d49db0515420af297a7349201e8bb7750ecb422048d32bb494273ee1cea78a5d`).
This is the exact existing Wiki revision cited by the five records; no live
source was acquired or updated.

**Corrected source-to-summary gaps:**

- `trait:deployable` now explicitly states that the independent item can be
  chosen as a target, retaining its own Attributes/profile, direct
  Unconscious-to-Dead transition and prohibition on activating other Deployables.
- `trait:disposable-x` now explains X as the number of uses and exhaustion as
  entry into Unloaded, including the English source's item-specific qualification
  when a Trooper carries several Disposable items. Existing expenditure on failed
  declarations, Burst caps, shared Modes and Special Dice exceptions remain.
- `trait:non-reloadable` now states that Unloaded cannot be cancelled, as well as
  preserving the prohibition on recovering uses. It remains distinct from the
  same-named Label and does not add a new restriction relationship.
- `trait:target-attribute` now states the no-effect and no-Saving-Roll consequence
  when the target lacks the specified VITA or STR Attribute. The parameter still
  belongs to the exact source occurrence; no target evaluator was introduced.
- `trait:silent-x` now explicitly conditions its MOD on making an Attack and
  states that it stacks with other applicable Dodge MODs. The Zone of Control,
  outside-LoF and English Face to Face conditions remain; a typed link now leads
  to the general Zone of Control rule rather than the distinct range Trait.

**Differences retained:** The English N5.3, English N5.2 and archived English
Wiki agree on these added clauses. Spanish N5.3 supports the core effects but
its Disposable paragraph mentions the Trooper without the English multi-item
qualification. Its Silent paragraph does not expressly limit Dodge to Face to
Face Rolls. The English-facing scope and the existing visible bilingual note
remain; adding stacking does not settle the unresolved Silent/Attribute-Immunity
question (SRC-002). Silent's facts, source notes and review-needed marker were
verified unchanged. Spanish citation sections use the printed Spanish headings.

**No new gap confirmed at this definition scope:** the other 28 Trait identities
retain their summaries, citations, facts and relationships. This screening does
not certify every existing extended interaction or outside-page exception.
General current semantics are maintained in
[rules semantics](rules-semantics.md#rs-gsg-trait-002--traits-encode-typed-parameters-and-cross-domain-relationships).

**Immediate provenance/publication gates:** the guarded v9 exporter confirmed
five changed composed records, with all other **494 stored identities**
unchanged, and preserved all facts/relationships, the v9 schema and typed
Restrictions. Deterministic repeat exports matched byte-for-byte. Changes are
confined to the selected summaries/review dates/citations and generated SQLite
statistics. A source-locator refinement changed only four Spanish citation rows.
Semantic-link coverage has zero candidates/residuals and retains the existing
Silent `scope-unclear` marker; its obsolete plain Zone of Control fingerprint
was removed after linking the actual general rule. Non-catalog classification
passed. Both dependent publications retain their previous content except the
expected fingerprints; guarded promotion retains private recovery copies.

#### V1S-082 bounded source review: Hackable profile help (2026-11-06)

**Project domains:** Data processing, Web backend, Web frontend

The published `rule:profile-help:hackable` Unit Profile help previously named
Hackable only as a generic eligibility flag. The pinned English N5.3 Core Rules
(SHA-256 `53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`,
printed p. 8) and Spanish N5.3 Core Rules
(`7f564c864c6f26d6b218a0477c8afeba99da5c56e1a5369bb259f423689bb28f`,
printed p. 9) agree on the omitted operative conditions: HI, REM, TAG and VH
are Hackable by default unless stated otherwise, and Hackers are Hackable
regardless of Troop Type. The source permits certain allied and enemy Hacking
Program targeting, not universal access independent of individual Program
requirements. English N5.2 p. 8 corroborates the same conditions.

The summary now presents those facts in normal Unit Profile help and Glossary,
links the canonical `skill:hacker`, and cites both current language editions.
It does not change the separate Hackable Label, decide a profile's live
eligibility, or add a Hacking Program legality evaluator.

**Immediate release-artifact gates:** the two PDF byte pins matched; the
guarded v9 exporter produced byte-identical repeat builds, preserved all
**498** unrelated rule identities, schema and existing typed Restrictions.
The two dependent published payloads retained their content except updated
rules/curated-source fingerprints. The matched promotion completed with verified
rollback copies under ignored `local/`. Published SHA-256 fingerprints:

| Artifact | SHA-256 |

| `rules.db` | `c058bd638065b51a390b87df2f41d9d60666cacbff190232c41c1049d12c59cd` |

Both publication verification commands returned success after promotion.
The maintained semantic-link audit reported **zero** new candidates/residuals.

**Deferred acceptance:** VD-0077/0078/0079 were reviewed and authored without

#### V1S-082 fixed pilot: Hacking Program source family, three further records (2026-11-06)

**Scope.** The next **3 of 35** pilot identities were independently
compared against pinned English N5.3 Core Rules pp. **58–61** and Spanish
N5.3 pp. **58–61**, using the PDF byte identities stated above:
`hacking-program:cybermask`, `hacking-program:spotlight` and
`hacking-program:trinity`. Read-only comparison covered printed
Program-chart rows, individual Program clauses, the curated records,
the existing chart/source-audit contract, and the actual Program API
composition and detail renderer. No new rules, profile values or
execution behavior were authored.

- **Cybermask:** English p. 59 / Spanish p. 59 require the Hacker to be
  outside enemy Trooper/Marker LoF, then enter Impersonation-2 without
  a Roll; while it persists, the Hacker cannot use other NFB effects.
  The empty chart Target cell does **not** mean that the Program
  cannot work; it affects the user. The curated facts/labels retain
  this distinction.
- **Spotlight:** English p. 60 / Spanish p. 61 allow targeting without
  the Hackable Characteristic, with AP, PS 5, Burst 2 in the Active
  Turn and a failed Saving Roll causing Targeted. The printed Target
  dash does not prohibit the documented eligible targets. The
  composed source-statistics layer and curated source-derived effects
  remain separate.
- **Trinity:** English p. 61 / Spanish p. 61 target enemy Hackers
  specifically, apply +3 to the user's WIP Roll, and have Burst 3 in
  the Active Turn and PS 6. Each failed BTS Saving Roll causes one
  Wound. Baseline Killer Hacking Device access on p. 58 is distinct
  from explicit Army Upgrade grants on other Hacking Devices; those
  exact profile-owned upgrades were not revalidated in this pass.

**Source batching and limits.** The shared chart at English p. **58**
(and Spanish p. **58**) was inspected once for all three Program
identities. The three detailed English Program sections occupy
**three pages** (59–61), while Spanish Cybermask and
Spotlight/Trinity occupy **two** pages (59, 61). This is reuse of two
chart-page reads across three named comparisons, **not** an elapsed
time benchmark or proof that the chart layout was visually signed off
now. The earlier [source-chart audit](testing.md#n53-hacking-programs-statistics-source-chart)
already documents independent geometry-aware validation of all 12
printed Program rows. The code path in
`src/infinity_db/hacking_program_catalog.py` retains Army-authored
source statistics and enriches them with the separate reviewed
curated record; `src/infinity_db/web/static/hacking-program-detail.js`
renders the composed fields and explains a missing Troop Type.
No new discrepancy was confirmed for these scoped clauses.

**Disposition.** This brings **source-clause screening to 10/35**
pilot records, but **0/35 whole-record and current-revision
normal-browser approvals** were newly granted by this pilot.
Neither the prior 36-page browser run from October nor the prior
all-12 chart-statistics audit substitutes for new exact-candidate
acceptance. The other **25 pilot records**, independent source-first
omissions, real time-cost benchmark, and final evidence/disposition
schema remain to be reviewed. No tests or browser sessions were run
in this particular source-only pass; the active queue currently has

`cdd33879c60b490537fc7f009d5dc7c493e6bf9d3e921a8e684dd56048de103a` and
`d670525be0d73803ccdbf455e53deb67a200cd896f1142c6ceaa3082424aea50`,
respectively. The selected question is entry, cancellation and exception
coverage, not static record existence.

**Isolated State finding.** The pinned English N5.3 State page (p. 168) and
Spanish N5.3 State page (p. 161) agree on the timing: an Isolated Lieutenant
causes the army to enter Loss of Lieutenant at the beginning of its next
Active Turn unless Isolated is canceled beforehand; after the army-level
situation has begun for that reason, canceling Isolated does not itself end
Loss of Lieutenant. The exact archived English Wiki revision is oldid 4095,
member SHA-256
`630bb61419b1cb56afd5f7b9553ab72ba355e6595416ac7d78290f06eb2a4064` in
`data/wiki/WIKI-en-history 20260928-165727.zip`. The N5.3 PDF byte hashes are
recorded in the source-pin table above.

At screening time, this clause was not explicitly maintained in the
`state:isolated`, `skill:lieutenant` or `rule:loss-of-lieutenant` facts. The
State record cited the Wiki revision but not either official State page; the
Loss of Lieutenant record cited Wiki oldid 3508 and described its trigger and
ordinary replacement cancellation without this post-trigger interaction. This
was a **confirmed source-to-record coverage gap**, not an English/Spanish
source conflict. No new SRC was opened.

The gap is now corrected in all three curated records with the pinned English
p. 168 and Spanish p. 161 citations. They distinguish canceling Isolated
before the Lieutenant's next Active Turn, which prevents the trigger, from
canceling it after Loss of Lieutenant has begun, which does not itself end the
army-level situation; the Lieutenant Skill now links to the owning rule. A
guarded schema-v9 promotion changed exactly these three composed records;
the other 496 records, schema and Restrictions payload were preserved. The
tracked rules database SHA-256 is
`a2a1e1e4ccd6bd5e79375fa98762f4bf4eebcd189e0dc7279ccc97d223094729`; matched
deployed-object and variant-usage publications were verified at
`13450656eec1764a9cfcc2663133474c94ba762aff5572b4b30be6ca99e46919` and
`f5ddfc569768b522b3ab93cb59e75453dd30a4c2fa900258959354f7c88d8d74`,
respectively. The maintained-text-link audit and both publication checks pass.
These are artifact-integrity results, not test or browser acceptance: no tests
have run, and whole-record/current-revision browser approvals remain zero.
The authored record, database, API and ordinary-browser regressions remain
open under VR-0122.

**Reused State evidence.** The Prone record's current N5.3 citations cover
English pp. 29 and 169 and Spanish pp. 30 and 173; its existing facts and the
prior bilingual FAQ reconciliation in [rules semantics](rules-semantics.md)
cover Movement-labelled transitions, Jump cancellation, the Berserk
exception, Unconscious transitions and Impetuous restrictions. The archived
Wiki oldid 4121 member SHA-256 is
`7fa9793de06384e0b624e4e86462a4b16a9d8ca74351adedf7ad9e746577e154` in the
same history archive.

The Camouflaged record already cites English N5.3 pp. 87 and 157–158 and
Spanish pp. 162–163. Its current facts include activation, Active Turn
re-entry, reveal/cancellation conditions and restrictions on attacks and
AROs. Its supplementary `Camouflaged_State` snapshot member has SHA-256
`b1348b945fa6e9df97233b70b02900379cbd6776d71988cf2dd999feeecaa2a1`;
the `Labels` member has SHA-256
`3b731a8f3ac150c66b79c4e5f0f57220fe10f257a2b18e7651ef68c2a3840bb9`, both
from `data/wiki/WIKI-en 20260926-200832.zip`. No additional selected-clause
gap was identified for Prone or Camouflaged in this screen.

The cohort adds three distinct selected-clause screens, bringing the fixed

browser test for `/equipment/tinbot` checks the family route and absence of

#### V1S-082 mixed-screen benchmark (2026-11-06)

**Design and inputs.** At commit
`1a11b098d638bf89a1067cf630b35bd1a05b5f5a`, a new deterministic,
risk-stratified sample selected 40 records from English-PDF-cited candidates
after excluding the earlier pilot IDs and literal ID mentions in this audit.
The seed was `v1s082-2026-11-06-benchmark-v2`; the selection manifest SHA-256
was `65e622c0d7d6c7b547b69d957da2e402157b4f00d7c3965651640f0652def58d`.
The audit input at that commit had SHA-256
`33ccb60441a7d0832ca83b9d8abae7679c2a420d18e9911374e08825d42bc96e`.
Each arm had two records in each of ten kinds, 50 citation occurrences and one
source-specific Equipment variant; four versus five records had a Spanish PDF
citation. This deliberately mixed simple metadata, shared pages and conditional
rules, but was not population-proportional and excluded Wiki-only candidates
and other domains. The English N5.3 PDF SHA-256 was
`53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`.

The record-first schedule used a randomized record order and one first English
PDF citation per record. The family-batched schedule grouped its own records by
that primary page. Both displayed pages in windows of up to four and recorded a
separate decision for one selected material claim per record; a shared page
was not a shared semantic approval. Heading-centered crops did not always show
the whole page. The two arms used different records, one reviewer and a fixed
record-first-then-batched order. This measures a narrow screening workload, not
the complete source-to-record-to-browser review.

| Arm | Records | Logged PDF page-access events | Distinct pages | Scoped support | Unresolved | Wall time |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Record-first | 20 | 20 | 17 | 16 | 4 | 93.65 s |
| Family-batched | 20 | 18 | 18 | 18 | 2 | 89.61 s |

These are actual rendered-page access events, not separate PDF-file opens.
The wall intervals include agent and tool latency, not measured active
reviewer time. They are not a causal comparison: workload and outcomes differ,
four primary pages occur in both arms, and the second arm can benefit from
familiarity. No 50% effort reduction, population-wide estimate or false-positive
or false-negative rate is established. The 40 decisions are per-record scoped
triage, not a count of distinct independently approved source clauses. No new
whole-record or current-revision browser approvals were granted. The raw event
log SHA-256 was
`331211f5dbd5811cac22ee42a00b49f9d67285c1a9b1306e78c7924f5ed25487`.

**Reproducible sample.** All page numbers below are printed English N5.3 PDF
pages. `U` denotes an unresolved decision at the end of the timed arm; the
other entries support only the selected claim. The raw event log remains
unchanged by subsequent follow-up.

| Kind | Record-first | Family-batched |
| --- | --- | --- |

| attribute | `attribute:arm` (p. 9); `attribute:bs` (p. 9) | `attribute:c` (p. 9); `attribute:ph` (p. 9) |

| equipment | `equipment:ecm-guided` (p. 122); `equipment:repeater` (p. 56) | `equipment:holoprojector` (p. 124); `equipment:tinbot-albedo` (p. 127, U) |
| hacking-program | `hacking-program:carbonite` (p. 59, U); `hacking-program:enhanced-reaction` (p. 55, U) | `hacking-program:controlled-jump` (p. 59); `hacking-program:white-noise` (p. 61) |

**Follow-up and controls, outside the timed arms.** English p. 92 corroborated

that p. 167 was needed should not be read as proof p. 166 lacked that section.
Carbonite's full curated requirements explicitly restrict both target
categories to enemies. Disconnected's full record describes direct-attack
recovery, and the linked Peripheral record covers the Peripheral's own Isolated

primary page. These follow-ups do not alter the six original unresolved
decisions or establish whole-record acceptance.

`a2a1e1e4ccd6bd5e79375fa98762f4bf4eebcd189e0dc7279ccc97d223094729`
and Army database SHA-256

#### V1S-082 P1 source-clause evidence: Deployable and Target (Attribute) (2026-11-06)

The first tracked P1 dispositions reuse the bounded Trait source review above,
but were independently checked against the pinned **English N5.3 p. 175**
(source `n5-core-v5.3-pdf`, SHA-256
`53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`)
and **Spanish N5.3 p. 182** (source `n5-core-v5.3-es-pdf`, SHA-256
`7f564c864c6f26d6b218a0477c8afeba99da5c56e1a5369bb259f423689bb28f`).
These are two semantic questions checked in two language sources, **not four
independent mechanics**:

- `trait:deployable`: its independent battlefield item has its own profile and
  Attributes, can be targeted, enters Dead directly from Unconscious, and cannot
  activate other Deployable items. Spanish *Posicionable* agrees. This does not
  approve Place Deployable's declaration, timing or targeting rules.
- `trait:target-attribute`: the Weapon/Equipment is effective only against a
  target possessing the indicated VITA or STR Attribute; otherwise it has no
  effect and requires no Saving Roll. Spanish *Objetivo (Atributo)* agrees.
  This is **not** a profile-specific target legality or roll evaluator.

The maintained manifest
`config/validation/source-coverage-dispositions-v1.json` now holds four
**scoped semantic** approvals with separate source/page/heading keys and current
record SHA-256 fingerprints: Deployable
`3a68e086d2fad39e42f9da820e571c214afe3f0b7313b988dda35249b1394a32`
and Target (Attribute)
`1d8eb474f3330a5b7b038aecbb943c57905491757672514dd60cbcf32a455902`.
Each source entry retains its own pin and a reviewer/date pointer to this
evidence. The local validator confirms those record fingerprints against
the current Core collection and checks the source pins against declared
provenance. The prior source/API/browser executions under retired VR-0115
remain **historical bounded evidence only**; these entries all retain
`browser: pending`. No full-record, complete source-page, Wiki, current
revision browser or release acceptance is granted, and no gameplay data changed.

**Immediate source-provenance integrity gate:** On the Windows checkout,
`tests/test_source_coverage_dispositions.py` passed 23/23, and
`tests/test_silent_immunity_source_scope.py` passed 15/15 with no skips,

module reported only the pre-existing environment-wide missing `pytest`

#### V1S-082 VR drain: MOV/TinBot browser checks and Isolated repair (2026-11-06)

The normal-browser additions in `tests/test_verify_scenario_faq_browser.py`
verify keyboard navigation from Profile Attributes to `#attribute-mov` and
the exact Army source-169 TinBot: Firewall variant (`-3`/`-6` usage),

Chromium cases at 1440/390/320 px, with all **808/808** SVGs present.

backlog/record/API checks. These are bounded local results, not full
source, browser or hosted release acceptance.

The same revision's cumulative Linux code profile, job
`linux-dev:a84304fb9ba2461bb0def8fc2beeab05`, completed with
**5,365 passed, five failed, 246 skipped, two Python fork warnings**
in 669.03 s pytest time; Ruff and Pyright passed (zero type
diagnostics). The five failures had three concrete causes:
`rule:loss-of-lieutenant`'s long single-paragraph summary,
the browser assertion's mismatched capitalization in all three widths,
and an outdated source-locator assertion of **828** rather than the
current **830** after two bilingual Isolated citations. The failures
were not attributed to the new MOV/TinBot selectors. Linux lacked
ignored local source PDFs, whose selected cases were checked separately
on Windows; their Linux skips are not source acceptance.

The repair separates only the summary's existing final sentence into a
new paragraph, aligns the browser assertion with the existing
source-backed requirement and corrects the count to 830. It does **not**
change source semantics, predicates, Rules, or typed relationships.
A reusable, fail-closed `tools.refresh_reference_summary_layout`
promotion verified a deterministic v9 rebuild with the other
**498** composed records unchanged, matching Restrictions and
unchanged deployed-object/variant-usage payloads except their
source/release fingerprints. The guarded promotion retained recovery
bytes under ignored `local/`, producing:

Both dependent-publication verification commands and the scoped
source-disposition validator passed. A Windows targeted repair run
(`81c6f6be67274ad380b5aacf48bb7a9d`) passed **11/11**:
both static regressions and all nine isolated/MOV/TinBot Chromium
width cases. The cumulative Linux rerun, individual VR
reconciliation and exact-commit CI acceptance remain separate and
pending at this point.

### V1-A09 — Occurrence parameters and the usage index remain open

**Project domains:** Data processing, Web backend, Web frontend

The original raw/application/rules-pair baseline reported **37 Skill and 14
Equipment source IDs** with unresolved occurrence parameters. Since then,
reviewed Super-Jump, Jump and Climb mappings reduced the tracked Skill
baseline to **34**; reviewed TinBot Firewall, Discover and ECM Guided
modifiers reduced Equipment to **11**, and the subsequent exact
Hacking Device compound reviews reduced it to **10**;
the N5.3 Bangbomb Dodge review reduced it further to **9**;
the ECM Guided/Hacker source-specific review reduced
it again to **7**, then the ranged MediKit Burst
review reduced it to **6**, and EVO Hacking Device
Trinity access/SR reconciliation reduced it to **5**.
These are source IDs, not counts of bugs or
missing definitions.
High-occurrence examples
include BS Attack, Immunity,
Terrain, Hacker, Hacking Device, MediKit, GizmoKit and Deactivator. Existing typed
Mimetism/Berserk/Dodge/Surprise Attack mappings do not close these other meanings.

The cross-army usage index remains explicitly unimplemented in the data model.
Review exact source signatures by semantic family before index materialization;
preserve raw labels and ambiguous combinations. Required facts and context must be
usable, but optional comparison/search controls are not an independent release gate.

### V1-A10 — Reinforcements procedural authority is unresolved

**Project domain:** Data processing

The source-applicability audit verifies two current N5/ITS references and two
historical N4 annex PDFs while reporting `n5ProceduresAuthorized: false`.
Commlink remains exposed with no reviewed definition. A reference to an unversioned
annex does not establish that the available N4 procedures apply unchanged to N5.

Identify and pin an official N5-applicable revision/adoption ruling, or explicitly
resolve the supported reference boundary under the existing release policy. If
procedures are supported, deliver their typed parameter, phase and browser slice.
This is an unresolved authority/disclosure decision, not a claim that no such
official evidence exists elsewhere or that a publisher must issue a new ruling.

## Verified boundaries and remaining acceptance

The 30 independent audit invocations produced 29 successful exits and the intended
strict-primary failure on Commlink. Passing output includes:

- exact Army archive/list provenance and selected nested occurrence parity;
- all 113 requested archived Wiki revisions and the supplied snapshot/PDF pins;
- zero occupied fields among the 39 statically unrepresented Army candidates;
- no open runtime-table ownership issue in 42 runtime probes;
- 171 Weapon Chart rows, with known source differences retained, plus 90 selected
  Weaponry clauses and all 17 required typed relationships;
- bounded delivery coverage for all 17 published objects, reviewed lexical queues,
  Restrictions, Orders/AROs, Hacking, Silhouette and other selected source tables;
- matched deployed-object publication and all 808 published assets.

The selected normal browser discovery checks passed eight pages at 1440/390 px,
with no page errors, failed/HTTP-error requests or horizontal overflow. They confirm
the search omissions above, not whole-application browser acceptance.

At the audit baseline, the broader targeted test run reported **413 passed,
16 skipped, one failed**; V1-A06 records the now-resolved stale-set assertion.
The separate opt-in tracked-pair FAQ
browser run passed **16 tests**. Full final-candidate, whole-documentation, hosted CI,
manual Silhouette interaction, deployed verification and host rollback/forward
acceptance remain governed by the existing release checklist. This audit does not
substitute for those gates.

Accurately disclosed publisher disagreements remain non-blocking under the release
policy. The Kobra/Weapon Trait/Mine/Dazer source differences must not be silently
converted into application corrections. Final source disclosure acceptance remains
necessary even when an external correction is deferred.

## Recommended order for the owning backlog slices

1. Continue the selected V1S-082 source-to-browser review. The bounded source/scope
   review in V1-A03 and publication gaps in V1-A01, V1-A02 and V1-A04 are resolved;
   final-candidate scenario acceptance remains open, and this does not certify
   whole-source coverage or dispose of the **821** remaining locator reviews.
2. Reconcile the v1.0 classifications and acceptance denominator (V1-A07/08).
3. Review remaining exact occurrence families and their required published context
   (V1-A09), while resolving the supported Reinforcements boundary (V1-A10).
4. Freeze the intended inputs and run the existing final source-to-browser and
   release acceptance gates. Any additional confirmed coverage gap needs an owning
   task before acceptance; this assessment does not bound all undiscovered work.

**This is a new, bounded section/family classification at
`470ce3b80b20c175bf7fd90e756c493911e90322`.** It does not
rewrite the October 31 findings or replace the frozen explanation
inventory. The selected primary source is the English N5.3 v5.3
PDF (SHA-256 `53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`),
with its own printed contents on pp. 2–4. It is paired with the
selected September 29 Army ZIP, the matched tracked
`infinity.db`/`rules.db` and the separately pinned bilingual
FAQ/Wiki evidence in [N5 source history](n5-source-history.md).
No live source was acquired and no release identity changed.

The current `Core + Hacking` inventory has **450 record
identities**, against **367** frozen in October: **350 retained,
100 added and 17 retired**. The added identities are 20
declaration categories, three Equipment records, 17 Rules,
two Skills, five Terms and 53 Weapons. The crosswalk retains
**46 historically screening-only** current records and
**743 source locators still needing full clause/browser
dispositions**. The **23-category / 126-source-group**
baseline spans Army and all rules collections; those numbers
are *not* distinct PDF headings or complete semantic reviews.

are saved under ignored `local/audits/v1s-016-*.json`.

### Source-section to model, storage and browser dispositions

Each line identifies the **source scope**, current maintained
owner, storage/read model, natural ordinary browser entry point
and exact *remaining* review boundary. A route is not approval
of every rule clause, and successful entry-point tests do not
replace full-record acceptance.

- **Introduction (p. 5):** the Objectives/Game Summary prose
  is overview context rather than a separate required
  `rule:*` identity. Existing Game Modes, phases and scenarios
  provide the operative references through `rules.db` and
  `/rules`/`/scenarios`. **Intentional contextual scope**;
  ensure no separate win-condition mechanic is omitted during
  V1S-039/042; see the [V1S-026 general reference review](#general-rules-training-and-glossary-scope-v1s-026-2026-11-04),
  rather than forcing a duplicate overview card.
- **Basic Rules (pp. 6–26):** curated Rules, Orders/Training,
  Attributes, Terms, declaration categories and related Skills
  own source mechanics. `rules.db` publishes these records;
  Army profile data stays in `infinity.db`. Ordinary routes
  include `/rules`, `/glossary`, `/orders-aros`
  and contextual `/skills`. Initiative, Combat Groups, Game
  Modes and the Orders/AROs matrix have independent source
  and browser tests. **Selected cases pass**; unaccepted
  order/phase, LoF, measurement and information exceptions
  remain V1S-032/033 after the bounded V1S-026 identity review
  and V1S-029's historical evidence join.

  `skill:jump` and associated Rule/geometry records are

  or `/rules`. Exact N5.3 Jump/Climb declaration
  categories and selected distance qualifiers have prior
  evidence. **Partial clause acceptance**; geometry,
  silhouette/contact and movement exceptions remain
  V1S-033; see the [V1S-024 Skill-family disposition](#skill-semantic-family-coverage-and-deferred-links-v1s-024-2026-11-04).

  Normal/Face to Face, Burst, MOD, PS/Saving Roll, Guts,

  Army profiles/Weapons remain distinct `infinity.db`

No **newly confirmed** gameplay-data or normal-browser defect

## Weaponry profile, prose and exception triage (V1S-019; 2026-11-03)

**Disposition:** bounded **source classification complete**, not final
gameplay/browser acceptance. Read-only auditors were rerun against
the pinned English N5.3 PDF
(`53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`),
Army database
(`8a5840d1ff632e89de26210ee7bcb47e54c6579c15b5c3436e226702e872d7c8`),
and tracked rules database
(`c016006b7eae19e80969f432a590b802bf989b5831bd6cfd1c1f26765bb3800c`).
Private JSON/Markdown reports are in ignored `local/audits/v1s-019-*`;
no raw publication was copied into the repository.

- **Shared effects versus missing definitions:** the current Army
  enrichment audit still flags **63** Weapons (62 standalone-rule
  definition omissions, one historical Flash Pulse citation).
  The independently rebuilt Weapon gap triage `--assess-release 1.0.0`
  classifies **62** as explicitly reviewed shared-effect cases,
  **one** as accepted historical citation, **two** former shared-effect
  omissions as now having current definitions (Crazykoala, Madtraps),
  and **zero** remaining *source/effect/mode triage* candidates.

- **Printed chart profiles:** `audit_weapon_chart_profiles` aligned
  **171/171** source rows and modes; 170 match all five profile fields
  and one is the already reviewed **Kobra Pistol CC** 1-versus-2

  CC Anti-materiel remains disputed as **SRC-021**. The single
  Katyusha MRL bare `3` versus `+3` range anomaly is the
  previously documented PDF sign-omission typo
  **RR-SRC-N53-003**; do not generalize a sign-normalization rule.
  The extractor does not prove full profile semantics.
- **Weapon Traits and footnotes:** of **171** aligned Trait rows,
  **145** literally match, **16** use reviewed notation aliases,
  **two** use verified source-cell readings and **eight** differ.
  The pinned Wiki `Weapon_Chart` revision `4083` comparison
  retains all **nine** historical reviewed cases (including a
  now-matching Cybermine): Wiki agrees with PDF on seven, Army
  on one and both on one; eight semantic interpretations are explained,
  one partial. The eight divergences are **Drop Bears BS**'s
  historical Throwing Weapon term; PARA Mine's `[*]`/`[**]`
  reference section; WildParrot's missing Army Non-Lethal label;
  the three PT entries' older Technical Weapon term (plus Endgame
  Double Shot); Kobra CC Anti-materiel; and Sepsitor Plus's
  omitted `[*]`. Keep exact imported fields and the reviewed
  provenance/conflict disclosures. **V1S-028/034/070–072**
  own any further source-backed facts, aliases, mode and browser
  presentation decisions.
- **Weaponry prose and Weapon Properties:** the **12** indexed
  named prose headings on N5.3 pp. 68–74 have 11
  exact-name curated Weapon entries; **Perimeter Weapons** has
  no dedicated same-name Weapon record, but its selected placement
  and Boost clauses are represented through `trait:perimeter`,
  `trait:boost`, `skill:place-deployable` and individual
  Weapons, not a missing gameplay mechanic by name alone.
  The selected **90** independently pinned clauses are
  explicitly represented in curated summaries, with **0**
  missing reviewed concepts and **17/17** required typed edges.
  The **12** descriptive `weapon-property:*` definitions
  already cover the approved Plasma, Viral, Breaker, K1, MULTI,
  Monofilament, Silenced, AP-combined and N+E/M cases;
  these are not Army Traits or ammunition grants.
  Remaining Properties and **unselected** pp. 68–74
  prose/exception clauses still require source-by-source
  review under **V1S-034/038**. Do not imply that 90 checked
  clauses exhaust the module.
- **Exceptional deployed/equipment profiles:** the separate Weapon
  Chart supplementary table has **14** matching Army identities

  rerun bilingual PDFs and both archived Wiki charts agree on

  Dazer has **S0/S1** disagreement across PDF, Wiki charts,

  The independent N5.3 **p. 74** quick-reference audit covers
  **13** printed rows and **10** distinct reference objects,
  preserving repeated names and the separate Armed Turret
  quick-reference **S1** versus detailed **S2** conflict.
  The broad curated Weapon/Equipment clause queue and expanded
  non-catalog queue each have **zero** unreviewed candidates
  (**25** expanded and **51** non-catalog reviewed distinctions).
  Non-four-attribute examples are distinct: **Deployable Cover**
  has a source-backed S3 Scenery Element but no conventional
  ARM/BTS/STR profile; **D-Charges** resolve within an Order;
  **SymbioBomb** is assignable, not a persistent deployed object;

  Broader nonstandard Equipment wording and independent
  source-table/object discovery stay **V1S-073–078**, following

**Remaining gates:** source clause-by-clause semantic acceptance
(**V1S-034/038**), exact modes and all public Weapon profiles
(**V1S-070–072**), newly identified or excluded objects
(**V1S-073–078**), cross-publication uncertainty
(**V1S-028/083**) and full release/browser acceptance
(**V1S-081–090**). The historical Weapon gap policy's
zero pending count is not the strict v1.0 source/browser verdict.
**No new confirmed gameplay-data bug** was established by
this classification pass, so source data, rules databases and
runtime code remain unchanged.

The existing `reinforcements-extra-n4-reference` collection is

## Ranked confirmed gaps and evidence batches (V1S-022; 2026-11-03)

**Scope:** this ranks *demonstrated incomplete contracts and acceptance
evidence*, not conjectural gameplay errors. The authoritative
1.0 standard is [the release gate](releasing.md#version-10-data-completeness-gate):
missing player-relevant facts or misleading source claims must be
resolved, but an unimplemented optional comparison UI or missing
publisher erratum is not automatically a release blocker.
Ranks below are relative **work order**, not assertions that every
entry is a P0 defect. Owners are existing unchecked V1S slices;
the list does not create parallel duplicate TODO tasks.

### Rank 1 — exact variant occurrence semantics (confirmed review gap)

- **Evidence/source:** the read-only `tools.audit_source_variant_inputs`
  against the current matched Army/rules/raw databases reports
  **10 Skill source IDs** with unresolved occurrence-parameter
  signatures: Infiltration `47`, Engineer `49`, Doctor `53`,
  Religious Troop `83`, Immunity `162`, BS Attack `201`,
  CC Attack `240`, Peripheral `243`, Transmutation `246`
  and Journalist `272`. One Equipment source ID,
  Hacking Device Plus `101`, remains similarly classified.
  Sources: selected September 29, 2026 English Army ZIP and
  exact applicable N5.3/FAQ rules, with special historical
  uncertainty in SRC-028/029 and Upgrade Program reviews.
  These are **11 partially unreviewed source families**, *not*
  eleven missing definitions or every one of their direct
  Army occurrences being unsupported. Previously reviewed
  signatures within the families remain valid.
- **Missing contract / owner:** classify each remaining raw
  ordered `source ID + extras` signature by the owning
  Skill/Equipment and its exact use scope, leaving genuinely
  unknown semantics fail-closed; V1S-050–061, then V1S-062.
  Highest priority goes to meanings that would change a
  player's Roll, eligibility, target or state, **not** an
  inferred global signed-MOD transformation.
- **Normal read surface:** relevant `/skills/<slug>` or
  `/equipment/<slug>`, linked to actual Unit/profile/loadout
  contexts; no new global variant meaning may be implied by
  shared raw labels.
- **Required checks:** rerun `tools.audit_source_variant_inputs
  --catalog skills|equipment --unresolved-only --brief`,
  pinned raw/application/rules signature comparisons,
  `tests/test_audit_source_variant_inputs.py` and
  source-specific owner tests; verify any promoted pair through
  guarded v9 artifact/release checks and normal browser for
  affected source variants.

### Rank 2 — cross-Army exact-variant usage projection (confirmed delivery gap)

- **Evidence/source:** the [accepted design](data-model.md#rule-variant-usage-index--implemented-release-artifact-gated)
  requires the canonical Skill/Equipment → reviewed
  Level/MOD/parameter → exact Army/Unit/profile/loadout usage index
  that was **not generated or served** at this 2026-10-31 baseline.
  The later [development candidate](#variant-usage-candidate-foundation-v1s-063-2026-11-05)
  does not yet close the public delivery gap. The current Army September 29
  source contains the occurrence owners; reviewed rules identities
  already exist in `rules.db`. Absence of this projection is
  confirmed, but the release gate allows basic navigable
  presentation instead of a specialized comparison feature.
- **Missing contract / owner:** V1S-062–066 must settle stable
  occurrence identity, source pair version, duplicate/Includes/
  Peripheral ownership, unknown exclusion, deterministic
  projection, read API and ordinary reverse links.
  **Dependency:** Rank 1's typed signatures; publish the
  reviewed subset rather than fabricating unknown variants.
- **Normal read surface:** `/skills` and `/equipment`
  definition/detail pages with links to relevant `/units`
  profiles/loadouts; an API-only lookup is insufficient for
  the required usable context.
- **Required checks:** exact raw-to-application-to-rules
  owner/provenance parity; repeat sources/duplicate handling;
  `tests/test_audit_source_variant_inputs.py`, new index
  negative/freshness regressions, normal desktop/mobile
  browser reachability and matched release-pair checks.

### Rank 3 — whole-source explanation/relationship/browser acceptance (confirmed evidence gap)

- **Evidence/source:** `tools.report_reference_baseline
  --reconcile-explanation-inventory` against current
  N5.3/Core/Hacking reports **450 current records**, compared
  with **367 frozen** (350 retained, 100 added, 17 retired).
  **743 source locators** still require individual
  *clause and browser dispositions*. Neither the 23 category/
  126 source-group inventory, selected PDF/Wiki clauses, a
  citation, nor V1S-021's passing 186-primary identity gate
  supplies that acceptance. These are **pending proofs**,
  not 743 confirmed missing facts.
- **Missing contract / owner:** the V1S-029 lineage/evidence join and
  bounded V1S-030 conditional review are complete;
  V1S-032–035 identify each source-native mechanic, explicit exception,
  material relation and normal entry, with source-version
  status and honest absence/conflict disclosure; V1S-082/086
  consolidate the final accepted coverage.
- **Normal read surface:** `/rules`, `/skills`,
  `/equipment`, `/states`, `/traits`, `/hacking-programs`,
  `/orders-aros`, `/ammunition` plus contextual links
  from related Army/Unit/Weapon pages.
- **Required checks:** `tests/test_report_reference_baseline.py`,
  exact N5.3 section/source inventories, audited explanation
  crosswalk and relational read models, then normal
  `test_verify_rule_fragment_browser.py` and wider
  source/route assertions on a *frozen* candidate. Do not
  automatically mark historical S/D records signed off.

### Rank 4 — remaining exceptional source and profile acceptance (confirmed coverage gap)

- **Evidence/source:** selected N5.3 Weaponry pp. 63–74,
  charts pp. 176–188 and the September Army snapshot have
  **171** aligned Weapon Chart rows and **90** previously
  reviewed Weaponry clauses, but those are only bounded
  checks. **62** reviewed shared-effect Weapon omissions
  are *not* 62 source-confirmed missing gameplay definitions.
  The four Core scenarios have **24** supported point/distance
  configurations and the dated FAQ has **29** selected rulings;
  neither subset is yet accepted against the final source/
  release/browser pair. The 17 deployed-object references
  do not certify all unusual source objects or carriers.
- **Missing contract / owner:** V1S-034/036–039/070–078
  for full Weapon/Ammunition/objects, V1S-042–045 for
  four Core scenarios, and V1S-046–049 for FAQ
  provenance, contextual selection and reverse links.
  Promote only an independently located missing fact,
  relation or misleading presentation; record other rows as
  checked or explicitly excluded. Source-specific
  conflicts belong to V1S-083, following V1S-028's source-disclosure review.
- **Normal read surface:** `/weapons`, `/ammunition`,
  `/deployed-objects`, `/scenarios`, and the owning
  normal Skill/Rule/FAQ topic pages.
- **Required checks:** pinned Weapon trait/chart/family
  audits and the full published-profile/API/Chromium acceptance
  recorded below; deployed
  object inventory and source-quick-profile checks;
  24-scenario configuration and FAQ source/browser suites.
  Run the release-only FAQ tracked-pair parity check
  **only** after the exact pair has been rebuilt.

### Rank 5 — candidate-specific operational and accessibility proof (confirmed unrun gate)

- **Evidence/source:** the above audits are local, subset
  or current-checkout evidence; no final frozen 1.0 candidate
  has passed the entire source/asset, release-only browser,
  host and human acceptance sequence. This is a **validation
  obligation**, not a demonstrated frontend defect.
- **Missing contract / owner:** V1S-079–080 and V1S-081–095,
  with independent human/host authorization where required.
  Recheck source/asset hashes, publication pairs, unknown/
  not-applicable values and the accuracy notice before
  merging/tagging/deploying.
- **Normal read surface:** all published routes, notably
  Unit/profile and reference navigation at narrow/touch/
  keyboard layouts; exact hosted URLs must match the
  tagged revision rather than a convenient development copy.
- **Required checks:** tracked complete local/Linux checks,
  CI, matched-pair/reproducibility preflight, 81-page Unit
  browser matrix, normal FAQ/scenario flows, manual
  accessibility/visual acceptance and exact-host deployed
  revision verification.

**Source conflicts and deliberate exclusions — not confirmed
application defects:** no verified N5-native Reinforcements Annex
(SRC-030) is required to retain the explicitly labelled N4-origin
optional reference. Kobra CC's Anti-materiel and chart save count,
Domination 350-point SWC, Mine/Dazer/Armed Turret silhouettes and
Haris/other bilingual FAQ differences remain independently
documented source claims requiring **clear public disclosure**,
not inferred repairs or prerequisites for a publisher ruling.
Similarly, the eight unselected ITS-only FAQ answers, 111 future
interaction leads, 62 reviewed Weapon shared-effect omissions,
reviewed no-profile non-devices, and optional comparison/roster
calculators are **excluded from confirmed missing-rule counts**.
A newly discovered misleading disclosure or wrong published
value must be promoted to its owning substantive V1S slice
with reproducible source/route evidence.

**Disposition:** this ranked register closes the *gap discovery,
triage and ownership* batch only. No new current product defect
was proven by the inventories alone. The remaining review,
build, release gate, actual official clarification requests
and human/host checks are unchanged and cannot be marked
accepted by this document.

## Skill semantic-family coverage and deferred links (V1S-024; 2026-11-04)

**Disposition:** reviewed *Skill-family inventory, maintained meanings,
source provenance and published-link boundaries*. The current
`data/generated/infinity.db` and `rules.db` are still paired with
the selected September 29 Army snapshot and N5.3 English Core PDF
(SHA-256 `53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`).
Neither a passing identity review nor this bounded family triage
constitutes **full page-by-page Skill/Rule explanation sign-off**;
V1S-032–037 and V1S-081–088 retain that obligation;
  the V1S-029 join was evidence bookkeeping, not full explanation sign-off.

**Coverage findings:** `tools.audit_enrichment_coverage` reports
**98/98 published Skill catalog identities with a curated definition,
zero missing Skill definitions and zero unresolved catalog relation
targets**. `tools.audit_rules_interactions` with the explicit
`catalog-scope-1.0.0.json` passes **186/186** primary and
**205/205** supporting review identities (all catalogs). The
maintained Skill records use reviewed Core/Army source maps rather
than treating a source Skill with a numerical/named parameter as
another canonical Skill. A selected source-variant inventory
independently reports **96 Army Skill source IDs with direct
occurrences**, of which **10** families still contain
unreviewed occurrence signatures; those families are not missing
Skill definitions, and their already-reviewed individual
signatures remain valid.

**Family-by-family source/semantics and presentation disposition:**

- **Attacks, defense and reactive declarations:** BS Attack,
  CC Attack, Dodge, Reset, Berserk, Guard, Discover,
  Intuitive Attack, Surprise Attack, Marksmanship, Martial Arts,
  Mimetism, Triangulated Fire and related Skills retain
  source-cited definitions and reviewed conditional interaction
  examples. Stable graph edges connect ordinary action reuse;
  reaction timing, per-target MODs, declaration eligibility,
  Cover/Range bypass and multi-effect precedence cannot be
  generalized from these edges. **Remaining:** V1S-034/036–038 after the bounded V1S-030
  conditional-declaration crosswalk, and exact BS Attack `201`/CC Attack
  `240` signature review (V1S-055–056). Normal surfaces:
  `/skills`, `/orders-aros`, `/ammunition` and context
  on `/weapons`.
- **Deployment, movement and terrain:** Aerial, Combat Jump,
  Parachutist, Infiltration, Forward Deployment, Climb,
  Climbing Plus, Jump, Super-Jump, Terrain, Minelayer and
  deployment Marker Skills have cited placement, entry and
  prohibition contracts. Aerial's Guard/Cautious Movement/
  Boost/Prone/Engaged edges are stable; Climbing Plus
  explicitly reuses Climb and affects Move/Dodge. Its
  failed-Guts vertical movement is already linked from the
  current `rule:guts-roll`, while terrain-type matching,
  Partial Cover suppression and failure-only deployment
  restrictions still require qualified representations.
  **Remaining:** V1S-033, Infiltration `47`
  (five bare ReRoll signatures remain unapproved;
  V1S-050), and source variants/geometry V1S-061.
  Normal surfaces: `/skills`, `/rules` and Unit
  placement/variant context.
- **Recovery, survivability and resource effects:** Doctor,
  Engineer, Paramedic, Regeneration, Remote Presence,
  Dogged, NWI, Shasvastii, Protheion and Immunity distinguish
  State transitions, Roll prerequisites, actual affected
  Trooper/Equipment, extra Criticals and failure outcomes.
  The `rule:saving-rolls` reference already exists; a
  generic `applies-effects-to` edge for Immunity still
  overstates ammunition/component-specific effects.
  **Remaining:** patient allegiance/source uncertainty
  (REA-038), Immunity `162`, Doctor `53`,
  Engineer `49` (V1S-051–054), and conditional
  results under V1S-036–038 after the V1S-029 evidence join. Normal surfaces:
  `/skills`, `/states` and related recovery Equipment.
- **Morale, command and game phases:** Courage's *optional*
  automatic Guts pass is different from Religious Troop's
  compulsory pass and possible WIP-based failed-Guts
  outcome; Warhorse, Frenzy, Impetuous, Lieutenant,
  NCO and Inspiring Leadership also use distinct
  source-defined Retreat, LoL, Command Token and Order
  rules. `rule:guts-roll` and `rule:army-retreat`
  **now exist** and link their Skill concepts in prose.
  The older future ledger's `rule:retreat` placeholder

  **Remaining:** the source `83` Religious Troop anomaly
