- **Peripherals, profile changes and Trooper roles:**
  Peripheral/G: Jumper, RemDriver, TAGCom, Mnemonica,
  Morpho-Scan, Transmutation, Technorganic, FT Master,
  Number 2 and specialist Skills require source-owner
  identity and receiving participant context. The
  canonical `rule:combat-group` now exists but does
  not by itself encode G: Jumper's multiple Proxies
  as one Trooper; an unconditional graph relation
  would misstate ownership. **Remaining:** Peripheral
  `243`, Transmutation `246`, occurrence/Unit
  identity (V1S-057–058/062–066), and conditional
  subgroup/role interactions (V1S-032/039).
  Normal surfaces: `/skills`, `/units`,
  `/fireteams` and parent Army context.
- **Hacking, chart-based and scenario-specific Skills:**
  Hacker, Forward Observer, Specialist Operative, Booty,
  MetaChemistry, Infinity Spec-Ops, Activate Disco Ball,
  Request SpecBall and the optional Reinforcements
  pair have separate source/scope owners. The actual
  N5.3 Hacking Area and shared chart records remain
  distinct from named Program Upgrade grants, random
  outcomes and mission/session legality. Commlink and
  Request Reinforcements remain *N4-origin optional
  reference* entries, not authorized N5-native procedures.
  **Remaining:** Hacking/FAQ scope V1S-035/039–041,
  chart and scenario context V1S-042–049, Journalist
  `272` (campaign versus ordinary Guts, SRC-029,
  V1S-059). Normal surfaces: `/skills`,
  `/hacking-programs`, `/scenarios` and
  `/rules/reinforcements-extra`.

also distinguish canonical targets from conditional
graph-edge acceptance. Regenerated
`docs/rules-interaction-checklist.md` records these
source-identity corrections.

## Equipment semantic-family and exceptional-profile review (V1S-025; 2026-11-04)

**Scope and provenance:** bounded review of public Equipment definitions,
their exact Army source variants, the current maintained N5.3 PDF
and Wiki-backed family contracts, and separately owned unusual
Weapon/Equipment objects. This is *not* complete N5.3 Equipment
clause-by-clause acceptance or proof of all normal browser
configurations. Sources retain their independently pinned identities:
the selected September 29 Army snapshot, English N5.3 PDF
(SHA-256 `53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`),
its Spanish counterpart, FAQ v0.1 where applicable, and
the exact reviewed Wiki revisions.

**Catalog and source-variant crosswalk:** on the tracked Army/rules
pair, `tools.audit_enrichment_coverage` reports **31/31** exposed
public Equipment identities with curated definitions and **zero
missing Equipment definition/related-link gaps**. The strict
1.0 interaction scope includes the same 31 reviewed primary
Equipment entries. Separately, the raw Army occurrence audit
has **34** published Equipment source IDs and **5,427** direct
occurrences. Only **Hacking Device Plus source ID 101** retains
an unresolved occurrence-parameter family (126 direct rows,
seven raw extra-signature shapes). This does *not* imply that
all seven signatures or all 126 occurrences lack an effect:
previously reviewed Trinity, Program Upgrade and other exact
forms must retain their source-specific typed interpretations;
V1S-060/061 and V1S-035 own the remaining distinction.

**Family dispositions and normal reader destinations:**

- **Recovery and resource Equipment:** MediKit, GizmoKit, Cube,
  Cube 2.0 and Baggage have reviewed Skill/State or source
  effects. MediKit and GizmoKit are not the same Roll or
  recovery target; Paramedic equips MediKit, while
  Tech-Recovery/Engineer own their separate cancellation
  conditions. Baggage automatically restores eligible
  equipment in the States Phase; Reload is a distinct
  Short Skill/ARO. Relevant readers should reach
  `/equipment`, `/skills` and `/states` with
  conditional non-Null, controller and target constraints,
  not unconditional reusable recovery effects.
- **Hacking, network and modifier variants:** Hacking Device,
  Hacking Device Plus, Killer/EVO Devices, Repeater,
  Deployable Repeater, FastPanda, Firewall, ECM and TinBot
  have different baseline ownership. The canonical
  `equipment:firewall` now exists with N5.3 p.55 and
  applicable FAQ v0.1 evidence; Device-granted Firewall
  stops when its Device is disabled. TinBot: Firewall
  source `169` has separately pinned `-3/-6` enemy
  Comms Attack MODs, **on the TinBot's owner**, not a
  device-wide unconditional modification. The family
  does not inherit every named TinBot variant, and its
  other Albedo/Discover/ECM Guided/Repeater/Neurocinetics
  variants retain distinct target identities and
  exact Army occurrences. Baseline Programs remain
  Army-metadata derived, not recreated as generic Equipment
  edges. Public destinations: `/equipment`,
  `/hacking-programs`, `/skills` and contextual
  `/units` profile/loadout occurrences. Remaining
  Hacking qualifiers/Firewall network ownership:
  V1S-035/060–066.
- **Visibility, protection and representation:** 360º Visor,
  Multispectral Visor, Biometric Visor, Albedo,
  Nanoscreen, HoloMask, Holoprojector and X-Visor
  use source-specific attack, Discover, LoF or Marker
  conditions; a linked State entry is not blanket
  unconditional State revelation or suppression.
  MSV3's Active-Turn exception and the bilingual Dodge
  (ARM+3) disagreement remain separately reviewed.
  Browsable through `/equipment`, `/skills`,
  `/states` and `/traits`, with V1S-028/033/037
  owning further source/interaction acceptance.
- **Carriers, vehicles and profile transitions:**
  Motorcycle restricts specific mounted actions and Prone
  entry; AI Motorcycle reuses Motorcycle, Peripheral
  (Synchronized) and Transmutation with *mounted versus
  dismounted* context. Escape System reuses Transmutation
  but a profile transition is not an extra base Equipment
  Attribute or a second Unit identity. SymbioMate reuses
  Immunity with its own applicability conditions.
  Public `/equipment`, `/skills` and relevant
  `/units`; further exact profile/state ownership
  remains with the downstream semantic owners
  V1S-039/057–058.
- **Deployables, scenery and special physical profiles:**
  Dazer, Bangbomb, Deployable Cover, Deactivator,
  Deployable Repeater and FastPanda are not all
  four-Attribute deployed objects. Independently
  reviewed N5.3 p.74 provides **13 printed rows /
  10 distinct reference objects** with known duplicates
  and one generic Mines family row; Armed Turret
  **S1 quick reference versus S2 detailed** and
  Dazer **S0/S1** source disagreements are disclosed.
  Six separate named Mines retain source-S0 versus
  imported Army-S1 uncertainty. Deployable Cover is
  an **S3 Scenery Element** without a conventional
  ARM/BTS/STR entry, **D-Charges** resolve within an
  Order, **SymbioBomb** is assignable/nonpersistent,
  and **Deactivator** is a user-held tool. Do not fill
  absent fields with zeroes, synthesize a deployable
  record for every tool/option, or silently resolve
  conflicts. The dedicated reader surfaces are
  `/deployed-objects`, `/equipment`, `/weapons`
  and source-linked Unit/Skill pages; independent
  source-table discovery, profile delivery and
  browser verification remain V1S-073–078.

**Maintained ledger correction:** The N5.3 Firewall rule was
already published, but the TinBot Firewall future-interaction
entry still said Firewall was *not* a standalone canonical rule.
Updated that reason and
[the TinBot family contract](rules-semantics.md#rs-eq-core-005--tinbot-family-browsing-does-not-erase-exact-variant-interactions)
to distinguish **available canonical reference** from
**an unapproved generic `uses-effects-of` graph edge**:
the exact variant's owner, modifier and applicable
Fireteam/network conditions need to be preserved. This
does not create a new rule interpretation; the existing
`[[equipment:firewall]]` link, source 169 typed
`enemy-comms-attack-mod` and Firewall facts already
provide source-backed user context. The interaction
remains in the future queue, with **111 deferred
interactions, 518 authored edges**, and no change to
published rules or the paired databases. A regression
asserts that the canonical target is retained without
promoting the generalized link.

**Acceptance boundary:** the basic public Equipment
definition/identity audit is complete, but this review
does not establish all exact Equipment occurrence semantics,
full linked-profile display or source-wide correctness.
V1S-028/029/035/037, V1S-060–066,
V1S-073–078 and frozen candidate V1S-081–090
remain open for exceptions, precise variants, actual
browser access and source-accurate profile values.
The external N4-origin optional Reinforcements annex is
not an N5-native Equipment rule authority. No newly
verified missing public Equipment definition or
fabricated profile fact warrants a gameplay-data change.

**Focused validation at `2824ebc2`:**

`tests/test_other_source_variant_provenance.py` **4 passed**,
`tests/test_audit_enrichment_coverage.py` **17 passed** and
`tests/test_deployed_object_quick_profiles.py` **3 passed**
(**39 total**). The read-only source-variant and N5.3 p.74

browser, full-source and external authority gates remain open.

## General Rules, Training and glossary scope (V1S-026; 2026-11-04)

**Review boundary:** the selected September 29 Army snapshot,
the pinned N5.3 English Core PDF (SHA-256
`53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`),
its independently reviewed Spanish/Wiki counterparts,
and the already published current rules/Army pair.
This is a *family and scope crosswalk*, not final
clause-by-clause approval or an independent translation
of the entire rulebook. `tools.report_reference_baseline`
still reports **23 category rows and 126 source groups**
requiring final dispositions. The active curated rules
database has **52 `rule:*` identities, 23 Terms,
two Training definitions and 13 Attributes**;
these are different populations from the 27
1.0-dated supplementary Rules, the historical
explanation inventory and the 186-primary identity gate.

**General-rule and reader dispositions:**

- **Pregame, Orders, phases and dice:** Initiative Roll,
  Combat Group, Regular/Irregular Order, Loss of
  Lieutenant, Order Expenditure Sequence, Coordinated
  Orders, Face to Face Rolls, Saving Rolls, Guts Roll,
  Special Dice and modifiers each have a maintained
  canonical reference and reviewed source locators.
  `/rules`, `/orders-aros`, `/skills` and
  contextual Unit references are the normal
  player entry points. The 57 published
  declaration/ARO rows are not an exhaustive
  semantic review of declaration eligibility,
  timing, reactive opponents or roll precedence:
  these remain V1S-032/034 after the V1S-029 evidence join
  and the bounded V1S-030 declaration review.
- **General game mode, scenery and ending context:**
  Game Modes' six recommended setup rows,
  Standard Game scoring/ending, army-level Retreat!,
  Special Terrain and Access Width are distinct
  `rule:*` records with printed N5.3 source
  versions. A recommended game-size value is
  not an Army-wide legal roster constraint;
  an army-level Retreat! trigger is not the
  same as Trooper `state:retreat`; table-specific
  Terrain and Gate rules are not globally
  asserted Unit properties. Read through
  `/rules`, `/glossary`, scenario and
  relevant State/Skill pages. Remaining
  exception/geometry/ending acceptance is
  V1S-032/033/039/042–045.
- **Training and profile-help identity:** the two
  `training:regular` / `training:irregular`
  references explain Order generation and
  availability, but are not replacement
  Skill or Army-profile flags. Source-native
  source Training, the associated General
  Order references and profile-help definitions
  retain their separate representations.
  `/rules`, `/glossary`, Unit/profile
  help and rendered Orders/AROs are the
  entry points; cross-army occurrence/ownership
  remains V1S-032/062–066. No standalone
  direct Training detail route is required
  when the embedded/general presentation is
  understandable and linked.
- **Glossary and descriptive terminology:** the
  **23** curated `term:*` identities,
  **13** Attributes, categorized Labels and
  the profile-help reference group are
  maintained data, not synonyms for Skills,
  Equipment or runtime State values. `/glossary`
  and `/labels` deliberately embed many of
  these instead of allocating separate URLs
  per term; contextual links in `/rules`,
  `/skills` and Unit profiles complete the
  normal access path. Selected Null and
  Visibility Zone concepts have distinct
  audited identities. **Not accepted here:**
  every term's full source section, every
  label/Attribute relation or the five newer
  Visibility Zone terms' final entire-corpus
  check (V1S-068–069), following the V1S-027 family review
  and the five-term V1S-031 source/browser sign-off
  and V1S-029 selected evidence join.
- **Core scenario/shared vocabulary:** four Core
  scenarios are part of 1.0 and the fifth
  current scenario is a separately scoped ITS
  pilot. Shared Killing, Supply Boxes,
  Specialists, Consoles, and objective/role
  terminology are available through maintained
  `rule:scenario:*` and scenario-included
  components, not by copying each fact into
  a global source Rule. Specifically,
  `rule:specialist-troops:standard` has an
  **unscoped InfinityDB-owned General Rules
  presentation** (`/rules/specialist-troops-standard`
  and search/Glossary), even though its
  *official source locators* are scenario
  sections. The actual selected scenario may
  add/remove qualifying Skills; the baseline
  is not a universal permanent Unit property.
  Scenario source version, objective relevance,
  specialist/FAQ applicability and ordinary
  browser linking remain V1S-039/042–049.
- **Hacking boundary:** `rule:hacking-area`,
  `rule:supportware` and general modifier/
  roll identities supply common context,
  while 12 first-class Hacking Program records
  and Army-derived Device/Upgrade entitlements
  retain their own exact scopes. V1S-035/060–061
  own unaccepted Hacking conditions. Do not
  inflate general Rule or glossary coverage by
  duplicating Program effects or treating
  historical N4 Reinforcements procedures as
  N5-native.

**Three stale review references corrected, without
a gameplay change:** two future edges (Surprise
Attack and Suppressive Fire State) targeted the old
singular `rule:face-to-face-roll` identity.
Both now target the existing canonical

Existing source-approved public content
already handles those distinctions.
The future ledger still has **111** entries
and the authored interaction count remains
**518**; no historic approval/review dates
or published Army/rules database bytes changed.

**Completion boundary:** maintained
identity, source ownership, and intended
general/embedded/scenario read surfaces
are classified. No new material
missing general Rule/Term/Training definition
was proven by this bounded inventory;
absence of an individual route per embedded
Term or Attribute is not a defect.

## States, Traits and Attributes with distinct semantics (V1S-027; 2026-11-04)

**Boundary:** reviewed semantic-family and public-link dispositions
against the pinned English N5.3 Core PDF (SHA-256
`53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`),
the source-scoped Spanish N5.3 checks, the archived English
Wiki `Traits` heading/fragment inventory (revision 4110), and the
tracked September 29 Army/rules database pair. This audits canonical
identity versus player-facing presentation, not every State's
full source clauses or all applicable roll/role exceptions.
The V1S-029 historical evidence join is now complete, while the
final V1S-081–090 source and browser gates remain unchanged.

**Observed identity and route coverage:**

- **24 in-game `state:*` records** have reviewed identities,
  current source references, reciprocal navigation where relevant,
  and normal `/states` / `/api/states` reads. The ordinary
  State catalog test checks every published State against its
  player-facing detail template, and checks Isolated, Fireteam,
  Coordinated Orders, recovery and State cancellation examples.
  Null is a *classification* of five current States, not
  one more in-game State or an extra profile statistic.
  Remaining conditional transition, declaration, timing and
  remaining visibility interactions are owned by V1S-032–037 and
  V1S-068–069.
- **33 canonical `trait:*` records** agree with the separately
  reviewed Wiki heading inventory, including exact parenthesized
  punctuation/anchors. The current `/traits` catalog, API,
  player-facing details, selected Weapon/Skill/Equipment links and
  the mapped source headings have independent validation.
  Army Weapon `properties` are *not* identical to Traits:
  `Comms. Attack` and `No LoF` are Labels, former
  Technical/Throwing Weapon names have reviewed current
  counterparts, and CC Attack modifier text is not another
  Trait identity. The `State` Weapon Trait describes a
  conditional *effect* that may cause an actual `state:*`;
  it is not interchangeable with an in-game State or the
  state of a Unit/Profile. Cybermine's source spelling
  anomaly resolves to two separately linked actual
  States without replacing the raw source text.
  Parameterized `Disposable (X)`, `Silent (X)`,
  `State: Stunned` and source-specific combined Traits
  still need per-Weapon application checks (V1S-028,
  V1S-034/036–038/070–072).
- **13 `attribute:*` definitions** (MOV, CC, BS, PH, WIP,
  ARM, BTS, VITA, STR, AVA, S, SWC and C) are
  source-cited canonical *embedded* Glossary records.
  These are read through `/glossary`, Unit/Profile and
  owning Skill/Equipment/Rule/Weapon contexts; not
  one standalone detail URL per Attribute. Characteristic
  values in Army data, Saving Roll defense attributes,
  Silhouette template geometry and transient in-game
  States have distinct source and runtime semantics.
  A negative/zero modifier or missing nonstandard
  deployed-object value is not automatically an
  in-game State or a missing Attribute definition.
  Remaining profile semantics, value provenance
  and rollout checks belong to the downstream
  V1S-028/029/033/037 and V1S-070–078.

**Reviewed State families and conditional boundaries:**
Camouflaged, Decoy, Hidden Deployment,
Holoecho/HoloMask and Impersonation retain different
Marker/Model, ARO, revelation and cancellation
conditions; selected Surprise Attack relations must
not be interpreted as universal permission. IMM-A,
IMM-B, Isolated and Targeted use different Dodge/Reset,
Roll MOD, Fireteam and Hacking/Peripheral conditions.
Unconscious, Dead, Disconnected, Possessed and
Sepsitorized differ in Null classification,
recovery, Controller roles and casualty accounting.
Prone, Foxhole, Suppressive Fire and Engaged have
distinct declared/automatic cancellation and
Movement/LoF cases. A reviewed typed graph edge
does not calculate the resulting State or waive
source timing and target constraints.

**Retreat! correction:** the maintained
`rule:army-retreat` is an *army-level situation*,
whereas `state:retreat` is an *individual
Trooper State*. Both are source-cited N5.3 and
already linked correctly in their published facts.
One State-review future edge still targeted a
noncanonical `rule:retreat-situation` placeholder.
This is now `rule:army-retreat`; the two
related Shasvastii/Inspiring Leadership future
leads were aligned to the same canonical
reference. All three reasons now explicitly
preserve phase/target/army-level and
scenario-scoring conditions rather than
promoting an unconditional `causes-state`
or other broadly implied gameplay edge.
Their planned statuses remain; historical
review timestamps and actual public rule facts
were not changed. A regression test pins the
three canonical future targets and requires
the State's current graph *not* to have
an unconditional outgoing Army Retreat edge.

**Remaining source uncertainties:** the
maintained-text audit finds **zero unlinked candidates,
zero reviewed-batch residuals**, and one
explicit `review-needed:scope-unclear`
on `trait:silent-x`: current source material
does not unambiguously resolve the interaction
of the conditional Dodge MOD with Immunity
during opposing-Roll resolution. No inference
or global override is published; V1S-028/036
own the source dispute and semantic acceptance.
The N5.3 PDF-versus-Wiki Dodge (ARM+3),
Weapon-chart `State: Stunned`, and conflicting
Army/PDF Mine/Dazer silhouettes remain
versioned source differences, not new State
or Attribute identities.

**Disposition:** this slice establishes the
canonical family/normal-read boundaries
without asserting full gameplay or browser
acceptance. The 1.0 interaction identity
guard still checks **186/186** primary and
**205/205** supporting review identities;
the existing **518 authored edges** and
**111 future leads** are unchanged.
The three Retreat metadata references,
review checklist and regression test are
the only implementation-adjacent edits;
no rule data, generated databases or
frontend behavior was changed.

## Cross-source conflicts, FAQ applicability and public uncertainty (V1S-028; 2026-11-04)

**Purpose:** reconcile the canonical
[official clarification register](official-source-clarifications.md)
against the pinned 1.0 source pair, existing published
source notes, FAQ selection, and the actual normal
read surfaces. A discrepancy between publications
is **not** automatically an InfinityDB data error,
an effective erratum, or permission to combine
two independently scoped answers. This is bounded
source-selection/disclosure review, not resolution
of the publisher's unanswered questions.
All **SRC-001–030** remain recorded under
their existing IDs and ownership; no publisher
response or new source supersession has been
established in this slice.

**Pinned source re-verification:** the read-only
`tools.audit_1_0_reference_inputs` with both
archived language-history ZIPs, both dated
Wiki snapshots, the English N5.3 PDF and
English FAQ v0.1 exited 0 and confirmed
**116/116** pinned historical Wiki revision
payloads, **90** English and **two** Spanish
snapshot heading/citation checks, and exact
declared SHA-256 matches for Core N5.3
(`53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`)
and FAQ v0.1 English
(`7bf26b7d039db7826ef5500b1a2159fe54855b4e632133cab416becee65b85aa`).
The same evidence report matched the **two
historical N4 Reinforcements PDFs**;
their bytes do *not* establish an N5 Annex
edition. These are version and citation
identity checks, not a newly audited
translation or a complete Weapon corpus.

**Disposition by source and player context:**

- **Shared Core rulings and bilingual examples
  (SRC-002/003/005–008):** Silent (X)
  versus Immunity and direct Doctor/Engineer
  target allegiance remain genuine
  *conditional-rule questions*, not inferred
  universal behavior. The `trait:silent-x`
  `review-needed:scope-unclear` marker and
  selected player-facing notes are preserved;
  V1S-036/037 own exact semantics. Dodge
  (ARM+3) publishes the English/Spanish N5.3
  PDF interpretation together with the
  failed-Dodge Wiki disagreement; Special Dice
  publishes the contradictory Spanish Fireteam
  six-dice reminder; Fireteam level examples
  disclose the Morat Level 3/4 conflict.
  These are visible on `/skills/dodge`,
  `/rules/special-dice` and `/fireteams`
  with actual citations, not merely stored
  in the clarification register.
- **Army identity and equipment/weapon
  disagreements (SRC-001/009–021,
  SRC-024/027–029):** source-specific
  Charontid `SR-1`, unresolvable Fireteam
  chart references, Armed Turret E/Mitter
  rolls/Silhouette, Mine/Dazer silhouettes,
  PARA/Sepsitor footnotes, WildParrot
  Non-Lethal, Pheroware legacy naming,

## Frozen explanation audit to reviewed current evidence (V1S-029; 2026-11-04)

**Purpose:** close the identity-and-evidence *join* between the
[immutable 1.0 explanation inventory](rules-explanation-audit-1.0-inventory.md)
and the maintained Core N5.3/Hacking current record set. This does **not**
approve all player-relevant source clauses, every interaction, or a final
browser/release candidate. Historic REA remediation notes, a curated
`reviewed` flag, and the separate 1.0 interaction identity gate are not
whole-record acceptance. The original inventory remains byte-for-byte
unchanged at SHA-256
`3f6b21bc6ba7e508e63cfaa74b1bdb3fdefe3ab3968418d813be18a36275675e`.

**Complete historical lineage:** the new optional
`--join-reviewed-evidence` mode in `tools.report_reference_baseline`
produces a deterministic JSON bridge with one explicit historical
record row for **every one of the 367 frozen identities** and one
current record row for **all 450 maintained Core/Hacking identities**:

- Original tiers remain **304 D / 63 S**, with their precise
  source-audit meaning; neither is a final approval. **350** old
  IDs are retained, **100** current IDs were added since the
  frozen inventory, and **17** retired IDs are all former
  page-specific declaration-category metadata. Exactly **46**
  retained records still carry their historical S tier.
- The **17** retired identities point to the maintained
  [declaration owner migration review](../config/validation/declaration-inventory-migration-v1.json),
  which independently verifies **20** current category headings,
  **three** classification corrections and **one** owner split.
  These mappings are *historic owner/source-heading
  transitions*, not public aliases, page-number-based inferred
  renames, or permission to rewrite the frozen rows.
  The validator refuses missing, duplicated and changed owner
  mappings and checks the pinned 2026-10-09 historical
  Git commit plus N5.3 PDF headings.
- Each current identity retains its lineage, original tier when
  applicable, curated-review status/date, count of source locators,
  source-vs-browser acceptance dispositions, and *specific*
  later selected evidence. An original row also carries the exact
  current ID(s) and kinds of downstream evidence reachable through
  that owner link. Sources and tests are never converted into
  an additional approval tier.
- **Later reviewed evidence joined:** **10** source-scoped
  conditional declaration/ARO cases backed by pinned source clauses
  and test anchors; **five** separately verified English/Spanish
  Visibility Zone terms; **20** current declaration owner/heading
  reviews; and **36** maintained selected-browser *test anchors*
  (not executions performed by the join). These overlap:
  **56 distinct current identities** and **26 of the original
  367 historical identities** have at least one later selected

  56 or 26 fully accepted records**.
- All **743** distinct Core/Hacking source-section locators retain

## Conditional declaration and reactive eligibility follow-up (V1S-030; 2026-11-04)

**Reviewed boundary:** after the ten selected N5.3
declaration/ARO cases in
[`conditional-declaration-evidence-v1.json`](../config/validation/conditional-declaration-evidence-v1.json),
this pass audited **12 additional conditional source owners**
in the independently pinned English N5.3 Core PDF
(SHA-256 `53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`),
the maintained curated Core collection, the tracked rules
database and normal public API/detail routes.
The new [V1S-030 evidence manifest](../config/validation/conditional-reactive-followup-v1.json)
preserves the selected original source page, exact
authored fact fragment, publication route and
**depth of source check** for each record.
It supplements the older ten-case review; it does
not change its dated source declarations or acceptance.

- **Movement and timing:** Cautious Movement (p. 32)
  suppresses AROs only with the specified *both-endpoint*
  LoF/ZoC/Hacking Area conditions, with Stealth,
  Hackability and Hidden Deployment exceptions.
  Climbing Plus (p. 88) and Super-Jump (p. 113)
  permit their first-declared Climb/Jump to be treated
  as a Basic Short Skill; both remain Automatic Skills,
  while the underlying Climb/Jump retain their
  Long Skill identities. Trajectory must be
  declared before reactions. These are source-scoped
  permissions, **not a universal reclassification**
  of movement or second-half declaration eligibility.
- **Reactive attack modes:** Neurocinetics (p. 104)
  has compulsory Active B1 versus reactive full
  Burst against one target; Total Reaction (p. 116)
  optionally permits full weapon Burst for a
  BS Attack ARO against one activated Trooper.
  Suppressive Fire State (p. 171) permits its
  source-defined SF Mode reactive B3, but most
  alternative declarations, an Order and the
  other printed cancellation cases end the State.
  These differences do not authorize a generic
  full-Burst ARO or a session-time evaluator.
- **Marker-delayed reactions:** Camouflaged State
  (p. 157) permits Discover/Dodge/Look Out!/Reset
  against the unrevealed Marker and a delayed ARO;
  an unrevealing second declaration loses that
  delayed chance. IMP-1 (p. 166) likewise constrains
  enemy declaration against a disguised Marker,
  but retains its *apparent Allied* status and
  IMP-1/IMP-2 Discover progression. Delayed
  reaction is not an extra second ARO, nor does it
  allow retroactively attacking an unrevealed Marker.
- **Multi-Trooper actor ownership:** G: Jumper
  (p. 93) permits one eligible Proxy to react
  normally while other reacting Proxies are
  Dodge/Reset-limited; generic Peripherals
  (p. 106) have separate ARO entitlements but
  normally share the same Skill and Controller
  rules. Coordinated Orders (p. 129) limit
  each reactive Trooper to one activated target
  and share active declarations/target rules.
  Fireteam General (p. 135) has independently
  eligible members, shared ARO Skill selection
  and distinct dissent/Leader departure consequences.
  The owner is a particular Trooper, Proxy or
  Fireteam member, not a universal Army-state
  ARO permission. Participant-specific exceptions
  remain separate.

**Evidence depth matters:** six selected cases have
a printed N5.3 *clause phrase* and six retain
a pinned printed *section-heading/location*
plus their independently maintained curated
conditional text. Heading matches are **not**
independent verification of every authored
exception, nor does a matching page imply
all adjacent facts are complete. The new focused
regression verifies all 12 selections against
the exact source hash, authored `facts`,
published `rules.db` facts/citations,
normal API/detail routes and fixed review
ownership; it deliberately fails on missing/
duplicate owners, mismatched publication text,
altered source boundaries or upgraded
acceptance claims. The existing
`/api/orders-aros` response still explicitly
sets `legalityEvaluation=false`; the new tests
guard against treating an Automatic Skill,
State or shared Rule as newly declarable because
it modifies another action.

**Disposition:** no independently verified missing
conditional-rule fact, broken selected public
route or unsafe category promotion arose from
this bounded follow-up. No source curation,
runtime eligibility code or database bytes
were changed. V1S-030's selected outstanding
family *review* is completed; this is
**not final 1.0 conditional source-clause,
cross-effect or normal-browser acceptance**.
The still-pending 743 source locators are not
743 confirmed gameplay defects. V1S-032–039
retain material declaration, Orders/AROs,
Marker/geometry, opponent, Device, Weapon,
State and effect-precedence questions;
V1S-082/086 and the final candidate-wide
browser gate own complete source/route sign-off.
Selected route availability is not responsive
Chromium acceptance.

**Focused validation:** `tests/test_conditional_reactive_followup.py`
**4 passed**, `tests/test_curated.py` **670 passed**,
and `tests/test_hacking_program_publication.py`
**10 passed** (**684 passed total**).
The earlier 10-case source audit also exited 0
against the pinned English N5.3 PDF.

## Whole-term Visibility Zone source/browser acceptance (V1S-031; 2026-11-04)

**Scope and verdict:** **five of five** N5.3 Visibility Zone
Game-term records are now independently accepted
for their **complete relevant source definitions and
normal Glossary/browser presentation** on the current
published Army/rules database pair. This is
*term-specific* whole-record approval, **not**
acceptance of the 450-record Core/Hacking corpus,
all 743 source locators, White Noise Program effects,
Smoke/Eclipse template placement or a tactical
visibility/modifier engine.

The independent maintained
[V1S-031 acceptance manifest](../config/validation/visibility-zone-whole-record-v1.json)
has one accepted decision per canonical term:
`term:visibility-zone`, `term:low-visibility-zone`,
`term:poor-visibility-zone`,
`term:zero-visibility-zone` and
`term:white-noise-zone`. It retains the
frozen **367-record** baseline denominator,
the current **450-record** Core/Hacking denominator,
the five scoped `/glossary#term-*` destinations,
the exact source pages/bytes, checked responsive
widths, and **zero** other source-section
approvals. It supplements—not alters—the earlier
selected-sentence evidence manifest
[`visibility-zone-terms-v1.json`](../config/validation/visibility-zone-terms-v1.json).

**Complete source-section review:** the
English N5.3 p. **144** PDF (SHA-256
`53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`)
and Spanish N5.3 p. **148** PDF (SHA-256
`7f564c864c6f26d6b218a0477c8afeba99da5c56e1a5369bb259f423689bb28f`)
both contain the entire applicable
*Visibility Condition* subsection on a single
printed page. I inspected both complete sections,
then added independent exact-source tests for
all operative paragraphs (not merely the
previous five selected sentence excerpts):

- **Shared Visibility Zone:** LoF-dependent
  Skills, Special Skills and Equipment in/from/
  through a Zone; Dodge excluded; Visibility
  MODs stack with Range, Partial Cover and
  other eligible non-Visibility MODs, but
  multiple Visibility Zones contribute
  **only the most restrictive one**.
- **Low/Poor:** respectively **-3** and
  **-6** to the applicable Roll, with the
  shared scope and non-stacking rule.
- **Zero:** blocks LoF into/through/out of
  the Zone; an affected **BS Attack target**
  instead treats it as Poor Visibility
  when drawing LoF **to the attacker**.
  This exception is not generic permission
  to shoot through the Zone.
- **White Noise:** acts as Zero Visibility
  **only** for Troopers with Marksmanship,
  a Multispectral Visor (any Level), or
  other expressly specified Equipment.
  When an MSV user is attacked through
  the Zone, their Visor cannot reduce
  the resulting Poor Visibility MOD.

Both source languages corroborate these
conditions. The adjacent Difficult Terrain
and Saturation paragraphs are separate
scenery properties and **not** additional
Visibility Zone effects. All five source
entries remain N5.3 current, source-cited,
and use `facts.scope=visibility-condition`
in curated and published data; their
authored summaries retain the operative
conditions. Source-dependent
Smoke/Eclipse/Reflective, MSV/Sixth Sense
and Hacking Program interactions remain
with their own source owners and final
release acceptance tasks, rather than
becoming extra rules on these five terms.

**Normal publication:** the accepted
destination is the **embedded Glossary
entry**, not a fictitious standalone
`/rules/*` route. `tests/test_visibility_zone_whole_record.py`
validates matched curated/`rules.db` facts,
exact EN/ES PDF paragraphs, canonical
identities/citations, all five live
`/api/glossary` entries and their
`/glossary#term-*` URLs, plus fail-closed
manifest identity/scope/source drift.
The existing search and contextual tooltip
tests verify ordinary discovery and
preview navigation. The maintained-text
reference audit reports **zero unlinked
candidates and zero reviewed-batch residuals**;
its one explicit review-needed marker is the
separate Silent (X)/Immunity uncertainty. The current **real
Chromium** `test_visibility_zone_terms_normal_browser`
rechecked all five anchors at **1440, 390
and 320 px**, including term content,
hash navigation, accessible rendered
entries, no JavaScript page errors
and no horizontal overflow. Prior
touch/keyboard/sticky-offset checks
remain recorded in
[testing](testing.md#10-five-visibility-zone-game-terms-browser-acceptance).

**Gate boundary:** V1S-031 is retired for
these **five exact records only**. The
generic `report_reference_baseline`
identity report still says all **743**
source locators need whole-corpus dispositions,
since it does not independently import
per-term acceptance decisions; do **not**
subtract five from the source-locator count
or interpret that aggregate as evidence
that these five remain unreviewed.
The separate manifest supplies their
independent current candidate decisions
for V1S-082/086; broader 1.0 source
and release-candidate browser signoff
remains pending.

## Command Token resource and phase coverage gap (V1S-032; 2026-11-04)

**Discovery finding (subsequently remediated in V1S-032A below).**
The pinned English N5.3 Core PDF (SHA-256
`53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`),
**Command Module pp. 128–129**, gives each player
**four Command Tokens for the match** and
defines *Strategic*, *Executive* and
*Operational* use of that same resource.
The operative mode procedures and the
explicit prohibition on spending Command
Tokens in the **Reactive Turn** were read
directly from both printed pages, not
inferred from a named Skill or Wiki snippet.

**Existing coverage:** the canonical
`rule:command-token-strategic-use` is already
published with N5.3 p.128 and pinned Wiki
citations, its Procedural/Logistical split
and maximum of one selection of each.
`rule:coordinated-orders`, `rule:guts-roll`,
`rule:combat-group`, `rule:army-retreat`,
`state:possessed`, `skill:doctor`,
`skill:engineer`, `skill:strategos-l2`
and related records cover **selected**
Command Token effects in their proper
participant/source contexts. The
N5.3 Orders, Combat Group and activation
records also remain published independently.
These selected references are **not**
a complete general Command Token manual.

**Missing baseline at discovery (V1S-032A, now remediated):**
there was no maintained `rule:command-tokens`
general resource identity or distinct
Executive/Operational use-mode reference.
Neither the then-current Strategic Use page nor
the scattered individual effects
collects all the following source-defined
choices and constraints into a normal
player-facing Command Token reference:

- **Strategic:** first-player extra reserve
  Trooper + Peripherals during Deployment;
  second-player one of the first-Turn
  procedural actions (removing two Regular
  Orders only when the printed >10-order
  prerequisite is satisfied, first-Turn
  Command Token use cap, or an eligible
  Suppressive Fire State activation);
  both players' separate Speedball
  Logistical option; maximum one
  Procedural and one Logistical use.
- **Executive:** the specified Tactical
  Phase step permits one permanent
  Trooper+Peripherals Combat Group
  transfer respecting the ten-member
  limit, **or** cancellation of a
  friendly Possessed State per token.
- **Operational:** during the Active
  Turn, one token can fund a
  `rule:coordinated-orders` declaration
  alongside the group's Regular Order,
  force an Allied Trooper's Guts pass
  or failure, convert an eligible
  Irregular Order into a Regular
  Order for that Turn, grant one
  Trooper lasting exemption from the
  army's Retreat! consequences, reroll
  a qualifying failed Doctor/Cube
  or Engineer/Remote Presence WIP
  Roll without another ARO, or create
  an eligible Fireteam. Multiple
  Command Tokens may be spent in one
  Active Turn and for repeat
  qualifying effects while available.
  **No Command Token may be spent
  during the Reactive Turn.**

These are **match/player resource actions**,
not persistent per-Unit Attributes,
automatic permissions for any target,
or one additional Order/Token type.
The existing Strategic source identity
should remain stable; a new common
reference can link it and the existing
effect owners without duplicating or
changing their source-defined effects.

**Implementation and final review split (recorded at discovery):**
V1S-032A owned a new maintained General Rules
definition, source citations, matched
`rules.db` + deployed-object publication,
the strict 1.0 interaction review,
normal catalog/detail/context links and
responsive browser tests; these have now
been implemented and tested below.
**V1S-032B** separately owns the
remaining Tactical/Impetuous/Orders/
States Phase and Order-activation
exceptions, after checking the
existing `rule:order-expenditure-sequence`
and `rule:coordinated-orders`
clauses so they are not duplicated.
V1S-032 **remains open** and cannot
be counted as an accepted release
gate. V1S-034/035/039 and final
V1S-082/086 retain their other
attacks, Hacking, scenarios and
source-wide coverage obligations.

**Reproducible source evidence:**
`tests/test_command_token_source_review.py`
pins the exact N5.3 PDF bytes, both
Command Module pages, the four-token
pool, all three mode headings and
selected operative conditions,
including first-/Reactive-Turn,
rerolls and Fireteams. It also
checks the maintained and published
Strategic-use record against the
source. **2 focused tests passed**
before any curated or release-data change.
That original two-test checkpoint proved
the omission's source scope; the published
V1S-032A evidence below proves its remediation.

## Command Token reference publication (V1S-032A; 2026-11-04)

**Implemented:** the canonical N5.3 `rule:command-tokens` now
appears in the normal General Rules catalog at
`/rules/command-tokens`, through search, its JSON API,
and contextual rule links. It presents the **four-Command-Token
match pool**, all three conditional use modes and their
source-defined boundaries: first-player Deployment reserve,
second-player first-Turn choices and >10-order threshold,
one Procedural plus one Logistical Strategic choice,
two Speedball Tokens, Tactical Phase Executive transfer
or Possessed cancellation, and all seven Operational
options (Coordinated Order, Guts, Irregular-to-Regular
conversion, Retreat! exemption, Doctor/Cube reroll,
Engineer/Remote Presence reroll, and Fireteam creation).
It preserves the **no Reactive-Turn expenditure** restriction
and eligible Active-Turn repeat spending, without creating
a legality evaluator, unconditional Unit resource,
or a new Order type. The prior standalone
`rule:command-token-strategic-use` is unchanged and linked.

**Source/version:** the maintained definition has exact
English N5.3 **pp. 128–129** citations. Its source
obligations are pinned to the ignored official PDF by
SHA-256 `53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`;
the same definitions and pages are asserted in
`tests/test_command_token_source_review.py`.
A dedicated strict-1.0 supporting interaction review
marks the *non-executable* canonical identity reviewed,
without adding unqualified typed links; the existing
**518** outgoing relation count is unchanged.

**Paired publication:** the completed one-use Command Tokens
promotion produced two identical deterministic schema-v9 candidates.
It verified all **483** previously published canonical
record identities retain identical composed content,
preserved typed Restrictions and every unrelated
deployed-object field, and promoted a matched
`data/generated/rules.db` and
`data/generated/deployed-objects.json` pair with
rollback under ignored `local/`. The one-use promotion script is retired;
Git history preserves its implementation.
The new rules SHA-256 is
`e3d367cf881833d9a4820c1d659467e51556a249146c63d7d933769e3240e6aa`.
The final rules export contains **484** records.
The current Core/Hacking evidence join now has
**451** identities (**101** added since the frozen
367 baseline), and **745** source locator entries;
these represent source acceptance work, not
745 confirmed gameplay defects. Historical audit
denominators, tier classifications and previously
retired/dated source decisions are unchanged.

passed **3/3**; normal Chrome catalog-to-detail-to-

checks passed **3/3** at **1440/390/320 px**. Reconciled

tests **166/166**, curated rules **670/670**,
interaction audit **15/15**, deployed-object publication
**12/12**, and maintained-text **38/38**
after adjusting the *current* denominators
(**956 focused tests in total**). The strict interaction
review, deterministic rules build, paired publication
verification and maintained-link audit also passed.
Final broader CI/release evidence is queued separately;
**V1S-032A is retired**. Parent **V1S-032** and
**V1S-032B** remain open for the rest of the
Orders/phases/activation source and browser review.

## Turn sequence, Order Pool and ARO clause dispositions (V1S-032B; 2026-11-04)

**Reviewed boundary:** The original V1S-032B task named N5.3 Core
**pp. 10–15 and 128–131**, but the actual *Game Sequence, Tactical
Phase, Impetuous Phase, Orders Phase and States Phase* are printed on
**pp. 16–17**, not those earlier pages. The source review therefore
covered **pp. 10–17, 18 and 128–131** of the pinned English N5.3 Core
PDF (SHA-256
`53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`),
plus the relevant Spanish N5.3 p. 17
(SHA-256
`7f564c864c6f26d6b218a0477c8afeba99da5c56e1a5369bb259f423689bb28f`).
This **review/classification**, with explicit new owners below,
does **not** declare all pages or 1.0 source sections fully accepted.

**Already published with appropriate scope:**

- **Combat Group, Order Types** (pp. 10–11):
  `rule:combat-group`, `rule:regular-order`,
  `rule:irregular-order`, `rule:special-lieutenant-order`
  and `rule:tactical-order` separate Combat Group ownership,
  Regular Pool versus private/special Orders, and member capacity;
  `skill:lieutenant`, `skill:tactical-awareness` and
  `skill:strategos` own their own exceptional generation.
- **Order declaration, simultaneous actions and Resolution**
  (pp. 13–15): `rule:order-expenditure-sequence` already
  publishes the first/second ARO checks, immediate versus
  Resolution-phase requirements, compulsory Idle when invalid,
  Disposable expenditure, target/route/mode choices,
  positive-before-negative simultaneous effects, Guts and
  Alert! conclusion. The existing short/long Skill categories
  and `/orders-aros` matrix correctly **do not**
  simulate declaration legality.
- **Initiative/Deployment and Impetuous** (pp. 15–17,
  Impetuous source p. 97): `rule:initiative-roll`,
  `rule:deployment-phase` and `skill:impetuous` describe
  general setup/activation/reserves. `rule:army-retreat`
  and `rule:loss-of-lieutenant` separately own the
  beginning-of-Turn checks and army-level consequences.
- **Command and Coordinated Orders** (pp. 128–131):
  completed `rule:command-tokens` now presents all three
  use modes and the Reactive-Turn prohibition. Existing
  `rule:coordinated-orders` owns Spearhead, identical Skill
  declaration, targets, Burst and recipient-specific
  restrictions, with distinct FAQ Special Dice provenance;
  nothing is reinterpreted as a universal four-Trooper
  activation for ordinary Orders.

**Confirmed player-facing omissions, not mere missing citations:**

- **V1S-032C1 — Game Sequence and Order Pool:**
  Source pp. **12, 16–17** define full Player Turn order
  (**Tactical: Executive Command Token Use → Retreat! →
  Loss of Lieutenant → Order Count**, then **Impetuous →
  Orders → States → End**), Regular/Irregular/Lieutenant/
  Tactical counts, an eligible deployed non-Null
  Model/Marker prerequisite, private off-table Orders,
  flexible/repeated active Order spending, unused Orders
  lost after the Turn, and **both players'** States
  checks with **no AROs from those checks**.
  The existing references contain *selected* parts of
  this procedure but there is **no canonical Game Sequence
  or Order Pool general reference presenting it all**.
  These are game-session timing/resource procedures,
  not static Unit properties. Do not infer a new
  Order/ARO legality evaluator.
- **V1S-032C2 — Core ARO and Deployment conditions:**
  Source p. **13** additionally establishes the ordinary
  four ARO eligibility families, the **activated Trooper**
  target restriction, no ARO-to-ARO, one ARO per eligible
  Reactive Trooper per Order, only source-authorized ARO
  Skills, default **B1** and no target splitting when
  an exception grants greater Burst. While source-backed
  individual interactions are maintained, the short
  `trait:aro` definition and published Order sequence
  do not collectively provide a discoverable complete
  baseline for these clauses. Source p. **16**
  Deployment Rules add the complete-base-inside-Zone,
  prohibited contact with enemy/neutral objects and
  objectives, optional initial Prone, base-size/available
  space and named pregame measurements. The existing
  `rule:deployment-phase` covers the reserve *sequence*
  from p. 15, **not** all p. 16 placement constraints.
  Implement these in their owning General Rules and
  verify source/browser presentation, without changing
  the old 57-row declaration chart or expanding
  `trait:aro` into a legality engine.

**New bilingual source inconsistency — SRC-031:**
The English N5.3 p. **17** Lieutenant Order Count
says `not in a non-Null State` for a deployed
Lieutenant, while **Spanish N5.3 p. 17** says
`Teniente en estado no Nulo` (a *non-Null* state).
The latter agrees with the neighboring Regular/Tactical
Order conditions and the English Loss of Lieutenant p. 18.
The inverted English wording is a likely **copy error**,
not a supported exception permitting Null Lieutenants
to generate a Special Order. The discrepancy is
tracked in
[`SRC-031`](official-source-clarifications.md#src-031--lieutenant-order-count-english-double-negative-versus-spanish-non-null)
as a **candidate correction**, not an official ruling.
The future published Game Sequence must disclose
the two versioned wordings while presenting
the corroborated deployed, non-Null eligibility;
no publisher answer is required to expose the
uncertainty accurately.

**Source regression:** `tests/test_phase_source_review.py`
verifies direct N5.3 English page clauses across
pp. 11–17 and 128–130, exact bilingual p. 17
discrepancy and the SRC-031 register, plus
curated/`rules.db` parity for **eight current
source-backed owners**. **3 passed**; Ruff
passed. The test is deliberately a **source and
ownership boundary**, not a passed browser signoff
for the still-missing Game Sequence, Order Pool or
general ARO reference.

**Disposition:** V1S-032B's source-family
*review* is complete, but V1S-032 remains open
for **V1S-032C1/C2**, final implementation and
normal-browser checks. Other movement/LoF geometry
is owned by V1S-033; additional effects and
exception rules remain V1S-034–039 and
release-wide V1S-082/086. Do not retire the parent,
inflate the historical 367-record sample, or
subtract source locators merely because their
owning records have been identified.

## Game Sequence and Order Pool publication (V1S-032C1; 2026-11-04)

**Implemented and reviewed:** Two canonical General Rules,
`rule:game-sequence` and `rule:order-pool`, now
appear at `/rules/game-sequence` and `/rules/order-pool`,
in the normal rules catalog, public API and search.
Both carry current N5 source scope, linked identity
references and separately reviewed 1.0 interaction entries.
No unconditional typed relation was added.

**Source-native Player Turn procedure:** N5.3 English
pp. **16–17**, not the earlier page-10–15 source subset,
specify a Game Round with two Player Turns in
Initiative-determined order. The new Game Sequence
presents the five phases **in order**: Tactical,
Impetuous, Orders, States, End of Turn. Tactical
executes Executive Command Token use, Retreat!
check, Loss of Lieutenant check and Order Count,
in that order. Impetuous eligibility remains
owned by `skill:impetuous`, and normal activation/
reaction by `rule:order-expenditure-sequence`.
The States Phase requires checks by **both players**,
does **not generate AROs** from its Rolls,
and clears remaining Order Tokens.

**Source-native Order Pool procedure:** N5.3
English pp. **11–12, 16–17** and Spanish p. **17**
establish one distinct Regular Order Pool per
Combat Group, recalculated at Tactical Order
Count; contributions require on-table eligible
non-Null Troopers. Irregular, Special Lieutenant
and Tactical Orders are **not pooled** Regular
Orders, and their own use constraints remain.
Off-table Troopers generally do not contribute
yet, and their Orders can remain Private Information.
Visible contributed Orders and expenditure are
Open Information. The Orders Phase permits
repeated activation of the same Trooper and
alternating Troopers within the group, without
requiring all Orders be spent; unused Orders
**expire**, not carry over into the next Turn.

**Source conflict preserved:** The p.17 English
double-negative Lieutenant Order condition
(`not in a non-Null State`) differs from
Spanish (`Teniente en estado no Nulo`).
The public Order Pool reference contains both
source-native phrasings and explicitly discloses
**SRC-031**; the supported non-Null reading
is not represented as an official correction.
No runtime Pool-count or Unit/list-legality
engine was introduced, and the existing
Command Token, Loss of Lieutenant, Orders,
Combat Group, Impetuous and Coordinated Order
identities were left intact.

**Guarded publication:** The
`tools.promote_turn_sequence_reference`
script pinned the official English and Spanish
N5.3 PDF hashes, double-built identical
schema-v9 rules candidates, checked every
one of the **484 prior composed records**
unchanged, and verified the existing typed
Restrictions and all unrelated deployed-object
fields remained equal. It atomically promoted
the tracked `rules.db` and Git-ignored matched
`deployed-objects.json` with recovery under
ignored `local/`. The resulting rules SHA-256
is `871a76f8fb69c4d4f9ba9120494635713cdc4970a79b448c191d83cff08a42ea`.
There are now **486 total rules records**.
The current Core/Hacking explanation inventory
contains **453 identities** (103 later additions
to the frozen 367, 350 retained, 17 retired),
and **752 source locators pending
whole-corpus clause/browser review**.
Those are *pending audit units*, not missing
gameplay rules; frozen historical evidence,
original tiers and migration decisions remain unchanged.

**Focused checks:** the new
`tests/test_turn_sequence_publication.py`
checks five phases, conditional owner distinctions,
source citations, deployed API, search and
matched deterministic pair (**4 passed**).
`tests/test_verify_turn_sequence_browser.py`
tests the actual tracked publication in Chrome
at **1440, 390 and 320 px** (**3 passed**),
including catalog-to-detail navigation, keyboard
links in both directions, source links,
narrow-width overflow and console exceptions.
The existing V1S-032B bilingual/source
regressions passed **3/3**. Reconciled
baseline **49/49**, rules DB **166/166**,
curated **670/670**, interactions **15/15**,
deployed-object publication **12/12**,
and maintained-text **38/38** also passed:
**960 focused tests in all**.
The data/rules build, strict interaction
audit, matched-pair check, full Ruff,
Pyright and docs links/lint passed.
C1 is retired for its documented
scope; V1S-032 and **V1S-032C2** remain open
for baseline ARO and Deployment Rule
publication. Release-wide approval,
Linux/full-suite validation and exact-SHA
hosted CI remain independent gates.

## General ARO and Deployment publication (V1S-032C2; 2026-11-04)

**Source and scope.** Pinned English N5.3 Core SHA-256
`53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`
provides the source-native ARO clauses on printed pp. **13–14**
and the Deployment Phase on pp. **15–16**. The new
`rule:automatic-reaction-order` is a canonical, discoverable
non-executable General Rule at `/rules/automatic-reaction-order`.
It distinguishes the four source-eligible ARO trigger families
(LoF; ZoC/Hacking Area; specifically authorized out-of-area
reactions; affected Template/Comms Attacks), one ARO per Order,
activated-Trooper targets, first/second ARO Check forfeiture,
simultaneous resolution, default Burst B1 and no Burst splitting.
Exceptions remain owned by their source Skills, Weapons, Equipment
or scenarios. The previously published `trait:aro` and the
Orders/AROs declaration matrix are not changed into an eligibility
engine; the authored outgoing interaction graph remains **518**.

**Existing rule completed, not replaced.** The
`rule:deployment-phase` reference retains N5.3 p.15's four-step
main-force/reserve placement and Initiative/Deployment choice
distinction. The N5.3 p.16 addition now requires the whole base
inside the allowed Deployment Zone, prohibits starting in contact
with enemy or Neutral Models/Markers/Tokens and mission objectives,
describes whole-base physical fit unless players pre-agree an
exception, allows initial [[state:prone|Prone]] deployment, and
lists allowable Deployment Zone, central-line, Forward Deployment
and scenario Exclusion Zone measurements. The separate FAQ
repositioning ruling and any special deployment/mission
exceptions retain their own scope. Existing Order Expenditure
and Coordinated Orders are unchanged.

**Matched publication.** `tools.promote_aro_deployment_reference`
pins the Core PDF; deterministically exports schema-v9 rules
twice; checks all **485 pre-existing rules other than the
intentionally expanded Deployment Phase** for exact composed-record
equality, including the source-backed C1 publication; and checks
typed Restrictions and the unrelated deployed-object projection.
Both `rules.db` and the ignored `deployed-objects.json`
were promoted as one rollback-protected pair (ignored
`local/aro-deployment-v9-recovery-*/`).
The exact tracked rules SHA-256 is
`1334b79375af5d4cf10ecb108225dc9df9bcd06f74454152b31d2ad3a4aba790`;
**487** rules records result. The reconciliation now contains
**454 current Core/Hacking identities** (350 retained of the
frozen 367, 17 retired, 104 added), and **755** still-pending
source locators. These are audit inventory units, **not**
755 gameplay defects or automatically approved clauses.

**Immediate acceptance:** the new
`tests/test_aro_deployment_publication.py` verifies the
pinned PDF passages, distinct ARO and Deployment clauses,
ordinary public APIs/search and deterministic pair.
`tests/test_verify_aro_deployment_browser.py`
exercises real Chrome at **1440, 390 and 320 px**,
including catalog navigation, keyboard navigation
to Order Expenditure, source citations and narrow-width
layout, with no console errors. C2 API/source **5/5**,
Chrome **3/3**, baseline **49/49**, rules DB
**166/166**, curated **670/670**, interaction
**15/15**, deployed publication **12/12**,
maintained links **38/38**, plus C1 model **4/4**,

record threshold instead of asserting that

## Cross-host rules rebuild comparison (V1S-032 validation; 2026-11-04)

**Confirmed:** at exact tracked revision `cd33920d`, the source-backed
schema-v9 rules database has the same entire SQLite schema, stored
table-row multisets (including optimizer statistics), application ID,
schema version, UTF-8 encoding and page size when rebuilt on Windows
and the private Linux worker. Neither source prose nor rule semantics
drifted. The original shipped, hash-pinned, release-matched
`rules.db` remains authoritative on both platforms.

**Physical byte difference:** Windows Python's SQLite **3.50.4**
rebuild matches tracked SHA-256
`1334b79375af5d4cf10ecb108225dc9df9bcd06f74454152b31d2ad3a4aba790`,
while Linux's SQLite **3.46.1** rebuild has SHA-256
`fc8c664656d95b43440f4de312a6e9d75829366c055c0b971f58b5614299427e`.
Both files have **317 × 4096-byte pages**, but **1,579 bytes**
differ, including the SQLite writer-version header. The SQLite
minor-version difference is a likely explanation for the page
serialization differences, not proof of an underlying rule-data defect.
The commands were executed independently with the actual pinned
source and existing curated material. The read-only
`tools.audit_rules_rebuild_equivalence` reports the exact host
digests, differing byte offsets and complete logical-row comparison.

**Guard adjustment:** read-only replay in the guarded
`tools.promote_turn_sequence_reference` and
`tools.promote_aro_deployment_reference` now accepts **strict
schema, metadata and every stored row equality** if the independent
host produces different physical SQLite bytes. Same-host
deterministic double-export remains mandatory, and any changed
logical record, schema, source pin or deployed-object payload still
fails. No cross-host byte parity is claimed, and the two-byte-replay
tests no longer equate a platform-local candidate hash with the
tracked release hash. Production still ships the exact tracked
database and its matching deployed-object JSON fingerprint.

**Outstanding release acceptance:** the separate three-OS hosted
`Source checks` manifest's **byte identity** remains mandatory at
V1S-085; this local logical-equivalence evidence does **not**
substitute for that hosted workflow. If the exact hosted CI
detects SQLite-version physical drift, align the builder SQLite
toolchain or explicitly review/change the reproducibility contract
rather than silently weakening the manifest comparison.
The due Linux queue remains open until its pinned full suite and
hosted CI are checked.**

## Movement, LoF, Cover and terrain source review (V1S-033; 2026-11-04, partial)

**Inputs and scope:** the pinned English N5.3 v5.3 Core Rules PDF
(`53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`),
the existing curated `n5-core-v5.3.json` collection and the current
matched `dev/v1.0.0` publication at `4af5ac14`. This is a source-to-curation
**discovery pass**, not completed whole-record, API/browser or release
acceptance. No new source was acquired and no generated artifact was altered.

- **Moving/measurement and the Move Skill (pp. 27–30): confirmed incomplete
  shared reference.** Page 27 specifies declaration, measurement from the
  same outer-edge/underside base point, route and endpoint determination.
  Page 28 requires at least half-base contact during Move, a sufficiently
  wide traversed surface and a full-base-size landing surface; it distinguishes
  first and second MOV values. Page 29 adds vaulting, LoF while vaulting,
  peeking, allied/neutral pass-through versus occupied endpoints, enemy
  contact, 360-degree movement LoF, Prone transition, no-fall/Idle fallback
  and completion despite later attack-induced States. The published
  `skill:move` currently has **no authored requirements**, a single
  generic movement effect and one generic restriction. Several special
  Skills refer to General Movement/Moving and Measuring, but no separately
  identified `rule:general-movement` or `rule:moving-and-measuring`
  is present in this curated collection. The printed movement procedure
  cannot be considered fully explained by the existing short Skill card.
- **Silhouette, LoF and zones (pp. 18–22): separate baseline procedures
  still need a normal reference owner.** Pages 18–19 define the Silhouette
  volume/height, 180-degree front LoF, 3-by-3-mm visibility threshold and
  specific 360-degree/mutual-LoF/Engaged/Marker exceptions. Page 20 defines
  the ZoC cylinder and inside/totally-inside geometry; page 21 defines
  Coherency timing and distance-measurement rules, and page 22 defines
  exact replacement positioning, including different-base alternatives
  and mandatory replacement when a Model cannot fit. These clauses are not
  equivalent to the already-published `trait:zone-of-control-zc` references
  or Fireteam-specific Coherency context. No dedicated canonical General
  Rule identities for LoF, Coherency, distance measurement or replacing
  game elements were found in the curated collection. Verify existing
  Silhouette help and ordinary browser routes before calling each a
  missing player surface.
- **Cover and vertical movement (pp. 32–34, 41–42): distinguish shared
  conditions from Skill-specific exceptions.** Page 41 makes Total Cover
  a LoF-blocking condition and Partial Cover dependent on scenery contact
  and a partly obscured Silhouette, with separate attack and Saving Roll
  MODs. Climb/Jump impose explicit no-Partial-Cover exceptions and
  vertical landing/route conditions on pp. 32–34. The existing
  `skill:climbing-plus` preserves its own no-Cover restriction and
  vertical contact requirement; that does not constitute a shared Cover
  reference. No `rule:cover` identity is present in the curated collection.
- **Terrain/visibility and scenery (pp. 116, 143–145): distinguish
  maintained table rules from unpublished zone effects.** The published
  `skill:terrain` already carries the matching-type requirement, first-MOV
  bonus and reviewed fixed/choose-one/Total source variants;
  preserve that scoped interpretation. The already-maintained
  `rule:special-terrain` (p. 143) owns the **optional, pre-agreed**
  area configuration and five canonical Types; `rule:access-width`
  (p. 145) already owns Narrow/Wide gates, Silhouette/Prone limits and
  precedence over General Movement. The p. 145 table is explicitly
  *suggestions/examples*, not mandatory terrain assignment; do not
  model the 18 labels as new Types or deployed devices.
  **Remaining p. 144 gap:** the curated rule collection lacks separate
  `rule:difficult-terrain` and `rule:saturation` identities. The first
  mechanic makes contact with the Difficult Terrain area end movement
  for the rest of that Order; continuing in another Order applies -1
  inch to both MOV values or the affected Movement Skill value, subject
  to matching `skill:terrain` exceptions. Saturation applies a -1
  Burst MOD on BS Attacks from/into/through such a zone **after**
  dividing B among targets, never below B1 for a target and never
  stacking across multiple Saturation Zones. These are session-scoped
  rules, not universal movement/weapon modifiers.
  The five Visibility Zone Game terms already have independent
  source/browser acceptance under V1S-031, including the strongest-only
  visibility MOD policy, which should be linked rather than duplicated.

**Disposition at discovery:** V1S-033 was open when this source-to-curation
review was written. The identified movement, geometry, Cover, positioning and
optional Difficult Terrain/Saturation gaps have since been published and
accepted through V1S-033A–E, including the combined cross-host, three-width
browser acceptance. Current explanations are in
[rules-semantics](rules-semantics.md#movement); the completed publication and
acceptance evidence is recorded in the later subsections below. Remaining
State-link decisions, scenery/object classification and release-wide
acceptance stay under V1S-068–069, V1S-074 and V1S-081–090, respectively.
Movement remains descriptive reference, not a pathfinding, ARO or
terrain-legality engine.

### Movement procedure publication (V1S-033A; 2026-11-04)

**Project domains:** Data processing, Web backend, Web frontend

The pinned English N5.3 pp. 27-30 now have shared canonical owners:
`rule:moving-and-measuring` and `rule:general-movement`. The existing
`skill:move` retains its identity, Basic Short Skill type and three labels,
with declaration-time half-base contact, traversed-width and full-base
endpoint requirements. First and second MOV values remain distinct.
The archived Wiki members Move (oldid 3530), Moving_and_Measuring
(oldid 3866) and General_Movement_Rules (oldid 4024), from the already
pinned English ZIP, corroborate these clauses. The latter preserves both
its September 2026 update and original wording; the published reference
uses the N5.3 Skill/ARO posture clause and its Jump/Berserk exceptions.
No new source acquisition or upstream modification occurred.

Shared prose covers declaration/measurement/endpoint order, consistent
base reference, detours, route checks, stairs/ladders, vault height on
both sides and successive levels, peeking, allied/neutral pass-through,
occupied endpoints, enemy contact, 360-degree movement LoF, final facing,
Prone transitions, no-fall/Idle fallback and completion despite later
attack-induced States. Typed links preserve the owning Skill, Attribute,
State and General Rule boundaries. Movement remains descriptive reference;
no pathfinding, unconditional State edge or session-time legality evaluator
was introduced. Source pp. 18-22, Cover/vertical Skill conditions and
optional terrain still belong to V1S-033B-D, with family-wide acceptance
under V1S-033E.

The guarded v9 promotion double-exported deterministically, preserved all

curated-source fingerprints; object contents and Army provenance are unchanged.
Existing recovery tooling retains both pre-promotion artifacts locally.
The new lexical mentions are reviewed as movement through existing game
elements or temporary route representation, not new deployed objects.

Immediate source/conditional checks passed **2/2**, API/search and matched
rebuild checks **2/2**, and real Chromium navigation **3/3** at 1440, 390
and 320 px, including keyboard links, source citations, request/page-error
and overflow assertions. Repeat/mismatch write-refusal passed **1/1**;

### Geometry owner trace (V1S-033B; 2026-11-04, review only)

**Project domains:** Data processing, Web backend, Web frontend

Read-only follow-through at pre-publication revision `fcfa1453`, after
the V1S-033A Linux dispatch, confirmed the next slice's ownership boundaries.
At that revision the collections contained
`attribute:s` and `trait:zone-of-control-zc`, but no dedicated general
LoF, Coherency, distance-measurement or replacement owner. The Glossary
already adds S1-S8 diagrams to the Silhouette Attribute; the reviewed
source table separately retains S0 and SX without SVGs. Preserve these
existing size/geometry owners instead of creating another Silhouette
catalog or moving a Weapon range Trait into the shared-rule role.

N5.3 pp. 18-20 require the shared reference to distinguish default
180-degree LoF and minimum 3-by-3-mm visibility from movement/Marker
360-degree exceptions, obstruction and Engaged conditions. The general
ZoC cylinder and inside/totally-inside/contact meanings are not fully
described by the existing range Trait. Page 21's Reference Trooper and
start/end timing must remain applicable to the owning rule: existing
Fireteam, Peripheral and Holoecho-specific Coherency effects do not
replace the common check or its Deployment reserve-adjustment exception.
Ordinary distance measurement between closest Silhouette points is also
distinct from declaring and measuring a movement route.

Page 22 replacement positioning preserves matching-base placement and
the two different-base choices, permits newly gained contact but cannot
cancel Engaged, and has a separate mandatory/inevitable Obligatory
replacement branch. A no-fit Trooper's special Immobilized-A cancellation
condition and the alternative removal of Equipment must not become a
generic State effect, an Engineer permission or a free-positioning rule.
These are source-confirmed conditions for the existing V1S-033B slice,
not newly published facts or completed browser acceptance. No curation,
runtime, generated artifact or validation contract changed in that review.
The publication below implements these shared owners.

### Cover and vertical-movement boundary review (V1S-033C; 2026-11-04, partial)

**Project domains:** Data processing, Web backend, Web frontend

Read-only review against implementation revision `a9f56465` confirms that
`rule:cover` is still absent. English N5.3 pp. 41-42 distinguish complete
LoF obstruction from scenery contact with a partially obscured Silhouette.
The printed Partial Cover modifiers are -3 to the BS Attack Roll and +3
to the target's Saving Roll when required; do not recast the latter as an
unconditional permanent ARM/BTS change. The pinned archive's `Cover` member
contains the Ballistic Skills page, revision 4094 and its Cover heading;
preserve that actual member/page identity rather than inventing a separately
acquired Cover publication.

Existing `skill:climb` and `skill:jump` already preserve Long Skill types,
first-MOV-plus-distance semantics and replacement of the normal 2-inch bonus
by an exact profile-listed distance. Preserve
[RS-MOV-PARAM-001](rules-semantics.md#rs-mov-param-001--parenthetical-movement-distances-modify-a-skill-not-mov-itself).
Printed pp. 32-34 still require fuller procedure coverage: consistent
outer-edge/underside measurement; Climb's stopping condition on full-base
horizontal contact, continued hanging and native declaration/ARO limits;
Unconscious/Prone handling; and Jump's trajectory, touching-surface endpoint
and no-fall/Idle fallback. Do not interpret Climb's landing conditions as
requiring every Order to reach horizontal ground: p. 33 explicitly permits
a Trooper to remain on the wall between Orders.

Keep Cover restrictions conditional. N5.3 p. 32 denies Partial Cover while
performing Climb or holding onto a vertical surface; p. 33's example still
denies it after that Climb reaches a horizontal upper surface. Jump denies
it for the entire Order in which Jump was declared. Climbing Plus p. 88 has
its own vertical-surface restriction and declaration modifications; preserve
the governing Skill and position instead of inferring a universal Cover
grant or ban. The archived Cover member retains the N5.2 versus N5.3 Climb
wording, which is historical evidence rather than a replacement for p. 32.
Visual review of the printed pp. 33, 41 and 42 diagrams corroborates the
specific Climb example and contact/obstruction distinctions; it does not
establish a blanket Cover rule for every elevated position.

The published `faq-ruling:vault-during-climb-and-jump` already supplies the
separately dated vaulting clarification for Climb and Jump. Preserve its
v0.0 original publication, selected v0.1 citation and N5.3 applicability;
do not duplicate it as a new undated core fact. Existing Climbing Plus
references and effects remain intact. This review changed no curation,
runtime artifact or test contract and does not complete V1S-033C or its
source/API/browser acceptance.
