ZIP actually contains `Immunity` revision **3643** and `Combined_Ammunition`
**3000**, matching the maintained exact-revision citations rather than a later
live page. `T2_Ammunition` **2999** and `Vulnerability` **3156** retain their
separate source identities. These support current agreement, not a new historical
before/after finding or an official expansion of the case set.

| Reviewed condition | Existing scoped result |
| --- | --- |
| ARM protection; ARM AP+DA source 10 or AP+Exp source 13 | Whole covered Ammunition becomes Normal: one hit save, two on Critical unless Critical immunity also applies |
| AP protection; ARM AP+DA source 10 | Only AP is removed; DA retains two hit saves and three on Critical, with full ARM |
| AP protection; Silenced Pistol AP+Shock source 30, BTS save | AP halving is removed; Shock remains, using full BTS with one/two saves |
| AP protection; AP+T2 CC Weapon source 40, ARM save | T2 remains; full ARM, one/two saves, two Wounds for the failed hit save versus one for the extra Critical save |
| BTS protection; E/M CC Weapon N+E/M source 5 | Covered combined Ammunition becomes Normal: one/two BTS saves, with the ordinary Normal Wound effect |
| BTS protection; Silenced Pistol source 30 | Both AP and Shock effects are covered; Normal result uses full BTS, not ARM |
| Shock protection; Silenced Pistol source 30 | AP remains: BTS/2, one/two saves; the Shock-specific direct-to-Dead effect is removed |
| E/M protection; E/M CC Weapon source 5 | E/M is removed while Normal remains: full BTS, one/two saves and ordinary failed-save Wounds |

The first row represents two separately stored cases; there are **nine**, not
eight, reviewed records. All retain ordered canonical components and the
`non-comms` condition; Weapon-specific cases retain exact Weapon identities.
Critical counts are conditional reference facts, not extra effects per component.
Neither the source composition map nor the runtime Weapon profile acquires a
calculated Immunity result. Unlisted theoretical pairings are not automatically
derived and do not require fabricating a new published example to restate the
general rule. Attribute/Comms/State/Critical exceptions and SRC-002 Silent timing
remain the separate V1S-037 review; the published uncertainty is preserved.

**Existing validation and no-change decision:** inspected
`tests/test_immunity_ap_t2_cc.py`, `tests/test_immunity_ap_silenced_pistol.py`,
`tests/test_immunity_bts_component_exceptions.py` and the matching closed-case
validator. They already pin printed clauses, exact source/Weapon/component
identity, surviving effects, curated/runtime/API parity and negative scope/evidence
mutations. The current Immunity record and these relevant implementation/tests
are unchanged from `b381756d317685c3acc1f0e57366cb1da73f25ef`, whose historical
validation is retained in [testing](testing.md#parallel-windows-development-and-linux-validation).
This source review ran **no tests** and does not attribute that historical run
to later Hacking/Template changes. No changed data, new consumer or missing
concrete regression assertion was identified, so there is **no additional VD/VR
obligation** and no changelog entry for this internal review.

**Disposition:** V1S-036 retires as source-reviewed no-change. Its parent remains
open; V1S-034/035 acceptance, V1S-037 exceptions, V1S-038 full Ammunition
publication parity, V1S-062–066 occurrence/index work and V1S-081–090 final
candidate/source-to-browser gates are not accepted by this review.

## BTS, Critical and State Immunity review (V1S-037; 2026-11-04)

**Project domain:** Data processing

**Selection:** V1S-034/035 remain deferred for phased acceptance, with no due
queue drain at entry (VD 3 / VR 4, dated November 4); V1S-036 has retired.
V1S-037 is the earliest independent remaining source review.

**Pinned evidence:** the English N5.3 PDF SHA-256
`53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`
and Spanish N5.3 SHA-256
`7f564c864c6f26d6b218a0477c8afeba99da5c56e1a5369bb259f423689bb28f`
were rehashed. English pp. 95–96 and Spanish p. 99 agree on Attribute-based
protection, the four printed examples, Comms/State exception and surviving
Non-Lethal/Stunned Traits. English p. 175 and Spanish p. 182 preserve the
known Silent wording difference. The already-selected Wiki Immunity revision
3643 and Combined Ammunition 3000 remain corroborating, separately identified
evidence; they are not a new ruling.

The stored Immunity record contains **23** reviewed cases: nine combined
(V1S-036), five Weapon, five Critical, two Combined Saving Roll, one Comms
and one Vulnerability. This review inspected the fourteen non-combined cases:

| Case group | Existing bounded representation |
| --- | --- |
| Five Weapon cases | Flash Pulse retains BTS, Non-Lethal and failed-save Stunned; E/Mitter and E/M Grenade Launcher keep their own Non-Lethal profiles; Missile Launcher uses Blast Mode; Monofilament ignores ARM=0/Dead only under the stated ARM protection |
| Five Critical cases | AP+T2, AP+DA, AP+Exp, Silenced Pistol and E/M CC Weapon remove only the Critical-added save, preserving original Saving Attribute, component effects and ordinary hit rolls |
| Two Plasma cases | Enhanced protection does not remove the ARM+BTS saves; Critical protection removes only the additional ARM save |
| Oblivion Comms case | BTS protection does not suppress the AP Comms Attack or Isolated effect; applicable State protection prevents Isolated without deriving a Saving Roll count from Program Burst |
| Viral Vulnerability | The explicit example matches a Weapon name containing Viral, not a newly invented Ammunition component |

Source and case evidence remain distinct: the four N5.3 printed examples keep
their explicit labels, while the additional exact-profile consequences remain
derived from general rules. State outcomes require their declared failure and
target conditions; no case declares a Unit's current State. The existing
Silenced Pistol cases do not decide Silent's earlier Dodge modifier.

**Remaining authority boundary:** Silent can modify a Dodge Roll before the
Saving Roll that activates ordinary Attribute protection. The selected English
clause explicitly refers to a Face to Face Dodge; the Spanish clause does not.
The supplied FAQ v0.0 English and v0.1 English/Spanish PDFs were rehashed to
their maintained pins and contain no Silent/Immunity term occurrence. That
selected-term inventory is not proof that no later ruling exists. Existing
public clarifications and the review-needed marker preserve both questions;
[SRC-002](official-source-clarifications.md#src-002--silent-versus-attribute-immunity-bilingual-qualifier)
still requires official authority. No publisher contact or new source
acquisition was performed.

**Validation and disposition:** existing source/API/negative-scope coverage in
`tests/test_immunity_oblivion_comms.py`,
`tests/test_immunity_combined_critical_completion.py`, the Plasma/Flash Pulse/
Monofilament/E/M profile tests and `tests/test_silent_immunity_source_scope.py`
already captures these exact facts and preserved uncertainty. This read-only
source review ran no tests and changed no data, runtime artifacts or semantics;
no missing concrete regression assertion or additional VD/VR was identified.
No changelog entry is warranted. V1S-037 is **still open**, narrowed to SRC-002
with the publisher/new-source retry trigger in TODO. Other source-confirmed
exceptions need no additional implementation from this review. V1S-038 and
whole-source/browser/release acceptance remain independent work.

## Immunity source-162 occurrence review (V1S-054; 2026-11-04)

**Inputs:** the pinned September 29 N5 Army source/raw archive, English
N5.3 Core Rules pp. 95–96 (PDF SHA-256
`53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`),
reviewed `skill:immunity` and RS-AW-IMM-001. The read-only
`tools.audit_source_variant_inputs --catalog skills --source-id 162`
reports **1,406 direct Skill occurrences**, each with exactly one `TEXT`
extra, no standalone raw value and no multi-extra signature. The ten exact
qualifiers are:

- **Ammunition components:** `30:Shock` (759) and `249:AP` (75).
  Their scope is the named Ammunition, not every component of a combined
  attack. On AP+DA, for example, immunity to AP does not suppress DA's
  additional rolls. Do not interpret a full attack's outcome from this
  occurrence alone.
- **Critical:** `49:Critical` (157). Removes the Critical-added Saving
  Roll; does not by itself remove other Saving Rolls or weapon effects.
- **State:** `247:POS` (8), `327:IMM-B` (20), and `348:Isolated`
  (17). These identify the listed State, with N5.3 p. 95's specific
  Comms/special-rule condition; they are not an unconditional immunity
  to every kind of attack or a static assertion of the Trooper's State.
- **Trait:** `292:Continous Damage` (7). Army's misspelled literal
  `Continous` is preserved as provenance; the printed rule and canonical
  `trait:continuous-damage` identify *Continuous Damage*. This is
  Trait-specific protection, not an Ammunition substitution or a general
  immunity to all wound-causing effects.
- **Attribute-based:** `315:Enhanced` (75), `319:ARM` (273), and
  `320:BTS` (15). Enhanced combines ARM/BTS protection; the matching
  Saving Roll Attribute and non-Comms conditions determine applicability.
  The two attribute protections must not be conflated with the narrower
  Immunity (AP)/(Shock) component cases.

**Disposition:** All ten source labels have a source-backed family/category
interpretation; this review found **no additional standalone gameplay
rule**, but did not publish reviewed per-occurrence metadata or the
cross-Army usage index. Source ID 162 therefore correctly remains
`unresolved-occurrence-parameter` in the current automated audit until
the exact typed signatures and fail-closed checks are promoted under
V1S-062–066. RS-AW-IMM-001 and V1S-036–038 retain mixed Ammunition,
Critical, Comms and target-specific precedence/acceptance. No data,
API, browser or generated artifacts were changed in this review.

## BS Attack source-201 occurrence review (V1S-055; 2026-11-04)

**Inputs:** pinned September 29, 2026 Army/raw snapshot and the English
N5.3 Core Rules pp. 39, 63–67 and 75 (PDF SHA-256
`53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`).
The read-only `tools.audit_source_variant_inputs --catalog skills
--source-id 201` reports **1,531 direct BS Attack occurrences**,
distributed over exactly **ten distinct single-TEXT-extra signatures**,
with no unqualified occurrences, raw value or multi-extra signature:

- **Roll/Burst:** `6/TEXT:-3` (77) applies a negative modifier to
  the opponent when an eligible BS Attack opposes the opponent in a Face
  to Face Roll; it is not an attacker BS penalty or unconditional
  opponent penalty. `8/TEXT:+1B` (273) improves the owner's
  Active-Turn BS Attack Burst subject to the general B6 limit and any
  stricter Skill-specific restriction. `308/TEXT:+1SD` (176) grants
  one extra die with a mandatory discard, not an additional Burst
  or additional Disposable use; p. 75 excludes Long Skills and
  Skills/Weapons/Equipment requiring no Roll.
- **Added Ammunition/Traits:** `30/TEXT:Shock` (231),
  `249/TEXT:AP` (116), and `294/TEXT:T2` (53) denote
  attack-scoped named Ammunition; `292/TEXT:Continous Damage` (26)
  denotes the Continuous Damage Trait, preserving the literal Army
  misspelling as source provenance. N5.3 p. 75 expressly illustrates
  BS Attack (Shock) and BS Attack (Continuous Damage). The canonical
  Ammunition/Traits definitions determine mechanics; do not change
  stored weapon profile Ammunition, infer legal combinations or collapse
  independent components into a calculated attack outcome.
- **Saving Roll modifiers:** `305/TEXT:SR-1` (507) is explicitly
  illustrated on N5.3 p. 75 as a target Saving Roll -1 MOD
  against the user's BS Attack hits. `337/TEXT:SR-2` (9)
  is a separate Army signature; the analogous -2 interpretation
  follows the source's SR notation but was **not separately illustrated**
  in the reviewed rulebook clause. Retain the precise source value
  and distinguish the BS Skill modifier from Hacking Program Upgrade
  `SR-1/SR-2` values and Firewall defenses. All nine SR-2
  matches occur on profile-owned records, including Mormaers,
  Gecko, Sheskiin and Caskuda; never aggregate repeated profiles
  across Armies as distinct Skill semantics.
- **Guided:** `31/TEXT:Guided` (63) is the specific
  BS Attack (Guided) Skill of p. 39: Active Turn, Targeted opponent,
  range/possible trajectory, Blast/circular-template preference,
  normally B1 regardless of Burst MODs, maximum five Active Player
  Attacks per Turn, and specific BS Weapon (PH)/(WIP) exclusions.
  It is not a generic modifier, extra Burst or permission to bypass
  normal attack constraints beyond the explicit exemptions.

**Disposition:** All ten source signatures have been inventoried and
mapped to their applicable source clauses; the different scopes are
not interchangeable. The current `skill:bs-attack` family definition
remains correct, and no generated data or public API/browser projection
changes were authorized by this *review*. The automated source family
still reports `unresolved-occurrence-parameter` until each permitted
signature is published as exact, tested typed metadata. The
source-characterization regression passed under VR-0072 at `b381756d`,
retiring V1S-055; V1S-062–066 own subsequent cross-Army
index publication and final browser/source-parity acceptance, not
a simulation of BS Attack or its interaction with every Weapon.

## CC Attack source-240 occurrence review (V1S-056; 2026-11-04)

**Inputs:** pinned September 29, 2026 Army/raw snapshot, English N5.3
Core Rules pp. 37, 51–52, 63–67 and 75 (PDF SHA-256
`53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`).
The read-only source-variant audit reports **625 direct CC Attack
occurrences**, consisting of ten distinct single-`TEXT` extras; every
occurrence has one extra, none has raw-only or multi-extra data:

- **Attacker CC modifier:** `1/TEXT:+3` (2) and `3/TEXT:+9` (2).
  N5.3 p. 75 applies positive Skill MODs to the owner when using the
  Skill. Both `+9` appearances are the same Gator Pilot option listed in
  two Army contexts; neither changes the saved base CC Attribute.
- **Opponent CC modifier:** `6/TEXT:-3` (77) and
  `7/TEXT:-6` (136) are negative modifiers against opponents in
  applicable Face to Face Rolls, not reductions to the owner's CC
  or blanket penalties on non-opposed Rolls (p. 75).
- **Burst:** `8/TEXT:+1B` (181) is an owner Burst modifier with the
  usual Active-Turn and B6 limits (p. 75). It is distinct from the
  multiple-Trooper CC bonus and the special Reactive-Turn rule on p. 52.
- **Ammunition/Traits:** `30/TEXT:Shock` (70),
  `249/TEXT:AP` (48), `292/TEXT:Continous Damage` (1) and
  `293/TEXT:Antimaterial` (106) name a specific Ammunition or
  Weapon Trait. Preserve the Army's literal `Continous` and
  `Antimaterial` spellings as provenance, while canonical references
  use Continuous Damage and Anti-materiel. These Skill annotations do
  not rewrite source Weapon profiles or establish arbitrary combined
  Ammunition outcomes.
- **Unresolved legacy Damage notation:** `4/TEXT:+1 Dam` (2) occurs
  only on profile-owned **REINF: SHONA CARANO** (unit 1639, occurrence
  13859) and **REINF: CHAKSA AUXILIARS** (unit 1675, occurrence 69477).
  N5.3 p. 37 uses **PS**, with *lower* numerical PS indicating a more
  dangerous weapon; p. 75 supplies no `CC Attack (+1 Dam)` rule. The
  literal string does not distinguish a legacy N4-style Damage bonus,
  a change to N5 PS or an additional Wound. Its meaning must remain
  unresolved (SRC-032), not converted into a made-up PS or Wounds effect.

**Disposition:** The ten source signatures and distinct modifier/attack
scopes are characterized without granting any unreviewed effects.
The current `skill:cc-attack` definition and source-aware Weapon
profiles remain unchanged. Source 240 remains
`unresolved-occurrence-parameter` pending exact typed publication;
SRC-032's `+1 Dam` meaning must remain excluded unless source authority
clarifies it. The exact regression passed under VR-0073 at `b381756d`,
retiring V1S-056; V1S-062–066 own the broader occurrence projection,
API/browser use and complete parity checks.

## Peripheral source-243 occurrence review (V1S-057; 2026-11-04)

**Inputs:** selected September 29, 2026 N5 Army/raw archive and English
N5.3 Core Rules pp. 106–108 (PDF SHA-256
`53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`).
The read-only `tools.audit_source_variant_inputs --catalog skills
--source-id 243` inventory reports **439 direct occurrences** and
exactly five single-`TEXT` signatures, without unqualified rows, raw
values or multi-extra signatures:

- `40/TEXT:Synchronized`: **206**. Coherency is checked relative to
  the Controller, with immediate Disconnected effects on a failed
  check; the linked Controller and Synchronized Peripherals act in
  one Order under the subtype restrictions (p. 106).
- `41/TEXT:Servant`: **56**. The Doctor/Engineer Controller is eligible
  to perform the Skill using the Servant's Silhouette contact, but the
  **Controller** performs the actual Roll (p. 106).
- `47/TEXT:Control`: **6**. The associated Control Unit uses a
  designated Spearhead; eligible active targeting is limited to
  Controller and Spearhead, with separate member Coherency rules
  (p. 107).
- `322/TEXT:Ancillary`: **163**. The Peripheral is deployed via
  the Controller's Place Deployable action, *not* alongside the
  Controller at initial deployment; there is a specific Fireteam and
  Coordinated Order exception before deployment or while disconnected
  or Null (p. 107).
- `374/TEXT:Cyberplug`: **8**. Four distinct Army-local
  Connected/Autonomous **profile pairs** (two Unit IDs, two Armies)
  carry the same subtype. Mode selection, Orders and AROs are governed
  by the subtype and not by a generic Connected-only assumption
  (pp. 107–108).

An exact source-extra ownership lookup for **all five signatures**
returned 439 rows, each with `owner=profile`, source `group_id`,
`parent_id`, `occurrence_id`, its Army and its literal subtype.
Examples include AUXBOT/Synchronized, PALBOTS/Servant,
ANTIPODE and Puppetbot/Control, Crabbot/Ancillary and Sartroid
Ranters/Puzzlers/Cyberplug. The **eight** Cyberplug rows represent
both connected and autonomous profiles in Combined Army and Next Wave;
there are not eight unique Controllers or eight independent Units.

**Ownership boundary:** Skill occurrence ownership identifies the
*Peripheral's own profile*. It does **not** establish a Controller
selection, a Controller's Special Skill, an Army-wide capability or an
`application_peripheral_controller_access` row. Existing Army-local
Peripheral definitions and attached Unit/Loadout relationships are
separate from the canonical five rules subtype identities and from
the Controller access-pool model. The current published source pair
intentionally has zero reviewed Controller access-pool rows; do not
manufacture them from the Skill subtype, profile name, source group,
or shared logical Unit ID. For Cyberplug, preserve both source profile
identities even when they share a subtype qualifier.

**Disposition:** All five raw signatures have exact, already-reviewed
family subtype meanings and no extra signed MODs. No additional
gameplay effects, controller assignments or source projection are
inferred by this review. Source 243 remains
`unresolved-occurrence-parameter` until its five complete signatures
are explicitly published with reviewed typed metadata; V1S-062–066
own cross-Army usage projection/API/browser acceptance. The pinned
ownership/subtype regression passed under VR-0074 at `b381756d`,
retiring V1S-057.

## Transmutation source-246 occurrence review (V1S-058; 2026-11-04)

**Inputs:** selected September 29, 2026 N5 Army/raw archive
and English N5.3 Core Rules pp. 117–118 (PDF SHA-256
`53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`).
The source audit identifies **295 direct Transmutation Skill occurrences**
in exactly eight distinct single-`TEXT` signatures, no unqualified
rows, raw values or multi-extra signatures. An owner lookup for each
signature returned **295 profile-owned occurrences**, not eight
standalone Unit identities or inferred source transition edges:

- **Wound thresholds:** `44/TEXT:1` (146),
  `267/TEXT:2` (64) and `354/TEXT:3` (1).
  The number counts Wounds to the initial profile, not extra
  attacks, a Roll modifier or a fresh VITA/STR pool. The profile
  changes at the end of the relevant Order, after the Guts Roll
  as clarified on p. 118; existing Wounds and applicable States
  transfer to the replacement profile.
- **Escape System:** `321/TEXT:Escape System -2` (57) and
  `323/TEXT:Escape System -3` (8, with **one trailing ASCII space** in the source value).
  The dash is part of the raw label before the Wound threshold,
  not a signed negative modifier. In addition to the threshold
  change, place an Eclipse circular template centered on the
  replacement and cancel Possessed State if applicable. Among
  the eight Escape System -3 rows are Iguana, Maximus and its
  Reinforcement counterpart; the source string is preserved
  without stripping whitespace or silently collapsing Army
  occurrences. The source label alone does not identify or
  authorize a destination profile link.
- **Movement/ARO triggered:** `254/TEXT:Auto` (14) and
  `346/TEXT:Hatching` (3). Auto permits profile choice
  at deployment and switching with a Movement-labeled Skill;
  Hatching begins in Seed-Embryo form and uses its Developed
  profile when the first Short Skill has the Movement Label or
  after a successful Dodge ARO. The fourteen Auto occurrences
  include *both* High Mobility/Combat forms of the Sù-Jiàn
  and mounted/dismounted contexts, which must not be merged
  into one source occurrence.
- **Roll-triggered:** `332/TEXT:WIP` (2), both attached to
  SWITCHERS GRUPPA's source profile in separate Nomads
  Army contexts. Under p. 117 the option to switch occurs
  before the Order/ARO on a successful Normal WIP Roll;
  the second profile persists through the current Player Turn,
  reverting at the start of the States Phase. **No modifier**
  is present in either source qualifier; the printed example
  `WIP-3` on p. 118 cannot be imported into these `WIP`
  occurrences.

**Ownership and transition boundary:** a source-246 annotation
belongs to its exact Army/Profile owner, which may be repeated
across different Army contexts or on both forms of a single
Trooper. The subtype identifies the transition rule and its
trigger, not the destination profile ID. The matched
application's Unit/Profile/Loadout relationship graph must
resolve actual before/after identities separately from source
signature evidence. Do not auto-connect two profiles solely
because they share a Unit ID, name or a subtype, or convert
an Escape System dash into a negative Wound threshold.

**Disposition:** eight source signatures and printed trigger
conditions are reviewed without creating a live transition
simulator or inventing Unit relationships. Source 246 remains
`unresolved-occurrence-parameter` in the automated audit
until exact typed source values are published. The pinned-source
profile/qualifier regression passed under VR-0075 at `b381756d`,
retiring V1S-058; V1S-062–066 retain the eventual cross-Army index
and browser acceptance.

## Hacking Device Plus source-101 qualifier reconciliation (V1S-060; 2026-11-04)

The pinned September 29, 2026 Army/raw snapshot and matched rules database
were reconciled with the existing reviewed Hacking Device metadata and
`tests/test_audit_source_variant_inputs.py`. Equipment source `101`
has **126 direct occurrences**: **22 unqualified**, **102 with reviewed
qualifiers** and **two unresolved**. The seven exact qualified
signatures are:

- `17:UPGRADE: Fairy Dust` (46, reviewed; source contains U+00A0);
- `269:UPGRADE: Oblivion (+1B)` (3, reviewed);
- `314:UPGRADE: Total Control (SR-2)` (46, reviewed);
- `339:UPGRADE: Trinity (SR-1)` (2, reviewed);
- the **ordered pair** `343:UPGRADE: Trinity (PS=5)` then
  `287:UPGRADE: Zero Pain` (2, reviewed **as a whole combination**);
- `344:UPGRADE: SR-1` (2, **unreviewed**; no named owning program);
- `378:UPGRADE: Trinity (+1SD)` (3, reviewed).

The published standalone extras attach their effects to explicit Hacking
Program identities (grant, Burst, Saving Roll or Special Die). The
complete ordered pair grants Zero Pain and modifies Trinity's PS;
approval of that pair does not approve a lone `343` or a reversed
pair. No Device Plus Firewall extra is present or inferred.
The two source-`344` unqualified Upgrade occurrences are already
pinned at **Charontid option occurrences 51441 and 51461** and remain
intentionally unresolved under [SRC-001](official-source-clarifications.md#src-001--charontid-hacking-device-plus-unqualified-sr-1).
The source provides neither the affected Program nor a basis to
apply this Saving Roll MOD to every Hacking Program.

**Validation ownership:** existing
`test_hacking_device_program_grants_preserve_unreviewed_overrides`,
`test_charontid_unqualified_sr_owners_are_exact_source_loadouts`,
and exact combination drift tests already pin the reviewed
single-extra effects, ordered pair, source owner and the remaining
fail-closed unknown. No new source/behavior change or previously missing
test assertion was identified, so **no additional VD/VR entry** was
created for this bounded review. The existing `unresolved-occurrence-parameter`
classification is correct, not a blocker to publishing source-backed
reviewed qualifiers with the unresolved two left visible as raw
provenance. Release-wide execution/CI and usage-index acceptance remain
separate; this review alone is not new test evidence.

## Cross-family variant collision review (V1S-061; 2026-11-04)

The selected September 29 Army/raw archive and current matched
rules database were checked using the read-only Skill/Equipment
`--unresolved-only` source-variant inventories. The current summary
has **10 unresolved Skill source IDs** (`47`, `49`, `53`, `83`,
`162`, `201`, `240`, `243`, `246`, `272`) and **one unresolved
Equipment source ID** (`101`); all have literal `TEXT` extras.
They are partially reviewed families, not missing complete rules
definitions or proof that every one of their direct occurrences is
unreviewed. Individual source reviews own the precise unresolved
semantics; prior reviewed singles/combinations remain valid.

**Cross-family alias hazards** (the same extra ID or spelling does
not grant the same modifier or effect to another Skill):

- `1/TEXT:+3` occurs under Engineer `49`, Doctor `53`
  and CC Attack `240`. A source ID plus its owning action and
  applicable Roll must be preserved, not just `extra_id=1`.
- `6/TEXT:-3` occurs under BS Attack `201` and CC Attack `240`,
  while it can be independently reviewed for other Skill/Equipment
  families with explicitly different applicability. It is not
  a universal attribute or target modifier.
- `30/TEXT:Shock`, `249/TEXT:AP`, and
  `292/TEXT:Continous Damage` occur in Immunity `162` and one
  or both of BS Attack `201` and CC Attack `240`.
  Immunity negates a matching effect; an Attack variant adds the
  named Ammunition/Trait, so identical string values have **opposite
  operational roles**.
- `277/TEXT:ReRoll` is unresolved independently for Infiltration
  `47` and Engineer `49`; same string does not determine a shared
  reroll success value or eligibility. Engineer and Doctor
  `366/TEXT:ReRoll -3` remain under [SRC-029](official-source-clarifications.md#src-029--doctorengineer-reroll--3-lacks-explicit-n5-wip-scope).
- Terrain `58` recognizes `33/TEXT:Total`, but Religious Troop
  `83` must not inherit that interpretation; [SRC-028](official-source-clarifications.md#src-028--confessor-heavy-spec-ops-terrain-total-apparently-attached-to-religious-troop)
  remains unresolved.
- Equipment Hacking Device Plus `101` carries two unqualified
  `344/TEXT:UPGRADE: SR-1` Charontid options. They cannot inherit
  named-program Trinity `SR-1` semantics or the unrelated BS Attack
  `SR-1` target-Saving-Roll modifier.

**Literal/identity guardrails:** report unknowns at the tuple
`(catalog, source_id, ordered (extra_id, type, raw_value))`.
Do not trim internal/trailing spaces, normalize NBSPs, discard an
unknown component, sort a compound extra signature, promote a
single reviewed component to an unreviewed pair, or conflate
a repeated Unit/Profile across distinct Army occurrences.
Transmutation's `Escape System -3` carries one trailing ASCII
space and the Device Plus `UPGRADE: Fairy Dust` uses NBSP; source
strings must remain byte-exact even when the player-facing
explanation uses canonical terms.

**Disposition:** this cross-family read-only sweep found no newly
unclassified source family and no authority to reinterpret an
unknown occurrence. It does reveal an explicit regression gap:
the earlier generic unknown-extra and per-family tests did not jointly
pin the negative *cross-family collision* cases above. The new
VD-0019/VR-0076 guard passed at `b381756d`, retiring V1S-061.
Full cross-Army occurrence publication stays V1S-062–066, and
publisher clarifications SRC-001/028/029 remain open.

## Shared core scenario concepts and collection supplements (V1S-039; 2026-11-04)

**Review boundary:** the pinned N5.3 Core collection
(`data/curated/rules/n5-core-v5.3.json`), the separately published
ITS Season 18 Battleground pilot, the reviewed FAQ layer, and their
existing storage, API and normal-browser composition contracts. This is
a bounded applicability and presentation review, **not** a fresh
whole-PDF clause reconciliation, full browser run or release acceptance.

- **Shared identities and local effects:** `rule:scenario:killing`
  is reused only through selected scenario inclusions; the unique
  `rule:specialist-troops:standard` definition is an InfinityDB
  General Rules baseline with seven source-listed qualifying Skills.
  Domination, Supplies and Firefight include that baseline with explicit
  per-scenario add/remove lists; Annihilation does not. Scenario-local
  Hack Consoles and Pick Up Supply Boxes are scoped Skills, not Army
  Unit/Profile grants. Consoles and Supply Boxes are mission elements,
  not inferred Equipment. Firefight's Lieutenant/landing exceptions
  remain locally included Rules rather than global replacements.
- **Collection and supplement isolation:** current Core publications
  select `n5-core@5.3`; the Battleground pilot requires an explicit
  `its-18@2026.09.01` selection. Absent/mismatched revisions do not
  fall back between collections. `ScenarioComponents.record` rejects
  out-of-scope definitions; `RulesDatabase._compose_records` filters
  scenario-scoped contributions when composing a selected scenario.
  Independent supplements remain attached with their own source
  collection/citations instead of overwriting the canonical definition.
  FAQ applicability is queried in explicit scenario/source context and
  never written back into the scenario's own facts.
- **Ordinary browser contract:** the Specialist Troops general Rule
  is searchable and explains that qualification depends on the
  selected scenario; links lead to Domination, Supplies and Firefight.
  Embedded scenario Rules and Skills use the shared reference renderer
  inside an explicitly selected scenario, while general reference
  details can display scope and source provenance. The five current
  published scenario identities are not a five-scenario Core set.

**Existing evidence inspected:** `tests/test_scenario_components.py`
(shared-definition reuse, specialist deltas, invalid scope and scoped
Skill rendering); `tests/test_scenario_catalog.py` (24 Core game-size
configurations and explicit ITS publication selection);
`tests/test_scenario_api.py` (missing/incorrect collection rejection,
Battleground isolation and FAQ applicability); and
`tests/test_verify_scenario_faq_browser.py::test_specialist_troops_search_leads_to_general_rule_and_scenarios`
(1440/390 px search-to-scenario navigation). The standard
`tools.audit_core_scenario_objects` returned the expected four
Core scenarios, four Consoles, three Supply Boxes and 17 separately
reviewed deployed objects; `tools.report_reference_baseline` reported
23 categories/126 source groups; both exited 0 at the clean starting
revision `0ccd35fb`. These inventories are not proof of every
source clause. Browser and API tests were **inspected, not rerun**
in this review; their previously recorded acceptance is not
relabelled as a new test result.

**Disposition:** no new source-backed applicability or ordinary
presentation defect was confirmed in this bounded review; V1S-039
retires as source-reviewed no-change. No new VD/VR is created because
no product or test implementation changed and no concrete missing
regression was identified. The full scenario source reconciliation,
responsive/accessibility browser reruns, FAQ-specific authority and
final release-pair acceptance remain V1S-042–049/081–090; the optional
Reinforcements browser acceptance remains V1S-041; source clarification SRC-030 is open.

## Four core scenario objective, setup, scoring and ending reconciliation (V1S-042; 2026-11-04)

**Inputs and boundary:** the locally pinned English N5.3 Core PDF
(`data/pdf/rules/n5-rules-v5.3-en.pdf`), printed pp. 149–156;
the Spanish N5.3 PDF (`data/pdf/rules/n5-rules-v5.3-es.pdf`),
printed pp. 153 and 155 for the two disputed chart cells; and
`data/curated/rules/n5-core-v5.3.json` scenario/component definitions.
The source pages were read for this reconciliation. Existing maintained
parse, API and browser assertions were inspected, **not rerun**. This
reviews mission facts, not the still-open spatial/map and live browser
release gates.

- **Annihilation (pp. 149–150):** all six Army Points/SWC sizes,
  both three-band 1/3/4-point objectives, 2-point enemy-Lieutenant
  objective, Killing inclusion, deployment and third-round or
  all-Troopers-Null endings match the maintained facts. The English
  350-point *surviving* VP cells overlap and leave a gap;
  Spanish N5.3 printed p. 153 explicitly supplies the published
  85–175 / 176–270 / greater-than-270 bands. Preserve the
  `350-point-survival-bands` reviewed source issue and its explanatory
  normal/API presentation; this is **not** a silent correction of
  the English source.
- **Domination (pp. 151–152):** the equal-but-at-least-one
  Quadrant point (1), more Quadrants (2), and four individually
  hacked Consoles (1 each) retain their distinct end-of-round/
  end-of-game scoring. Quadrant occupancy/Null/Shasvastii, Hacker
  +3 Console interaction, seven qualifying Specialist Skills,
  Peripherals exception, six size/minimum-VP rows and the
  minimum-VP Tactical Phase ending are represented.
  English 350-point SWC is **6**; Spanish N5.3 p. 155 prints
  **7**. Keep the public `350-point-swc`
  `needs-verification` source issue. No publisher adjudication
  was inferred.
- **Supplies (pp. 153–154):** the three Supply Boxes yield
  2 points each, with independent +2 awards for *more* and
  *all* Boxes, at game end. Scenarios retain the Specialist-only
  pickup (no Roll), no Silhouette-contact deployment, one-Box
  carrier maximum, Model-versus-Marker/Null/enemy-contact rules,
  seven Specialist qualifiers/Peripherals exclusion, all six
  sizes/minimum-VP rows and the same scenario ending boundary.
- **Firefight (pp. 155–156):** four comparative end-of-game
  objectives score 2/1/3/4 for surviving Specialists,
  killed Specialists, killed Lieutenants and killed Army
  Points, respectively. Killing, Open-Information/replacement
  Lieutenant, Designated Landing Area (+3 Combat Jump and
  enemy-Deployment-Zone AD exception), Specialist list and
  third-round/all-Troopers-Null endings remain scenario-local.
  All six source sizes have the standard SWC table.

**Existing verification contract:** `tests/test_scenario_components.py`
pins each scenario's parsed scoring, source issues and mission endings;
`tests/test_scenario_definition.py` pins per-size awards,
constraints and reusable setup; `tests/test_scenario_catalog.py`
checks all 24 size configurations and source pages; and
`tests/test_scenario_api.py` checks published values and source
disclosures. This review found no additional source-backed mismatch
in the four mission-fact domains and made **no** product/data/test
change, so no new VD/VR obligation is due. V1S-042 retires as a
bounded source-reconciled no-change review. Do not interpret it
as execution of those suites or the outstanding V1S-043–045/088
map, scoped Skill/FAQ, responsive/browser and frozen-candidate gates.

## Core scenario geometry and deterministic map acceptance (V1S-043; 2026-11-05)

**Pinned inputs:** English N5.3 Core Rules printed pp. 150, 152,
154 and 156, with the scenario source tables on pp. 149–155;
`data/curated/rules/n5-core-v5.3.json`; the maintained
`scenario_definition`, geometry and SVG renderer; and the tracked
`data/generated/rules.db` accessed by the ordinary scenario API.
This is the bounded geometry/marker/publication gate at
`9daddcbda1bee21660ba3839e354904a70c9ed32`, **not** the frozen
v1.0 release-pair or hosted deployment sign-off.

- **All 24 game-size choices:** each of four current Core scenarios
  has exactly 150, 200, 250, 300, 350 and 400 Army Point selections.
  The source charts give 24 × 32 in with opposing 8-inch Deployment
  Zones at 150, 32 × 48 in with opposing 12-inch zones at 200/250,
  and 48 × 48 in with opposing 12-inch zones at 300/350/400.
  Annihilation and Firefight reuse these same three configurations.
  The indexed scenario API resolves their selected configuration
  without drawing a hypothetical intermediate size.
- **Domination:** the four scoring Quadrants remain distinct from
  the four Console A Markers at their centers. The source-position
  nearest edge/center measurements are 6/12 in (150 points),
  8/18 in (200/250) and 12/18 in (300/350/400).
  The standalone map retains the four 40 mm physical Console
  footprints and source-backed position rulers in both in/cm
  display modes; it does not change the game-table geometry or
  reinterpret the four Quadrants as small marker areas.
- **Supplies:** exactly three 25 mm Supply Box Markers appear
  along the table's central line, one at center and two with
  exactly 8 inches between the *marker boundary* and the
  corresponding table edge at every supported size.
  The SVG's boundary-measurement labels remain source-derived.
  Annihilation and Firefight contain no mission markers.
- **Determinism and distance labels:** repeated rendering of
  each of the twelve maintained geometry presets is byte-identical
  and produces valid SVG with an inch-based `viewBox`. Inch/cm
  selection changes ruler text (using the maintained 2.5 cm
  game-distance conversion) but not geometry or marker footprints.
  Unknown units, marker types and styles fail closed. The API
  serves each of the 24 selected Core sizes as an SVG; the
  marker-bearing maps are also checked in both units for all
  six size choices. This is compositional coverage, **not** a
  claim that every one of 48 possible size/unit pairs was separately
  byte-compared in the API.

## Core scenario scoped Skills, Specialists, overlays and FAQ (V1S-044; 2026-11-05)

(pp. 149–156), particularly Domination's Hack Consoles and
Specialist Troops clauses (pp. 151–152), Supplies' Pickup/Box
and Specialist clauses (pp. 153–154), and Firefight's Lieutenant
and landing exceptions (p. 155). Core scenario selection remains

requires `its-18@2026.09.01`. This is focused scope/application
acceptance on `49eab17cc64b855b1abf0d7a5ba3f05bdc4c3ef5`,
not final source- and release-matched acceptance.

- **Scenario-local Skills and Rules:** `skill:hack-consoles` is
  available through Domination only; `skill:pick-up-supply-boxes`

  `rule:scenario:designated-landing-area` remain Firefight-only.
  Deliberately attaching a foreign scenario Rule fails rather

  are a shared InfinityDB General Rules baseline, not a permanent

  undeployed-ancillary/Killing clarification; Supplies has the
  scenario-element/Marker-State clarification. Domination and
  Firefight have no selected FAQ ruling; Firefight does not
  inherit the Annihilation ruling merely because they reuse
  `rule:scenario:killing`. The explicitly selected ITS
  Battleground pilot also receives none. Rulings remain separate
  source/version-scoped API and normal-browser overlays, never
  replacements of scenario facts or unscoped global Rules.
- **Player navigation:** ordinary search resolves the single
  General Rules Specialist Troops entry and links to Domination,
  Firefight and Supplies. The selected scenario card displays its
  qualifying Skills and citations. Browser visits to all four
  scenarios preserve the independent FAQ cards only where
  applicable, in both 1440 px and 390 px layouts.

## FAQ provenance, supersession and effective-boundary review (V1S-047; 2026-11-05)

**Reviewed current edition:** all 29 records in
`data/curated/rules/n5-faq-v0.1.json`, the independently authored
v0.0/v0.1 question inventory, and the source/citation selection
policy in `docs/faq-v0.1-scope-inventory.md`. The reviewed
revision is `fd35cd21ded3b97f991a886748941d1501038cc6`.
This is current-edition provenance/scope review, not historical
FAQ replay or approval of all ITS-season answers.

- **Carried identity, not retroactive answer publication:** twenty
  current `faq-ruling:` records retain `facts.originalPublication`
  on English FAQ v0.0 with its individually reviewed original
  printed page. Nine first-recorded v0.1 identities point both
  original and selected citation to English v0.1. Every current
  selected answer remains cited to English v0.1; its corresponding
  Spanish citation is corroboration. The original `2025-10-15`
  and current `2026-09-01` date fields are maintained reference
  anchors, **not** independently established PDF upload dates.
- **Supersession is explicit, not assumed:** all 29 currently
  published rulings have `supersedes: []`; a carried-forward
  question does not auto-supersede its own provenance. The
  single reviewed numerical Haris cross-language discrepancy
  is a `conflictingCitations` entry, **not** a new ruling,
  supersession event or reason to discard the Spanish citation.
  Synthetic regressions confirm that a true successor hides
  an eligible predecessor only in overlapping time/scenario
  contexts, while unscoped predecessor and base reference
  still remain readable where appropriate. Export rejects
  missing predecessors, cycles, original-publication drift
  and nonoverlapping rules, season, scenario or date scopes.
- **Current effective boundary:** each current entry requires
  exact `rulesRevision: "5.3"` and
  `effectiveFrom: "2026-09-01"`. Twenty-seven have that
  general N5.3 applicability, and two add one explicit Core
  `scenarioIds` constraint (Supplies carrier/Marker State,
  Annihilation undeployed Ancillary/Killed). None of the 29
  authors an ITS-season selector; this does not implicitly
  enable the eight deferred ITS-only questions. The selector
  independently gates selected-source date, original-source
  date, collection effective date, ruling effective interval,
  exact revision and any applicable scenario/season identity.
  The v0.0 page/date therefore cannot select a current v0.1
  answer before the chosen current reference anchor.
- **Fail-closed season behavior:** synthetic ITS applicability
  requires *both* season ID and the exact season revision;
  wrong or absent revisions do not match. Draft entries,
  invalid topic/scenario identities, mismatched source
  selection, and off-revision queries do not silently
  join global Core rules or fabricate historical revisions.

**Focused verification at `fd35cd21` (Windows local,
`-m "not full_assets"`, 2026-11-05):**
`test_current_faq_temporal_gates_are_independent_for_all_published_rulings`
**1 passed** (29 records, four separately shifted date gates);
`test_export_rejects_bad_faq_references` **11 passed**
(including season/revision/scenario/time mismatches,
bad predecessors/cycles, original drift);

**1 passed**; and
`test_faq_season_revision_is_exact_not_inferred`
**1 passed**. The unchanged-code parent `dd031b2e`

selection (**79 passed, one release parity opt-in skipped**).
This review does not claim to rerun the opt-in packaged
`rules.db` check, browser source cards or hosted CI.

**Disposition:** no new identity, supersession or

## Infiltration bare ReRoll source boundary (V1S-050; 2026-11-05)

**Scope and authority:** the pinned 2026-09-29 N5 Army
application/raw archive, English and Spanish N5.3 Core PDF
printed p. **97**, English general modifier rules pp. **68/75**,
current [official Infiltration Wiki](https://infinitythewiki.com/Infiltration),
and N5 FAQ v0.1. This is a **source-owner and semantic-boundary
review**, not a proposal for new gameplay. The owner probe
`tools.audit_source_variant_inputs --catalog skills --source-id 47
--owner-extra-id 277` is read-only.

- **Direct source inventory:** Infiltration source/application ID
  **47** has **216** occurrences. **17** have reviewed, exact
  single-extra `PH=X` signatures: `250/TEXT:PH=12` (4),
  `353/TEXT:PH=15` (4), `340/TEXT:PH=16` (2),
  `317/TEXT:PH=18` (2) and `318/TEXT:PH=19` (5).
  **Five** have only `277/TEXT:ReRoll`, without a PH
  value; **194** have no source extra. All reviewed fixed-PH
  substitutions remain limited to the PH-3 **Infiltration
  Roll**, never the Unit's PH Attribute or an additional
  reroll.
- **Five bare ReRoll owners, all profile-scope only:**
  CORREGIDOR BANDITS (unit 400), Corregidor/Army 502,
  occurrence **44546**, and StarCo/905, occurrence
  **74177**, both position 9; DASYUS (unit 587),
  ALEPH/701, occurrence **61511**, and OSS/703,
  occurrence **65105**, both position 7; and
  REINF: DASYUS (unit 1727), Ank Program/799,
  occurrence **67248**, position 7. Each record has
  source extra position 1, raw kind TEXT, exact value
  `ReRoll`, and no separate source-raw value.
  These five direct source rows do not establish five
  distinct Unit identities or a global Army-wide ability.
- **Unresolved source precedence:** English p. 97 states
  `Infiltration (ReRoll PH=X)`, explicitly using that
  selected PH for the PH-3 reroll and accepting its
  result. Spanish p. 97 repeats the same contract as
  `Infiltración (ReRoll FIS=X)`. The general
  `ReRoll` notation in English pp. 68/75 permits a
  one-die reroll when using the named Skill, but the
  Infiltration-specific passage does not say how to
  resolve an Army entry lacking `X`. Neither the
  reviewed FAQ nor the current official Infiltration
  Wiki establishes the missing attribute value or
  explicit override for bare Army notation. Using
  the Trooper's own PH-3 via the general rule is a
  possible hypothesis, **not a reviewed exception**.
  It would be equally unsupported to invent a fixed
  PH value or suppress a source qualifier silently.
- **Disposition and external trigger:** source extra
  `277/TEXT:ReRoll` remains **unreviewed**, with
  no typed `reroll-one-die` effect. Exact ownership
  and deliberately rejected mixed/invalid signatures
  are asserted by the extended test. The authority
  question is retained under
  [SRC-033](official-source-clarifications.md#src-033--infiltration-reroll-without-the-rulebooks-phx-parameter).
  An official Army correction, FAQ, or explicit ruling
  identifying the intended parameter/roll is required
  before enabling this occurrence type. This closes the
  bounded **V1S-050 review**, not its external rule
  question, downstream variant usage index or final
  release acceptance.

**Validation at working base `4b289fc5fe3412da3633a5e9eb1fee226bc23d30`
(2026-11-05):** exact source owner/typed-read-model test
`test_infiltration_ph_exact_source_47_and_unreviewed_reroll`
**1 passed** after adding the five-identity guard;
`test_infiltration_ph_review_rejects_unapproved_values_and_type`
**1 passed**, retaining the negative classification.
Targeted Ruff lint **passed**.
The read-only `tools.audit_source_variant_inputs` inventory
and owner probe both exited **0**.

**Important separate release-pair check:** the prior
read-only `tools.review_infiltration_ph_variant`
invocation exited **1** with `Lieutenant release rows
differ: records` in the full tracked-vs-fresh
rules database equivalence checker. It checks all
records, not only Infiltration: tracked `rules.db`
has not yet been rebuilt after V1S-048 changed a

equivalence gate**, not source evidence justifying

## Engineer occurrence and operational boundary (V1S-051; 2026-11-05)

**Source set:** pinned 2026-09-29 N5 Army application/raw
data, English N5.3 Core p. **91**, Spanish N5.3 p. **98**,
general bracketed-modifier rules (English p. **75**), and
the current official [Engineer Wiki](https://infinitythewiki.com/Engineers)
which retains the superseded pre-5.2 version for
source-history comparison. This is a reviewed
source/family boundary, not a new generic modifier
implementation. Baseline: `c10ad0d3d350d7c8888b1ce6422030d77351c1ed`.

- **Exact signatures and ownership:** Army Skill source/application
  **49**, `Engineer`, has **389** direct rows.
  **15** qualified rows carry exactly four single TEXT
  extras: `1:+3` (**3**, Parvati's three Army profiles),
  `277:ReRoll` (**5**, Johnny Kao across five Armies),
  `282:WIP=14` (**1**, Shénji Yíng in Imperial Service)
  and `366:ReRoll -3` (**6**, Sophotects and
  Chief Mech-Engineer Chung-Hee Jeong, including
  Reinforcement copies). The other **374** rows have
  no extra. Every qualified row is `profile`-owned,
  with an exact source occurrence ID, TEXT type,
  position **1** and no independent raw string;
  `test_engineer_source_49_qualifiers_remain_unreviewed_and_owner_exact`
  pins all 15 occurrence IDs and their source
  signatures. None is evidence for an Army-global
  or unqualified Engineer buff.
- **Rule-defined repair versus cancellation:** Engineer's
  direct requirement is Silhouette contact.
  Ordinary STR repair uses a WIP Roll to restore
  **one Wound** (or cancel appropriate Unconscious
  State), but failed repair inflicts a Wound.
  The distinct eligible-State cancellation path
  removes all qualifying non-Unconscious States on
  a successful WIP Roll; a failed attempt does
  **not** inflict a Wound. Stunned uses the
  target-dependent Doctor (VITA) versus Engineer
  (STR) exception. Successful repair for a Remote
  Presence target can clear either Unconscious
  level with one Roll. Self-use is disallowed while
  the user is in a Null State. Existing source-cited
  Engaged/target-allegiance, delegated Servant/
  Cyberplug, positive-before-negative Order
  resolution and non-Allied assumptions remain
  separately scoped; none follows merely from
  a source occurrence string.
- **Reroll semantics and edition boundary:**
  English/Spanish N5.3 require
  `Engineer (ReRoll WIP=X)`: the specified WIP
  applies only to the Skill reroll, once per
  Order; the second result is binding and
  grants no new enemy ARO. A later eligible
  Command Token reroll is separate and does
  **not** use this Skill-specific replacement
  WIP. The official Engineer Wiki records the
  preceding `ReRoll -X` text and labels the
  explicit `ReRoll WIP=X` form as the October
  **2025 PDF 5.2** change. Thus source extra
  `366:ReRoll -3` resembles a superseded
  notation, but that history is not authority
  to apply a `-3` to an N5.3 Engineer Roll.
  The publisher-facing question remains
  [SRC-029](official-source-clarifications.md#src-029--doctorengineer-reroll--3-lacks-explicit-n5-wip-scope).
- **Qualifier policy:** the base `skill:engineer`
  record remains reviewed with
  `variantSemantics: {"inheritance": "family"}`.
  The positive `+3`, generic `ReRoll`,
  `WIP=14`, and historic-form `ReRoll -3`
  are retained as **unreviewed exact source
  qualifiers**, with `reviewedExtra: null`
  and no implicitly typed extra/combination,
  replacement Attribute, universal modifier,
  generic target-selection license or
  automatic reroll chain. A future exact
  signature interpretation must preserve
  all operational exceptions above and obtain
  adequate reviewed source authority.

**Fresh focused checks (Windows local,
`-m "not full_assets"`, 2026-11-05):**
`tests/test_audit_source_variant_inputs.py::test_engineer_source_49_qualifiers_remain_unreviewed_and_owner_exact`
**1 passed**;
`tests/test_curated.py::test_rea001_engineer_target_eligibility_and_repair_vs_cancel_examples`
**1 passed**; and
`tests/test_command_token_source_review.py::test_n5_command_resource_and_all_three_modes_are_pinned_source_obligations`
**1 passed** (pinned N5.3 pp. 128–129).
The exact owner/inventory CLI checks for all
four source extras exited **0**.
No runtime, curated rule or release database
change was made by this review.

**Disposition:** no new source-to-family defect
was confirmed; all four source extras remain
exactly and explicitly unresolved. V1S-051
retires as a source-bounded **no-change**
review with existing passing tests and
SRC-029 retaining the external question.
V1S-052 independently reviews Doctor rather
than reusing Engineer qualifiers by analogy.
V1S-062–066 still own the cross-Army
usage-index contract, and V1S-084 owns
the separately pending release-pair refresh.

## Doctor occurrence and scoped recovery review (V1S-052; 2026-11-05)

**Sources and baseline:** pinned 2026-09-29 Army Skill
source/application **53**, English N5.3 Core
p. **90**, Spanish N5.3 p. **100**, and the existing
curated Doctor record with scoped Stunned, NWI,
Technorganic and Command Token references.
The reviewed Windows base is
`172f6d19339bf3ca0fc2118d2787b78a6b8a0113`.
Doctor's evidence was examined independently
of Engineer's source-49 parameters.

- **Raw occurrence inventory:** source **53** has
  **235** direct Doctor rows: **85** with
  `1/TEXT:+3`, **15** with `253/TEXT:2W`
  (11 loadout-option and four profile owners),
  **three** with `366/TEXT:ReRoll -3`
  (Bipandra, raw occurrences `767`,
  `8714`, `12414`), and **132** with
  no extra. Existing source-inventory tests
  pin exact count, all 15 2W owner IDs, the
  three `ReRoll -3` IDs, raw TEXT signatures
  and owner types. The 85 `+3` occurrences
  are not inferred to be globally applicable.
  All three extra signatures remain classified
  unreviewed at the source occurrence layer;
  `variantSemantics` retains the reviewed
  family-only inheritance contract.
- **Doctor is not Engineer:** ordinary Doctor
  requires a VITA target in Unconscious State
  and Silhouette contact; successful WIP
  recovery removes a Wound and cancels
  Unconscious, while failed ordinary recovery
  places the target in Dead State. This is
  **not** Engineer's STR repair or eligible
  State-cancellation failure behavior.
  A separate Stunned cancellation rule
  permits Doctor for VITA targets outside
  ordinary Unconscious recovery, while
  Engineer is used for STR Stunned targets.
  The NWI self-use and Technorganic STR
  recovery exceptions remain conditional,
  not a blanket ordinary healing permission.
  Doctor self-use normally excludes Null State;
  eligible Command Token rerolls remain
  separate from any Skill-specific
  `ReRoll WIP=X` substitution.
- **Bounded textual correction:** English
  Doctor p. 90 describes `Doctor (2W)`
  removing two Wounds on successful recovery;
  Spanish p. 100 explicitly says
  `hasta dos` (up to two) on the same
  ordinary Unconscious cancellation. The
  maintained Doctor summary was tightened
  from `removes two Wounds` to
  `removes up to two Wounds on successful
  ordinary recovery`, and the corroborating
  Spanish printed p. 100 citation was added.
  This does not restore Wounds that have
  not been lost, change the Roll's success
  conditions, or create a new typed 2W
  occurrence effect.
- **Unresolved source qualifiers:** the
  `+3` and `ReRoll -3` extras are
  literal Army qualifications, not
  an approved universal WIP adjustment.
  The current rulebook specifies
  `Doctor (ReRoll WIP=X)`, including the
  separate Command Token WIP boundary.
  The bare signed `-3` supplies no
  explicit `X` and remains under
  [SRC-029](official-source-clarifications.md#src-029--doctorengineer-reroll--3-lacks-explicit-n5-wip-scope).
  Neither the 2W source signature nor
  Engineer's Remote Presence rule permits
  inventing another Doctor effect.

tree, 2026-11-05:** the amended

**passed**, verifying the additional Spanish
citation and `up to two` condition alongside

## Variant usage candidate foundation (V1S-063; 2026-11-05)

**Status: partially implemented, V1S-063 stays open.** Base

requires the existing matched Army/rules/deployed-object
publication; compares the raw and application Army

Existing reviewed Skill source levels and
whole reviewed combination signatures have

deduplicated by logical Unit and concrete Army;
distinct source rows are preserved for drilldown,
not equated with distinct Units.

  **515** unmapped-or-ambiguous rule links.
  These are not *missing Unit counts* and must

- **1,273** Include relationships retained as

### Source-row and Include-chain hardening (2026-11-05)

**Project domain:** Data processing

The follow-up to `a0c49350faffa873f27c234de37c3a5d880178be` adds
`variant_usage_includes.py`. It compares the full multiset of raw Include
rows with materialized relationships, then traverses exact source owner
paths rather than interchangeable payload IDs. Missing/ambiguous parents
or targets, duplicate source keys, changed quantity/raw fields, truncated
inventories and disconnected cycles fail closed. Repeated target visits
through distinct positions preserve their own chains; quantity does not
multiply them. Shared Unit options retain their source-only parent identity
even when their target resolves in several Armies. Edges and paths remain
context-only and do not increase direct variant totals.

VD-0025–0027 were designed individually without executing tests. The subsequent
Windows VR run of `tests/test_variant_usage.py` passed **56 tests, zero skips**
on the implementation working tree based on the SHA above. This includes
display-order parity for both catalogs/all owner kinds, missing/duplicate
source keys, ambiguous catalog and owner mappings, seven source rows sharing
payloads across two Armies, set-based counts, repeated and two-edge Include
chains, shared-option target context, deterministic ordering and self/multinode/
disconnected cycles. The matched raw-archive integration ran rather than skipped.

The publication-integrity CLI also passed against the pinned candidate, retaining
the **151 groups, 10,923 reviewed rows, 4,938 unresolved rows and 1,273 Include
edges** recorded above, now with **1,417 context-only Include paths**. This
internal candidate improvement warrants no standalone changelog entry.

**Pinned Linux acceptance:** at
`b1a16a638d6730c5be2c10ece7d69608b73e0b28`, job
`linux-dev:2588397049ce4fc68fe10784f9e24a20` ran
`tools.run_checks --stage test --assets required` for
`tests/test_variant_usage.py`, `tests/test_peripheral_relationships.py` and

### Contextual Game States reference inventory (V1S-068, 2026-11-05)

**Project domains:** Data processing, Web backend, Web frontend

**Method and boundary:** Classified the maintained, source-cited N5.3 core
collection (454 records), Hacking Programs v5.3 (12), N5 FAQ v0.1 (29) and
the Season 18 Battleground record (1), plus existing reviewed State
interaction semantics. The review inspected explicit `[[state:...]]` links,
typed `relations`, FAQ `topicIds`, and the relevant rule prose. The core
collection has 151 records with explicit State links or relations, covering
24 canonical State targets; this is not a claim that all those references
require a new typed edge. It is an inventory of **rule-definition references**,
not in-game State evaluation or a complete Army-snapshot re-audit.

- **Fireteams — membership, cancellation and scoped exceptions.**
  `rule:fireteam-general` (N5.3 pp. 132–136, Ancillary p. 107) already
  links to Isolated, Suppressive Fire, Disconnected and Retreat! in reviewed
  prose. `state:isolated` already owns `prevents-membership-in` toward
  `rule:fireteam-general` and `prevents-participation-in` toward
  `rule:coordinated-orders`. **Classification:** Isolated causes a
  *contextual membership loss*; Leader versus non-Leader controls whole-team
  cancellation. Joining a Fireteam **cancels** Suppressive Fire; being in
  Retreat! ends the team, but the army-level situation and individual State
  are not interchangeable. An Ancillary Controller's eligibility while its
  Peripheral is undeployed, Disconnected or Null is a **role-conditioned
  exception**, not generic Disconnected eligibility. Existing forward
  links are intact; State-side contextual back-links need V1S-069 review.
- **Hacking — direct entry versus sustained-effect cancellation.** Eight
  of the 12 Hacking Program records explicitly mention a canonical State;
  five have source-authored State relations: Carbonite
  `causes-state` IMM-B; Cybermask `enters-state` IMP-2; Oblivion
  `causes-state` Isolated; Spotlight `causes-state` Targeted; Total
  Control `causes-state` and `cancels-state` Possessed. These are
  conditional program outcomes, not declarations of a Unit's current State.
  Separately, `rule:supportware` (N5.3 pp. 55, 60) states that sustaining
  Hacker Isolated/Null **cancels a Program effect**, not the State. Likewise,
  Hacking Device Firewall is disabled under those conditions: **effect
  suppression/override**, not State entry/cancellation. Those cases retain
  explicit prose and FAQ topic links; do not invent `cancels-state` edges.
- **Ammunition — failed-save entry, not automatic application.** E/M
  `causes-state` Isolated after a failed Saving Roll and **also** IMM-B only
  for HI/TAG/REM/VH; PARA causes IMM-A on a failed PH-6 Save; Stun causes
  Stunned on its applicable failed Save; Shock has a conditional Dead outcome.
  These reviewed graph edges already exist, with target/roll conditions in
  the owning Ammunition facts (E/M N5.3 p. 64). DA and EXP mention
  Unconscious in roll-sequencing examples but do **not** create a direct
  unconditional `causes-state` edge. Neither `ammunition:em` nor a
  compound N+E/M profile grants a static current State.
- **Scenarios — scoring and carrier restrictions, not State transitions.**
  Core Dominate Quadrants uses Normal or Shasvastii-Embryo eligibility
  (N5.3 p. 151); Killing includes Dead, end-of-game Null classification
  and never-deployed Troopers (pp. 150, 155). Season 18 Battleground
  mentions Unconscious/Dead in scoring context. Core Supplies and its
  FAQ restrict a Supply Box carrier from entering *Marker State*, but there
  is no single canonical generic Marker State identity. **Classification:**
  mission-specific scoring or conditional prevention; do not create
  `causes-state`, `cancels-state` or one-state `prevents-state-entry`
  shortcuts that change victory or scenario scope.
- **FAQ — related topics versus unconditional rules.** Of 29 FAQ records,
  seven carry a `state:...` `topicIds` reference. Thirteen records
  matched a broad State-related wording review (including incidental
  usages), rather than thirteen verified missing links. Existing topic
  coverage includes Camouflaged, Holoecho, Isolated, Disconnected,
  Unconscious and Prone. The Peripheral Controller declaration FAQ
  mentions *Stunned or Immobilized* generically but correctly owns the
  `skill:peripheral` topic; it cannot select IMM-A or IMM-B without
  further qualification. The Supply Box carrier FAQ scopes to Supplies,
  the Protheion Dead example scopes to Protheion, and the undeployed
  Ancillary 'killed' FAQ scopes to Annihilation/Killing. These are
  **eligibility, exception, or scoring disclosures**, not new State
  entry/cancellation edges. A keyword occurrence of 'targeted' in an
  unrelated FAQ is not evidence for `state:targeted`.

**V1S-069 disposition:** Review and, where helpful, add only
source-backed **reciprocal textual navigation** from the Suppressive Fire,
Disconnected (Ancillary-specific) and possibly Retreat! State descriptions
to canonical Fireteam rules. Preserve role, timing, army-situation and Null
qualifications in the link sentence. Verify real browser navigation both
ways, FAQ topic links, Hacking and Ammunition source edges, and source
conditions at desktop and narrow widths. Do not create broad new graph
relations for program cancellation, generic Marker/Null abstractions,
scenario scoring, or temporary Unit State; the present typed links already
represent the stable entry/cancellation cases. This inventory closes
**V1S-068**; V1S-069's scoped curated acceptance is recorded below.

States and Properties groups against each profile's ordered `trait_references`;
source parity also checks those reference labels against the source-backed
`traits` field for every published profile. Ammunition publication, exact modes,
and reviewed Combined Saving Roll behavior were covered by the same run.

The pinned `infinity.db` was unchanged (SHA-256
`8a5840d1ff632e89de26210ee7bcb47e54c6579c15b5c3436e226702e872d7c8`) and
contains 183 `metadata_weapons` rows with 183 distinct `(id, mode)` pairs.
This count is the source-table inventory, not a claim that every row is a
published Weapon. The 171 printed chart rows and their two disclosed source
discrepancies remain as recorded in V1S-070. No Linux checkout was synchronized;
the acceptance is Windows-local and does not replace hosted CI or release gates.

`53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`),

and its matched `infinity.db` (SHA-256

The audited PDF identifies N5 v5.3 and has 196 file pages. The Army archive,
database and rules PDF remain distinct source identities.

The independent N5 tables were cross-checked with their existing read-only
auditors: the p. 74 Deployable Profiles table yields 13 printed rows, 10
reference identities, one shared Mines-family row and repeated FastPanda and
Madtraps evidence. All rows resolve to the already reviewed inventory; no
generic Mine or duplicate-label identity is added. The other pinned table
audits likewise preserve their own scope: six Game Modes, four Hacking Device
types and 11 distinct Programs, ten Silhouette source rows, six Retreat!/table
setup rows, five Terrain Types with 18 suggested examples and two Gate labels,
and four Core scenarios with four Console and three Supply Box objectives.
These tables do not establish another Weapon/Equipment deployed-object profile.

An alternate-wording check searched normalized English N5.3 page text for
stationary/independent references near object-like entities. Its five matches
are confined to p. 9 (an immobile Trooper), p. 36 (the general Deployable
Weapon class), p. 70 (the already reviewed Armed Turret), p. 107 (an
Autonomous Peripheral in the separate Trooper/Peripheral model), and p. 175
(the Deployable glossary definition). They describe a Trooper, a class,
previously classified identities or another domain—not a new identity or
delivery edge. Broader placement, representation, scenario and scenery
wording remains a bounded discovery screen, not proof of total N5.3 coverage.

The current reproducible lexical audits account for 106 curated
Weapon/Equipment definitions, 24 summary candidates, 10 additional source
clause leads, 12 candidate decisions and 19 delivery-origin records. Their
overlapping classifications leave no summary candidate unreviewed. The
expanded non-catalog scan classifies 28 additional definitions; the
non-catalog scan classifies 61. Both report zero unreviewed candidates and
zero missing maintained N5.3 citations. Together with the 14 supplementary
Weapon Chart profiles, these reviews produce no new deployed-object identity,
attribute, delivery path or Unit-use relationship. The reviewed inventory
remains 17 objects and 28 delivery paths.

No curated or generated data, runtime behavior or release artifact changed.
The previously documented Mine Dispenser subtype and Mine, Dazer and Armed
Turret Silhouette conflicts remain unresolved and disclosed. This source
review creates no artificial VD/VR obligation or Unreleased note. V1S-073 is
retired; scenery/Gate discoverability, reverse-use reconciliation, any
source-confirmed additions and final API/browser acceptance remain under
V1S-074–078.

## Scenery, Gates, terrain combinations and scenario elements (V1S-074; 2026-11-05)

**Project domains:** Data processing, Web backend, Web frontend

At development revision `14d7d6e79febdd7c1bbc2830a46663d0d0e0265c`, this
read-only review used the pinned English N5.3 PDF (SHA-256
`53921e91c2d3d62ad5f7125abcd4174b2cf937d45320233eed5b6d301b66af3f`),
printed pp. 143–145, 151 and 153, the maintained Core collection and its
matched runtime publication. No source acquisition or artifact change occurred.

| Source element | Current owner and disposition |
| --- | --- |
| Scenery Element | Embedded Game term `term:scenery-element`; a game-element class, not another deployed-device profile |
| Five Terrain Types | `skill:terrain` and `rule:special-terrain`; profile applicability remains distinct from table/session characteristics |
| Eighteen named terrain combinations | Suggested examples on p. 145, not eighteen extra Terrain Types. The inventory retains names, but their per-example characteristic combinations are not published: confirmed reference gap owned by V1S-077 |
| Narrow Gate and Wide Gate | Scenery access labels explained by `rule:access-width`, not independently deployed Weapons or Equipment. Neither label is an alias or separate Glossary entry; global `/api/search?q=Narrow+Gate` and `q=Wide+Gate` return empty `items`: confirmed discoverability gap owned by V1S-077 |
| Four Consoles | Domination-scoped objectives and marker geometry, with Hack Consoles; not generic Equipment/carrier uses |
| Three Supply Boxes | Supplies-scoped objectives and marker geometry, with Pick Up Supply Boxes and carrying restrictions; not generic Deployables |
| Annihilation and Firefight | No additional physical objective-marker identity in their three maintained configurations each |

The terrain/scenery auditor, run with `--pdf` against the pinned bytes,
reported five Types, eighteen examples, two Gate labels and the maintained
Special Terrain/Access Width definitions. The scenario-object auditor reported
all four Core scenarios and the expected four Console/three Supply Box
objectives. Both retain the existing seventeen reviewed deployed objects.
These are read-only inventory results, not new browser acceptance.

The missing example values must retain their source distinctions: optional
Difficult Terrain, dashes rather than assumed Boolean values, optional Zero-G
with conditional difficulty, combined Low Visibility/White Noise, and Storm's
relative visibility adjustment. Publishing these examples must preserve the
players' pre-game agreement and avoid imposing suggestions on a scenario.
The Access Width baseline already preserves the Narrow S2 limit, unrestricted
Wide passage, the Prone/Silhouette X exception, movement-rule priority and
the default open/LoF rule. Gate-label discovery can reuse that owner without
creating a device or silently resolving a live Trooper's passage.

V1S-074's classification review is complete and retired; V1S-077 explicitly
owns both confirmed reference gaps and their source-to-browser acceptance.
V1S-075–076 retain source/Include and reverse-use reconciliation, and
V1S-078 retains final deployed-object API/browser acceptance. No tests were
added or executed, no artificial VD/VR obligation was created for this
read-only review, and no Unreleased entry is warranted until delivery.
